## I Thery’s Trade / 一 特里的营生
===Original===
Four men sat about a table on the sidewalk in front of the Café of the Nations in the High Street of Cádiz and talked business.

Leon Gonsalez was one, Poiccart was another, George Manfred was a notable third, and one Thery, or Saimont, was the fourth. Of this quartet, only Thery requires no introduction to the student of contemporary history. In the Bureau of Public Affairs you will find his record. As Thery, alias Saimont, he is registered and to all students of Criminology and Physiognomy, he must need no introduction.

===Chinese===
四个人围坐在加的斯（Cádiz）大街万国咖啡馆（Café of the Nations）门前人行道的一张桌旁，谈着一桩买卖。

莱昂·冈萨雷斯（Leon Gonsalez）是一个，波伊卡特（Poiccart）是另一个，乔治·曼弗雷德（George Manfred）是名声在外的第三个，还有一个名叫特里（Thery）——又叫赛蒙特（Saimont）——的是第四个。这四人当中，唯有特里无须向研究当代史的学子多做介绍。在公共事务局的档案里，你自会查到他的记录。作为特里，化名赛蒙特，他早已登记在案，凡是治犯罪学与相貌学的学者，对他必不陌生。

===Original===
He sat at the little table, this man, obviously ill at ease, pinching his fat cheeks, smoothing his shaggy eyebrows, fingering the white scar on his unshaven chin, doing all the things that the lower classes do when they suddenly find themselves placed on terms of equality with their betters.

For although Gonsalez, with the light blue eyes and the restless hands, and Poiccart, heavy, saturnine, and suspicious, and George Manfred, with his grey shot beard and single eyeglass, were less famous in the criminal world, each was a great man, as you shall learn.

===Chinese===
此刻这人坐在小桌旁，显然浑身不自在，捏捏肥嘟嘟的脸颊，抹抹浓乱的眉毛，又摸摸没刮的下巴上那道白疤——下层人骤然同体面人平起平坐时，多半都这副做派。

因为，尽管长着浅蓝色眼睛、一双手总不安生的冈萨雷斯，还有阴沉、忧郁、多疑的波伊卡特，以及灰白胡须、单片眼镜的乔治·曼弗雷德，在罪案世界里名气都算小，可个个都是了不起的人物——这一点下文自见分晓。

===Original===
Manfred laid down the *Heraldo de Madrid*, removed his eyeglass, rubbed it with a spotless handkerchief, and laughed quietly.

“These Russians are droll,” he commented.

Poiccart frowned and reached for the newspaper. “Who is it﻿—this time?”

“A Governor of one of the Southern Provinces.”

“Killed?”

Manfred’s moustache curled in scornful derision.

“Bah! Who ever killed a man with a bomb! Yes, yes; I know it has been done﻿—but so clumsy, so primitive, so very much like undermining a city wall that it may fall and slay﻿—amongst others﻿—your enemy.”

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德放下《马德里先驱报》（*Heraldo de Madrid*），摘下单片眼镜，用一方一尘不染的手帕擦拭，轻声笑了。

「这些俄国人真是滑稽。」他评了一句。

波伊卡特皱起眉头，伸手去拿报纸。「这回——是谁？」

「南方某省的一个省长。」

「死了？」

曼弗雷德的髭须轻蔑地往上一撇。

「呸！谁用炸弹杀成过人！是，是，我知道有过先例——可那多笨拙，多原始，活像挖墙脚叫城墙塌下来，砸死一大片人——你的仇人——也捎带在其中。」

===Original===
Poiccart was reading the telegram deliberately and without haste, after his fashion.

“The Prince was severely injured and the would-be assassin lost an arm,” he read, and pursed his lips disapprovingly. The hands of Gonsalez, never still, opened and shut nervously, which was Leon’s sign of perturbation.

“Our friend here”﻿—Manfred jerked his head in the direction of Gonsalez and laughed﻿—“our friend has a conscience and﻿—”

“Only once,” interrupted Leon quickly, “and not by my wish; you remember, Manfred; you remember, Poiccart”﻿—he did not address Thery﻿—“I advised against it. You remember?” He seemed anxious to exculpate himself from the unspoken charge. “It was a miserable little thing, and I was in Madrid,” he went on breathlessly, “and they came to me, some men from a factory at Barcelona. They said what they were going to do, and I was horror-stricken at their ignorance of the elements of the laws of chemistry. I wrote down the ingredients and the proportions, and begged them, yes, almost on my knees, to use some other method. ‘My children,’ I said, ‘you are playing with something that even chemists are afraid to handle. If the owner of the factory is a bad man, by all means exterminate him, shoot him, wait on him after he has dined and is slow and dull, and present a petition with the right hand and﻿—with the left hand﻿—so’!”

===Chinese===
波伊卡特照他一贯的做派，不慌不忙地读着那封电讯。

「亲王受了重伤，行刺者丢了一条胳膊。」他念道，抿嘴不以为然。冈萨雷斯那双从不安生的手紧张地一张一合——这是莱昂心绪不宁的标志。

「咱们这位朋友」——曼弗雷德朝冈萨雷斯那边一甩头，笑了——「咱们这位朋友有副良心，而且——」

「就那么一回，」莱昂赶忙插嘴，「还非我本意；您记得的，曼弗雷德；您记得的，波伊卡特」——他没有对特里说——「我那时是反对的。记得吧？」他一副急于洗清这桩没说出口的罪责的模样。「那是桩不起眼的小事，我当时在马德里，」他上气不接下气地往下说，「有人来找我，是巴塞罗那一家工厂的几个工人。他们说了打算干的事，他们连化学定律的入门都一无所知，吓得我目瞪口呆。我把原料和配比写下来，求他们——不错，几乎是跪着求他们——换个别的主意。『孩子们，』我说，『你们摆弄的这个东西，连化学家都不敢碰。要是厂主是个恶人，尽可以消灭他，枪毙他，等他吃完饭、又迟又钝的时候伺候他，右手递上一份请愿书，左手——这么一下』！」

===Original===
Leon twisted his knuckles down and struck forward and upward at an imaginary oppressor. “But they would listen to nothing I had to say.”

Manfred stirred the glass of creamy liquid that stood at his elbow, and nodded his head with an amused twinkle in his grey eyes.

“I remember﻿—several people died, and the principal witness at the trial of the expert in explosives was the man for whom the bomb was intended.”

===Chinese===
莱昂攥起指节朝下一拧，朝着假想中的暴徒向前、向上一击。「可我说的话他们一个字也听不进。」

曼弗雷德搅了搅手肘边那杯乳白色的酒，灰眼睛里闪着被逗乐的光，点了点头。

「我记得——死了好几个人，那位爆破专家受审的时候，主要证人正是炸弹原本要炸的那个人。」

===Original===
Thery cleared his throat as if to speak, and the three looked at him curiously. There was some resentment in Thery’s voice.

“I do not profess to be a great man like you, señors. Half the time I don’t understand what you are talking about﻿—you speak of governments and kings and constitutions and causes. If a man does *me* an injury I smash his head”﻿—he hesitated, “I do not know how to say it﻿ ﻿… but I mean﻿ ﻿… well, you kill people without hating them, men who have not hurt you. Now, that is not my way.﻿ ﻿…” He hesitated again, tried to collect his thoughts, looked intently at the middle of the roadway, shook his head and relapsed into silence.

===Chinese===
特里清了清嗓子，像要开口，三人好奇地望着他。特里的嗓音里带着几分怨气。

「我不敢冒充诸位先生（señores）这样的伟人。你们说的话我一半听不懂——你们讲政府、国王、宪法，还有种种主义。谁要是伤了*我*，我就敲碎他的脑袋」——他迟疑了一下，「我不知道该怎么说……可我是说……唉，你们杀人不带恨，杀的还是从没招惹过你们的人。这可不是我的路数……」他又迟疑起来，努力收拢思绪，直勾勾盯着路中央，摇了摇头，重新归于沉默。

===Original===
The others looked at him, then at one another, and each man smiled. Manfred took a bulky case from his pocket, extracted an untidy cigarette, rerolled it deftly and struck a government match on the sole of his boot.

“Your﻿—way﻿—my﻿—dear﻿—Thery”﻿—he puffed﻿—“is a fool’s way. You kill for benefit; we kill for justice, which lifts us out of the ruck of professional slayers. When we see an unjust man oppressing his fellows; when we see an evil thing done against the good God”﻿—Thery crossed himself, “and against man﻿—and know that by the laws of man this evildoer may escape punishment﻿—we punish.”

===Chinese===
其余三人望望他，又彼此相望，各自莞尔。曼弗雷德从口袋里摸出一只鼓鼓的烟盒，抽出一支皱巴巴的烟，麻利地重新卷紧，拿一根官制火柴在靴底上一划。

「你的——路数——我——亲爱——的——特里，」——他吐了口烟——「是蠢人的路数。你为私利杀人，我们为正义杀人，这一点便使我们高出职业凶手的芸芸之辈。当我们看见一个不义之徒欺压同胞；当我们看见一件恶事冒犯了好上帝」——特里画了个十字——「也冒犯了世人——又明知按人间的法律，这作恶的人可能逃脱惩罚——我们就施以惩罚。」

===Original===
“Listen,” interrupted the taciturn Poiccart; “once there was a girl, young and beautiful, up there”﻿—he waved his hand northward with unerring instinct﻿—“and a priest﻿—a priest, you understand﻿—and the parents winked at it because it is often done﻿ ﻿… but the girl was filled with loathing and shame, and would not go a second time, so he trapped her and kept her in a house, and then when the bloom was off turned her out, and I found her. She was nothing to me, but I said, ‘Here is a wrong that the law cannot adequately right.’ So one night I called on the priest with my hat over my eyes and said that I wanted him to come to a dying traveller. He would not have come then, but I told him that the dying man was rich and was a great person. He mounted the horse I had brought, and we rode to a little house on the mountain.﻿ ﻿… I locked the door and he turned round﻿—so! Trapped, and he knew it. ‘What are you going to do?’ he said with a gasping noise. ‘I am going to kill you, señor,’ I said, and he believed me. I told him the story of the girl.﻿ ﻿… He screamed when I moved towards him, but he might as well have saved his breath. ‘Let me see a priest,’ he begged; and I handed him﻿—a mirror.”

===Chinese===
「听着，」寡言少语的波伊卡特插话，「从前有个姑娘，年轻，漂亮，就在北边」——他凭着分毫不差的直觉朝北一挥手——「还有一个神父——一个神父，你懂吗——做父母的对此睁一只眼闭一只眼，这种事向来多得很……可那姑娘满心憎恶与羞耻，不肯去第二次，他便设局困住她，把她关在一座宅子里，等花容凋残又把她赶出门，是我找到了她。她与我素不相识，可我说：『这里有一桩冤屈，法律没法给它足够的昭雪。』于是一天夜里，我压着帽檐去拜访那位神父，说想请他去见一个垂死的旅人。他本不肯来，我告诉他那垂死的人有钱，是个大人物。他骑上我牵来的马，我们一路骑到山上一座小屋。……我锁上门，他转过身来——就这样！落入陷阱，他心里明白。『你要干什么？』他喘着气问。『我要杀了你，先生（señor）。』我说。他信了。我把那姑娘的事讲给他听……我朝他走去时他尖叫起来，可他不如省省力气。『让我见一位神父。』他哀求道；我便递给他——一面镜子。」

===Original===
Poiccart stopped to sip his coffee.

“They found him on the road next day without a mark to show how he died,” he said simply.

“How?” Thery bent forward eagerly, but Poiccart permitted himself to smile grimly, and made no response.

===Chinese===
波伊卡特停下来，呷了一口咖啡。

「第二天，人们在路上发现了他，周身上下找不到一点致命的痕迹。」他平平淡淡地说。

「怎么弄的？」特里急切地探身向前，波伊卡特却只冷冷一笑，不置一词。

===Original===
Thery bent his brows and looked suspiciously from one to the other.

“If you can kill as you say you can, why have you sent for me? I was happy in Jerez working at the wine factory﻿ ﻿… there is a girl there﻿ ﻿… they call her Juana Samarez.” He mopped his forehead and looked quickly from one to the other. “When I received your message I thought I should like to kill you﻿—whoever you were﻿—you understand I am happy﻿ ﻿… and there is the girl﻿—and the old life I have forgotten﻿—”

===Chinese===
特里皱起眉，满腹狐疑地把三人挨个瞧过去。

「你们要真像嘴上说的那样能杀人，干吗还派人叫我？我在赫雷斯（Jerez）的酒厂干活，本来快快活活……那边有个姑娘……人家管她叫胡安娜·萨马雷斯（Juana Samarez）。」他抹了抹额头，飞快地把三人挨个瞧了一遍。「接到你们口信那会儿，我想的是干脆把你们杀了——不管你们是谁——你们明白，我快快活活的……又有那姑娘……还有那种我已经忘掉的旧日子——」

===Original===
Manfred arrested the incoherent protests.

“Listen,” said he imperiously; “it is not for you to inquire the wherefore and the why; we know who you are and what you are; we know more of you even than the police know, for we could send you to the garotte.”

Poiccart nodded his head in affirmation, and Gonsalez looked at Thery curiously, like the student of human nature that he was.

“We want a fourth man,” went on Manfred, “for something we wish to do; we would have wished to have had one animated by no other desire than to see justice done. Failing that, we must have a criminal, a murderer if you like.”

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德止住了这番语无伦次的抱怨。

「听着，」他不容分说，「不该你刨根问底；我们知道你是谁、是干什么的；我们对你的底细比警察知道的还多，因为我们能送你上西班牙绞刑（garotte）。」

波伊卡特点头称是，冈萨雷斯则一副天生的研究人性的学者做派，好奇地打量着特里。

「我们要办一件事，缺个第四人，」曼弗雷德接着说，「本来巴不得能找到一个除了眼见正义伸张、别无他念的人。这样的人寻不着，我们就得用一个罪犯——你要愿意，说凶手也行。」

===Original===
Thery opened and shut his mouth as if about to speak.

“One whom we can at a word send to his death if he fails us; you are the man; you will run no risk; you will be well rewarded; you may not be asked to slay. Listen,” went on Manfred, seeing that Thery had opened his mouth to speak. “Do you know England? I see that you do not. You know Gibraltar? Well, this is the same people. It is a country up there”﻿—Manfred’s expressive hands waved north﻿—“a curious, dull country, with curious, dull people. There is a man, a member of the Government, and there are men whom the Government have never heard of. You remember one: Garcia, Manuel Garcia, leader in the Carlist movement; he is in England; it is the only country where he is safe; from England he directs the movement here, the great movement. You know of what I speak?”

===Chinese===
特里张了张嘴，像要说话。

「一个坏了我们的事、我们一句话就能打发去死的人；你就是这个人；你不会担任何风险；你会得到重赏；也未必就叫你动手杀人。听着，」见特里张嘴要说话，曼弗雷德继续道，「你了解英国吗？我看你不了解。你知道直布罗陀？唔，那边就是同一族人。那是个北边的国家」——曼弗雷德那双富于表情的手朝北一挥——「一个古怪、乏味的国家，住着一群古怪、乏味的人。那里有一个人，是政府的一员；还有些人，是政府从来没有听说过的。有一个你记得：加西亚，曼努埃尔·加西亚，卡洛斯派运动的领袖；他就在英国；那是唯一能容他安身的国家；他在英国遥控着这边的运动，那场大运动。你知道我说的是什么？」

===Original===
Thery nodded.

“This year as well as last there has been a famine, men have been dying about the church doors, starving in the public squares; they have watched corrupt Government succeed corrupt Government; they have seen millions flow from the public treasury into the pockets of politicians. This year something will happen; the old regime must go. The Government know this; they know where the danger lies, they know their salvation can only come if Garcia is delivered into their hands before the organization for revolt is complete. But Garcia is safe for the present, and would be safe for all time were it not for a member of the English Government, who is about to introduce and pass into law a bill. When that is passed, Garcia is as good as dead. You must help us to prevent that from ever becoming law; that is why we have sent for you.”

===Chinese===
特里点头。

「今年和去年一样闹了饥荒，教堂门口倒毙了人，公共广场上有人活活饿死；他们眼看一届腐败政府接着一届腐败政府；眼看千百万公款流进政客的腰包。今年要出大事；旧政权非倒不可。政府知道这一点；他们知道危险在哪儿，也知道唯有赶在叛乱组织大功告成之前，把加西亚交到他们手里，他们才有活路。可加西亚眼下安然无恙，若不是英国政府里有一位成员要提出并通过一项法案，他会一直安然无恙下去。那法案一旦通过，加西亚就等于死定了。你必须帮我们阻止它变成法律；这就是我们叫你来的缘故。」

===Original===
Thery looked bewildered. “But how?” he stammered.

Manfred drew a paper from his pocket and handed it to Thery. “This, I think,” he said, speaking deliberately, “is an exact copy of the police description of yourself.” Thery nodded. Manfred leant over and, pointing to a word that occurred halfway down the sheet, “Is that your trade?” he said.

Thery looked puzzled. “Yes,” he replied.

“Do you really know anything about that trade?” asked Manfred earnestly; and the other two men leant forward to catch the reply.

===Chinese===
特里一脸茫然。「可是……怎么阻止？」他结结巴巴地说。

曼弗雷德从口袋里抽出一张纸，递给特里。「我想，这一张，」他字斟句酌地说，「是警方那份关于你的相貌描述的分毫不差的抄件。」特里点头。曼弗雷德俯过身，指着纸页半腰上的一个词：「那是你的本行？」他说。

特里显出困惑。「是。」他答。

「这一行当真在行？」曼弗雷德恳切地问；另外两人也探身过来，要听他怎么回答。

===Original===
“I know,” said Thery slowly, “everything there is to be known: had it not been for a﻿—mistake I might have earned great money.”

Manfred heaved a sigh of relief and nodded to his two companions.

“Then,” he said briskly, “the English minister is a dead man.”

===Chinese===
「该知道的，」特里缓缓地说，「我全知道：要不是出了——一桩岔子，我这会儿早挣了大钱了。」

曼弗雷德如释重负地舒了口气，朝两位同伴点点头。

「那么，」他干脆利落地说，「那位英国大臣已经是个死人了。」

## II A Newspaper Story / 二 一桩报纸新闻
===Original===
On the fourteenth day of August 190‒ a tiny paragraph appeared at the foot of an unimportant page in London’s most sober journal to the effect that the secretary of state for foreign affairs had been much annoyed by the receipt of a number of threatening letters, and was prepared to pay a reward of fifty pounds to any person who would give such information as would lead to the apprehension and conviction of the person or persons, etc. The few people who read London’s most sober journal thought, in their ponderous Athenaeum Club way, that it was a remarkable thing that a minister of state should be annoyed at anything; more remarkable that he should advertise his annoyance, and most remarkable of all that he could imagine for one minute that the offer of a reward would put a stop to the annoyance.

News editors of less sober but larger circulated newspapers, wearily scanning the dull columns of *Old Sobriety*, read the paragraph with a newly acquired interest.

===Chinese===
190‒ 年 8 月 14 日，伦敦最严肃持重的报纸在一版不起眼的页面下角，登出一段小启事，大意说：外交大臣因接连收到多封恐吓信而深感困扰，愿出五十英镑悬赏任何提供线索、使作案者一人或数人被捕定罪的人士，云云。少数几个读这份伦敦最严肃持重的报纸的人，以他们雅典娜俱乐部式的迂腐腔调想：一位国务大臣竟会被什么事搅扰，已是奇事；他竟把这份搅扰登报告知，更是奇事；而最奇的，是他居然会幻想一笔悬赏便能让这搅扰就此止息。

几家发行量更大、却没那么庄重的报纸的新闻编辑，正没精打采地翻着「老古板」（*Old Sobriety*）那些乏味的版面，读到这段启事，忽然来了兴致。

===Original===
“Hullo, what’s this?” asked Smiles of the *Comet*, and cut out the paragraph with huge shears, pasted it upon a sheet of copy-paper and headed it:

“Who is Sir Philip’s Correspondent?” As an afterthought﻿—the *Comet* being in Opposition﻿—he prefixed an introductory paragraph, humorously suggesting that the letters were from an intelligent electorate, grown tired of the shilly-shallying methods of the Government.

The news editor of the *Evening World*﻿—a white-haired gentleman of deliberate movement﻿—read the paragraph twice, cut it out carefully, read it again and, placing it under a paperweight, very soon forgot all about it.

The news editor of the *Megaphone*, which is a very bright newspaper indeed, cut the paragraph as he read it, rang a bell, called a reporter, all in a breath so to speak, and issued a few terse instructions.

===Chinese===
「喂，这是什么？」《彗星报》（*Comet*）的斯迈尔斯（Smiles）问道，随即用大剪刀把这段启事剪下来，贴到一张稿纸上，加了个标题：

「菲利普爵士的通信人是谁？」转念一想——《彗星报》站在反对党一边——他又在前头添了一段引子，打趣说这些信件出自一个聪明的选民团体，他们受够了政府优柔寡断的做派。

《夜世界报》（*Evening World*）的新闻编辑——一位举止从容的白发绅士——把这段启事读了两遍，仔细剪下，又读一遍，压在镇纸底下，很快便忘了个一干二净。

《传声筒报》（*Megaphone*）的新闻编辑——这份报可是机灵透顶——一边读一边就剪了下来，按铃，唤来一名记者，可以说一口气完成，然后下了几道简短的指令。

===Original===
“Go down to Portland Place, try to see Sir Philip Ramon, secure the story of that paragraph﻿—why he is threatened, what he is threatened with; get a copy of one of the letters if you can. If you cannot see Ramon, get hold of a secretary.”

And the obedient reporter went forth.

===Chinese===
「去一趟波特兰街（Portland Place），设法见上菲利普·拉蒙爵士（Sir Philip Ramon），把这段启事的来龙去脉挖出来——他为什么受威胁，受的什么威胁；能弄到一封信的抄件更好。见不着拉蒙，就抓个秘书。」

听话的记者领命而去。

===Original===
He returned in an hour in that state of mysterious agitation peculiar to the reporter who has got a “beat.” The news editor duly reported to the editor-in-chief, and that great man said, “That’s very good, that’s, very good indeed”﻿—which was praise of the highest order.

What was “very good indeed” about the reporter’s story may be gathered from the half-column that appeared in the *Megaphone* on the following day:

===Chinese===
一小时后他回来了，带着记者抢到「独家」时特有的一股子神秘亢奋。新闻编辑如实禀报主编，那位大人物说：「不错，这真是——非常之好。」——这已是最高规格的褒奖。

记者这条稿子「非常之好」在哪儿，从次日《传声筒报》登出的半栏文章里可见一斑：

===Original===
> Considerable comment was excited by the appearance in the news columns of yesterday’s *National Journal* of the following paragraph:

> So unusual was such an announcement, remembering that anonymous and threatening letters are usually to be found daily in the letter-bags of every statesman and diplomat, that the *Daily Megaphone* immediately instituted inquiries as to the cause for this unusual departure.

> A representative of this newspaper called at the residence of Sir Philip Ramon, who very courteously consented to be seen.

===Chinese===
> 昨日《国民日报》（*National Journal*）新闻栏刊出如下一段启事，引起颇多议论：

> 要知道，匿名恐吓信原是每位政治家、外交官每天的信袋里都少不了的东西，这样一纸公告实属罕见，《每日传声筒报》遂立即着手探询这桩破例之举的缘由。

> 本报代表登门拜访菲利普·拉蒙爵士宅邸，爵士彬彬有礼地接受了采访。

===Original===
> “It is quite an unusual step to take,” said the great Foreign Secretary, in answer to our representative’s question, “but it has been taken with the full concurrence of my colleagues of the Cabinet. We have reasons to believe there is something behind the threats, and I might say that the matter has been in the hands of the police for some weeks past.

> “Here is one of the letters,” and Sir Philip produced a sheet of foreign notepaper from a portfolio, and was good enough to allow our representative to make a copy.

> It was undated, and beyond the fact that the handwriting was of the flourishing, effeminate variety that is characteristic of the Latin races, it was written in good English.

> It ran:

===Chinese===
> 「这一步确实不同寻常，」这位显赫的外交大臣回答本报代表的提问说，「但它是征得内阁同僚一致同意后迈出的。我们有理由相信，这些威胁背后另有文章；我还可以说，此事交到警方手里，已有些时日了。

> 「这里是一封信。」菲利普爵士从文件夹里抽出一页外国信笺，并好意允许本报代表抄录了一份。

> 信不落日期；字迹是拉丁民族特有的那种花哨柔媚一路，除此之外，倒是一手漂亮的英文。

> 信文如下：

===Original===
> “The Bill referred to,” Sir Philip resumed, “is of course the Aliens Extradition (Political Offences) Bill, which, had it not been for the tactics of the Opposition, might have passed quietly into law last session.”

> Sir Philip went on to explain that the Bill was called into being by the insecurity of the succession in Spain.

> “It is imperative that neither England nor any other country should harbour propagandists who, from the security of these, or other shores, should set Europe ablaze. Coincident with the passage of this measure similar Acts or proclamations have been made in every country in Europe. In fact, they are all in existence, having been arranged to come into law simultaneously with ours, last session.”

===Chinese===
> 「信里提到的那项法案，」菲利普爵士接着说，「自然是《外侨引渡（政治罪）法案》（Aliens Extradition (Political Offences) Bill）。若不是反对党的策略作梗，上届会期它本可悄无声息地通过成为法律。」

> 菲利普爵士进而解释说，这项法案之所以问世，是因为西班牙的王位继承危机四伏。

> 「无论英国还是别国，都绝不能窝藏鼓动家，让他们凭借这片或那片土地的安全，去把欧洲点着。与本措施同步，欧洲各国也已颁布类似的法令或公告。事实上它们都已就绪，只等与我们的法案在上届会期同时生效。」

===Original===
> “Why do you attach importance to these letters?” asked the *Daily Megaphone* representative.

> “Because we are assured, both by our own police and the continental police, that the writers are men who are in deadly earnest. The ‘**Four Just Men**,’ as they sign themselves, are known collectively in almost every country under the sun. Who they are individually we should all very much like to know. Rightly or wrongly, they consider that justice as meted out here on earth is inadequate, and have set themselves about correcting the law. They were the people who assassinated General Trelovitch, the leader of the Serbian Regicides; they hanged the French Army Contractor Conrad in the Place de la Concorde﻿—with a hundred policemen within call. They shot Hermann le Blois, the poet-philosopher, in his study for corrupting the youth of the world with his reasoning.”

===Chinese===
> 「您为何如此看重这些信件？」《每日传声筒报》代表问。

> 「因为我国警方和欧陆警方都向我们担保，写信的人是玩真的。『**四义士**』（The Four Just Men）——他们是这么署名的——普天之下几乎无人不知其名号。至于他们究竟是哪四个，我们谁都巴不得知道。不论对错，他们认为尘世间执行的正义并不完善，便自任其职，要修正法律。刺杀塞尔维亚弑君党首领特雷洛维奇将军（General Trelovitch）的是他们；在协和广场（Place de la Concorde）绞死法国军需承包商康拉德（Conrad）——方圆之内随时可召集上百警察——的也是他们；诗人哲学家赫尔曼·勒布卢瓦（Hermann le Blois）以他的歪理毒害世界青年，他们便在他的书房里枪杀了他。」

===Original===
> The Foreign Secretary then handed to our representative a list of the crimes committed by this extraordinary quartet.

> Our readers will recollect the circumstance of each murder, and it will be remembered that until today﻿—so closely have the police of the various nationalities kept the secret of the Four Men﻿—no one crime has been connected with the other; and certainly none of the circumstances which, had they been published, would have assuredly revealed the existence of this band, have been given to the public before today.

> The *Daily Megaphone* is able to publish a full list of sixteen murders committed by the four men.

===Chinese===
> 随后，外交大臣把这份离奇四人组的罪案清单交给了本报代表。

> 本报读者对每桩命案的案情都会有所记忆；也应记得，直到今天——各国警方把这个「四义士」的秘密守得如此之严——没有一桩案子与另一桩被联系到一起；而那些一旦公布便必然暴露这伙人存在的情节，在今天之前，也从未有任何一点透露给公众。

> 《每日传声筒报》现在得以公布这四人全部十六起命案的完整清单。

===Original===
> “Two years ago, after the shooting of le Blois, by some hitch in their almost perfect arrangements, one of the four was recognized by a detective as having been seen leaving le Blois’s house on the Avenue Kléber, and he was shadowed for three days, in the hope that the four might be captured together. In the end he discovered he was being watched, and made a bolt for liberty. He was driven to bay in a café in Bordeaux﻿—they had followed him from Paris; and before he was killed he shot a sergeant de ville and two other policemen. He was photographed, and the print was circulated throughout Europe, but who he was or what he was, even what nationality he was, is a mystery to this day.”

> “But the four are still in existence?”

> Sir Philip shrugged his shoulders. “They have either recruited another, or they are working short-handed,” he said.

===Chinese===
> 「两年前，勒布卢瓦被枪杀之后，因为他们那近乎天衣无缝的安排出了一点岔子，四人中的一个被一名侦探认出，正是有人看见离开克莱贝尔大街（Avenue Kléber）勒布卢瓦宅邸的那个人，于是被盯梢三天，指望把四个人一网打尽。末了他发觉自己被人盯上，拔腿就逃。他们从巴黎一路追到波尔多（Bordeaux），把他堵在一家咖啡馆里；及至被击毙前，他枪杀了一名城市巡警（sergent de ville）和另外两名警察。他的尸身被拍了照，照片传遍欧洲，但他是谁、干什么的，连是哪国人，至今仍是谜。」

> 「可这四个人如今还在？」

> 菲利普爵士耸了耸肩。「他们要么另补了一人，要么就缺着一个人照旧行事。」他说。

===Original===
> In conclusion the Foreign Secretary said:

> “I am making this public through the press, in order that the danger which threatens, not necessarily myself, but any public man who runs counter to the wishes of this sinister force, should be recognized. My second reason is that the public may in its knowledge assist those responsible for the maintenance of law and order in the execution of their office, and by their vigilance prevent the committal of further unlawful acts.”

> Inquiries subsequently made at Scotland Yard elicited no further information on the subject beyond the fact that the Criminal Investigation Department was in communication with the chiefs of the continental police.

> The following is a complete list of the murders committed by the “Four Just Men,” together with such particulars as the police have been able to secure regarding the cause for the crimes. We are indebted to the Foreign Office for permission to reproduce the list.

> (Here followed ten other cases, all on a par with those quoted above, including the cases of Trelovitch and le Blois.)

===Chinese===
> 最后，外交大臣说：

> 「我借报纸将此事公之于众，为的是让人们看清这股凶险势力所带来的危险——它威胁的不一定是我，而是任何拂逆其意愿的公职人员。第二个原因，是希望公众知情之后，能够协助负责维护治安的官员履行职责，并以自己的警觉防止进一步的非法行为发生。」

> 其后在苏格兰场（Scotland Yard）的问询没有透露更多消息，只证实刑事调查部正与欧陆各国警察首脑保持联络。

> 以下为「四义士」所犯罪行的完整清单，并附警方已掌握的作案缘由。本报蒙外交部惠允转录此单，谨此致谢。

> （此后另有十案，皆与上述诸案不相上下，其中包括特雷洛维奇案与勒布卢瓦案。）

===Original===
It was undoubtedly a great story.

The editor-in-chief, seated in his office, read it over again and said, “Very good indeed.”

The reporter﻿—whose name was Smith﻿—read it over and grew pleasantly warm at the consequences of his achievement.

The foreign secretary read it in bed as he sipped his morning tea, and frowningly wondered if he had said too much.

The chief of the French police read it﻿—translated and telegraphed﻿—in *Le Temps*, and furiously cursed the talkative Englishman who was upsetting his plans.

In Madrid, at the Café de la Paix, in the Place of the Sun, Manfred, cynical, smiling, and sarcastic, read extracts to three men﻿—two pleasantly amused, the other heavy-jowled and pasty of face, with the fear of death in his eyes.

===Chinese===
这无疑是条了不起的新闻。

主编坐在自己办公室里重读一遍，说：「非常之好。」

那记者——名叫史密斯（Smith）——把它重读一遍，想到自己这番成就的后果，心里暖洋洋的。

外交大臣在床上就着早茶读罢，皱起眉头，思忖自己是不是说得太多了。

法国警察总监读到它时，它已被译出、电传，登在《时报》（*Le Temps*）上；他破口大骂那个多嘴的英国人搅了自己的布局。

在马德里太阳门广场（Place of the Sun）的和平咖啡馆（Café de la Paix）里，曼弗雷德玩世不恭、笑吟吟、话里带刺，正给三个人念着选段——两个听得津津有味，第三个腮帮沉重、面色灰白，一双眼睛里满是死亡的恐惧。

## III The Faithful Commons / 三 忠诚的下议院
===Original===
Somebody﻿—was it Mr. Gladstone?﻿—placed it on record that there is nothing quite so dangerous, quite so ferocious, quite so terrifying, as a mad sheep. Similarly, as we know, there is no person quite so indiscreet, quite so foolishly talkative, quite so amazingly gauche, as the diplomat who for some reason or other has run off the rails.

There comes a moment to the man who has trained himself to guard his tongue in the councils of nations, who has been schooled to walk warily amongst pitfalls digged cunningly by friendly powers, when the practice and precept of many years are forgotten, and he behaves humanly. Why this should be has never been discovered by ordinary people, although the psychological minority who can generally explain the mental processes of their fellows have doubtless very adequate and convincing reasons for these acts of dis-balancement.

Sir Philip Ramon was a man of peculiar temperament. I doubt whether anything in the wide world would have arrested his purpose once his mind had been made up. He was a man of strong character, a firm, square-jawed, big-mouthed man, with that shade of blue in his eyes that one looks for in peculiarly heartless criminals and particularly famous generals. And yet Sir Philip Ramon feared, as few men imagined he feared, the consequence of the task he had set himself.

There are thousands of men who are physically heroes and morally poltroons, men who would laugh at death﻿—and live in terror of personal embarrassment. Coroners’ courts listen daily to the tale of such men’s lives﻿—and deaths.

The foreign secretary reversed these qualities. Good animal men would unhesitatingly describe the minister as a coward, for he feared pain and he feared death.

===Chinese===
有人——是格莱斯顿先生吧？——留下过一句名言：天下再没有什么比一只发疯的羊更危险、更凶残、更骇人的了。同理，众所周知，也再没有人比某个不明缘由脱了轨的外交官更不谨慎、更饶舌犯傻、更笨拙得惊人的了。

对于一个在列国会议上练就了守口如瓶本事的人，一个受过训、能在友邦狡狯掘下的陷阱之间谨慎行走的人，总会有那么一个时刻——多年的修习与信条尽数忘却，他行事忽然像个凡人了。缘何如此，普通人始终未曾参透；倒是那少数惯于解释同胞心理活动的心理学家们，对这种天平失准之举，想必自有一套充分且令人信服的解释。

菲利普·拉蒙爵士性情异于常人。我怀疑这茫茫世上还有什么能拦得住他，只要他拿定了主意。他是个性格坚强的人，下巴方硬、嘴巴阔大，眼睛里那一种蓝色，是人们只在特别冷酷的罪犯和特别著名的将领身上寻得到的。然而菲利普·拉蒙爵士心里的恐惧，却远超旁人的想象——他怕的是他给自己定下的这桩任务的后果。

世上有成千上万的人，身体上是英雄，道德上是懦夫；他们能笑对死亡——却活在对个人难堪的恐惧里。死因裁判法庭每天听取的，便是这种人的一生——与一死。

外交大臣把这些品质颠倒了个个儿。血气充沛的好汉们会毫不犹豫地说这位大臣是个懦夫，因为他怕痛，也怕死。

===Original===
“If this thing is worrying you so much,” the premier said kindly﻿—it was at the cabinet council two days following the publication of the *Megaphone*’s story﻿—“why don’t you drop the bill? After all, there are matters of greater importance to occupy the time of the House, and we are getting near the end of the session.”

An approving murmur went round the table.

“We have every excuse for dropping it. There must be a horrible slaughtering of innocents﻿—Braithwaite’s Unemployed Bill must go; and what the country will say to that, Heaven only knows.”

===Chinese===
「这件事既然叫你如此烦心，」首相和颜悦色地说——这是《传声筒报》那篇报道见报两天后的内阁会议上——「何不把法案撤了？说到底，还有更要紧的事占用议院的时间呢，况且会期也快到头了。」

一圈附和的低语绕桌而行。

「咱们要撤它，借口有的是。总得有一场对无辜法案的可怕屠戮——布雷思韦特（Braithwaite）的《失业法案》就非搁下不可；国人对此会说什么，只有天晓得。」

===Original===
“No, no!” The foreign secretary brought his fist down on the table with a crash. “It shall go through; of that I am determined. We are breaking faith with the Cortes, we are breaking faith with France, we are breaking faith with every country in the Union. I have promised the passage of this measure﻿—and we must go through with it, even though there are a thousand ‘Just Men,’ and a thousand threats.”

The premier shrugged his shoulders.

===Chinese===
「不，不！」外交大臣一拳砸在桌上，砰然作响。「它必须通过，我意已决。我们这是在对西班牙议会失信，对法国失信，对联盟里每一个国家失信。我许诺过要让这项措施通过——我们就必须办到底，哪怕有一千个『义士』，一千道威胁。」

首相耸了耸肩。

===Original===
“Forgive me for saying so, Ramon,” said Bolton, the solicitor general; “but I can’t help feeling you were rather indiscreet to give particulars to the press as you did. Yes, I know we were agreed that you should have a free hand to deal with the matter as you wished, but somehow I did not think you would have been quite so﻿—what shall I say?﻿—candid.”

“My discretion in the matter, Sir George, is not a subject that I care to discuss,” replied Ramon stiffly.

Later, as he walked across Palace Yard with the youthful-looking chancellor, Mr. Solicitor-General, smarting under the rebuff, said, apropos of nothing, “Silly old ass.” And the youthful guardian of Britain’s finances smiled.

===Chinese===
「恕我直言，拉蒙，」副检察长博尔顿（Bolton）说，「可我总觉得，你把细节那样捅给报界，未免欠考虑。是，我知道我们说好由你全权处置此事，可我怎么也没想到你会那么——怎么说呢——坦白。」

「我在这件事上的分寸，乔治爵士，不是我愿意讨论的话题。」拉蒙生硬地答道。

稍后，副检察长先生与那位看上去少壮的财政大臣并肩穿过宫廷大院，挨了抢白正憋着气，没头没脑冒出一句：「蠢老头儿。」年轻的英国财政守护人微微一笑。

===Original===
“If the truth be told,” he said, “Ramon is in a most awful funk. The story of the Four Just Men is in all the clubs, and a man I met at the Carlton at lunch has rather convinced me that there is really something to be feared. He was quite serious about it﻿—he’s just returned from South America and has seen some of the work done by these men.”

“What was that?”

“A president or something of one of these rotten little republics﻿ ﻿… about eight months ago﻿—you’ll see it in the list.﻿ ﻿… They hanged him﻿ ﻿… most extraordinary thing in the world. They took him out of bed in the middle of the night, gagged him, blindfolded him, carried him to the public jail, gained admission, and hanged him on the public gallows﻿—and escaped!”

Mr. Solicitor-General saw the difficulties of such proceedings, and was about to ask for further information when an undersecretary buttonholed the chancellor and bore him off. “Absurd,” muttered Mr. Solicitor crossly.

===Chinese===
「实话实说吧，」他道，「拉蒙怕得要命。四义士的故事传遍了各家俱乐部，午餐时我在卡尔顿俱乐部碰到一个人，倒真让我相信这事确有可怕之处。他那副样子极其郑重——他刚从南美回来，亲眼见过这几位的手笔。」

「什么手笔？」

「这些烂糟糟的小共和国里某一个的总统之类……大约八个月前——清单上查得到。……他们绞死了他……天下最离奇的一件事。他们半夜把他从床上拎起来，堵住嘴，蒙上眼，抬到公立监狱，闯了进去，在狱中的绞架上把他绞死——然后全身而退！」

副检察长先生看出了此举的难处，正要细问，一名次官已一把拽住财政大臣，把他架走了。「荒唐。」副检察长先生悻悻地咕哝。

===Original===
There were cheers for the secretary for foreign affairs as his brougham swept through the crowd that lined the approaches to the House. He was in no wise exalted, for popularity was not a possession he craved. He knew instinctively that the cheers were called forth by the public’s appreciation of his peril; and the knowledge chilled and irritated him. He would have liked to think that the people scoffed at the existence of this mysterious four﻿—it would have given him some peace of mind had he been able to think “the people have rejected the idea.”

For although popularity or unpopularity was outside his scheme of essentials, yet he had an unswerving faith in the brute instincts of the mob. He was surrounded in the lobby of the House with a crowd of eager men of his party, some quizzical, some anxious, all clamouring for the latest information﻿—all slightly in fear of the acid-tongued minister.

===Chinese===
外交大臣的四轮马车辚辚驶过聚集在议院入口通道两侧的人群，欢呼声四起。他丝毫没有飘飘然，声望本非他渴求之物。他本能地知道，这阵阵欢呼出自公众对他身陷险境的激赏；这认知令他心冷，也令他恼火。他倒宁愿相信民众对这神秘四人组的存在嗤之以鼻——他若能想「民众根本不信这一套」，心里反倒安宁几分。

因为，受不受欢迎虽在他的要务清单之外，他对人群的畜生本能却怀有毫不动摇的信仰。议院走廊里，他被本党一群热切的人围住，有的揶揄，有的焦虑，人人嚷着要最新消息——人人对这位舌头带刺的大臣都有几分忌惮。

===Original===
“Look here, Sir Philip”﻿—it was the stout, tactless member for West Brondesbury﻿—“what is all this we hear about threatenin’ letters? Surely you’re not goin’ to take notice of things of that sort﻿—why, I get two or three every day of my life.”

The minister strode impatiently away from the group, but Tester﻿—the member﻿—caught his arm.

“Look here﻿—” he began.

“Go to the devil,” said the foreign secretary plainly, and walked quickly to his room.

“Beastly temper that man’s got, to be sure,” said the honourable member despairingly. “Fact is, old Ramon’s in a blue funk. The idea of making a song about threatenin’ letters! Why, I get﻿—”

===Chinese===
「我说，菲利普爵士，」说话的是西布伦兹伯里选区那位心宽体胖、不知忌讳的议员，「我们听说的这些恐吓信都是怎么回事？你总不会把这类玩意儿当真吧——咳，我这辈子天天都要收上两三封呢。」

大臣不耐烦地大步离开人群，却被特斯特（Tester）——那位议员——一把抓住了胳膊。

「我说——」他开口。

「见鬼去吧。」外交大臣说得干脆，快步走向自己的房间。

「这家伙脾气真是坏透了，没跑。」尊敬的议员绝望地说。「实情是，老拉蒙吓破胆了。为几封恐吓信就闹得天翻地覆！咳，我还收——」

===Original===
A group of men in the members’ smoke-room discussed the question of the Just Four in a perfectly unoriginal way.

“It’s too ridiculous for words,” said one oracularly. “Here are four men, a mythical four, arrayed against all the forces and established agencies of the most civilized nation on earth.”

“Do you think Ramon will withdraw the Bill?” asked the senior member for Aldgate East.

“Ramon? Not he﻿—he’d sooner die.”

“It’s a most unusual circumstance,” said Aldgate East; and three boroughs, a London suburb, and a midland town nodded and “thought it was.”

===Chinese===
议员吸烟室里，一群人正用最毫无新意的方式讨论着四义士的问题。

「荒唐得没法说。」有人以先知的口吻道。「四个人，子虚乌有的四个人，公然与地球上最文明国家的全部武力和建制机构为敌。」

「你看拉蒙会撤法案吗？」阿尔德盖特东区（Aldgate East）的资深议员问。

「拉蒙？他才不呢——他宁可死。」

「这真是极不寻常的局面。」阿尔德盖特东区说；于是三个自治市镇、一个伦敦郊区、一座中部城镇纷纷点头，齐称「确实如此」。

===Original===
“In the old days, when old Bascoe was a young member”﻿—Aldgate East indicated an aged senator bent and white of beard and hair, who was walking painfully toward a seat﻿—“in the old days﻿—”

“Thought old Bascoe had paired?” remarked a listener irrelevantly.

“If I were Ramon,” resumed Aldgate East profoundly, “I know exactly what I should do. I should go to the police and say ‘Look here﻿—’ ”

A bell rang furiously and continuously, and the members went scampering along the corridor. “Division﻿—’vision.”

Clause Nine of the Medway Improvement Bill having been satisfactorily settled and the words “Or as may hereafter be determined” added by a triumphant majority of twenty-four, the faithful Commons returned to the interrupted discussion.

===Chinese===
「想当年，老巴斯科还是个年轻议员的时候」——阿尔德盖特东区指了指一位弯腰驼背、须发皆白、正艰难挪向座位的老参议员——「想当年——」

「不是说老巴斯科已经结好对子缺席了吗？」一名听众没头没脑地插了一句。

「我要是拉蒙，」阿尔德盖特东区深沉地续道，「我清楚自己会怎么做。我会去找警察，说：『听着——』」

铃声狂暴而不歇地响起来，议员们沿着走廊飞奔。「表决了——表决。」

《梅德韦改良法案》第九条既已圆满解决，「或依日后另行裁定之」字样又以二十四票的赫赫多数获得增补，忠诚的下议院便回到了方才被打断的讨论上。

===Original===
“What I say, and what I’ve always said about a man in the cabinet,” maintained an important individual, “is that he must, if he is a true statesman, drop all consideration for his own personal feelings.”

“Hear!” applauded somebody.

“His own personal feelings,” repeated the orator. “He must put his duty to the state before all other﻿—er﻿—considerations. You remember what I said to Barrington the other night when we were talking out the Estimates? I said, ‘The right honourable gentleman has not, cannot have, allowed for the strong and almost unanimous desires of the great body of the electorate. The action of a minister of the crown must primarily be governed by the intelligent judgment of the great body of the electorate, whose fine feelings’﻿—no﻿—‘whose higher instincts’﻿—no﻿—that wasn’t it﻿—at any rate I made it very clear what the duty of a minister was,” concluded the oracle lamely.

===Chinese===
「我一向的见解，也是我对内阁中人的定论，」一位举足轻重的人物坚称，「是：他若是真正的政治家，就必须把一己私情的一切考虑抛到一边。」

「高见！」有人喝彩。

「一己私情，」演说家重复道。「他必须把对国家的义务置于一切其他——呃——考虑之上。诸位还记得前些天夜里我们耗着预算法案闲谈时，我对巴林顿说的话吧？我说：『尊敬的诸位大人没有、也不可能体察到选民大众强烈而近乎一致的意愿。王国大臣的举动，首先必须受选民大众明智判断的支配，他们细腻的情感』——不对——『他们高尚的本能』——不对——不是这么说的——总而言之，我把大臣的义务讲得再清楚不过了。」这位先知有气无力地收了尾。

===Original===
“Now I﻿—” commenced Aldgate East, when an attendant approached with a tray on which lay a greenish-grey envelope.

“Has any gentleman dropped this?” he inquired, and, picking up the letter, the member fumbled for his eyeglasses.

“To the Members of the House of Commons,” he read, and looked over his pince-nez at the circle of men about him.

“Company prospectus,” said the stout member for West Brondesbury, who had joined the party; “I get hundreds. Only the other day﻿—”

“Too thin for a prospectus,” said Aldgate East, weighing the letter in his hand.

“Patent medicine, then,” persisted the light of Brondesbury. “I get one every morning﻿—‘Don’t burn the candle at both ends,’ and all that sort of rot. Last week a feller sent me﻿—”

“Open it,” someone suggested, and the member obeyed. He read a few lines and turned red.

“Well, I’m damned!” he gasped, and read aloud:

===Chinese===
「且听我——」阿尔德盖特东区刚起了个头，一名侍者托着盘子走来，盘上躺着一封灰绿色的信。

「是哪位先生落下的吗？」他问。议员拾起信，摸索着找眼镜。

「致下议院诸位议员。」他念道，从夹鼻眼镜上方环视围在身边的一圈人。

「股份公司招股书。」西布伦兹伯里那位胖议员也凑了过来，说道，「我一天收几百份。前几天还——」

「招股书哪有这么薄的。」阿尔德盖特东区掂了掂手里的信说。

「那就是卖专利药的。」布伦兹伯里的这位贤才锲而不舍，「我每天早上都收一封——什么『莫要两头点蜡烛』，全是那路废话。上礼拜还有个家伙给我寄——」

「拆开呗。」有人提议，议员从善如流。读了几行，他涨红了脸。

「好家伙，见鬼了！」他倒吸一口气，朗声念道：

===Original===
> Citizens,

> The Government is about to pass into law a measure which will place in the hands of the most evil Government of modern times men who are patriots and who are destined to be the saviours of their countries. We have informed the minister in charge of this measure, the title of which appears in the margin, that unless he withdraws this Bill we will surely slay him.

> We are loath to take this extreme step, knowing that otherwise he is an honest and brave gentleman, and it is with a desire to avoid fulfilling our promise that we ask the members of the Mother of Parliaments to use their every influence to force the withdrawal of this Bill.

> Were we common murderers or clumsy anarchists we could with ease wreak a blind and indiscriminate vengeance on the members of this assembly, and in proof thereof, and as an earnest that our threat is no idle one, we beg you to search beneath the table near the recess in this room. There you will find a machine sufficiently charged to destroy the greater portion of this building.

> (Signed) Four Just Men

> Postscript.﻿—We have not placed either detonator or fuse in the machine, which may therefore be handled with impunity.

===Chinese===
> 公民们：

> 本政府即将把一项措施付诸法律，而这项措施将把一批爱国志士、一批注定成为本国救星的人，交到现代最邪恶的政府手中。我们已通知主管该措施的大臣——其案名见页边——倘若他不撤回此案，我们必将杀死他。

> 我们本不愿走此极端，深知除此一事之外，他是一位正直勇敢的君子；正为着不愿兑现我们的承诺，我们才吁请诸位——议会之母的议员们——竭尽各自的影响力，促使此案撤回。

> 倘若我们是寻常凶手或笨拙的无政府党人，要向本院诸君滥施一场不分皂白的报复，易如反掌。为证此言不虚，也为了让诸位确信我们的威胁绝非虚声恫吓，请诸位查看本室壁龛旁那张桌子的底下。那里有一台装足了药量的机器，足以摧毁本建筑的大半。

> （签名）四义士

> 又及。——机器中未装雷管，亦未装导火索，故可随意搬动，无性命之忧。

===Original===
As the reading of the letter proceeded the faces of the listeners grew pallid.

There was something very convincing about the tone of the letter, and instinctively all eyes sought the table near the recess.

Yes, there was something, a square black something, and the crowd of legislators shrank back. For a moment they stood spellbound﻿—and then there was a mad rush for the door.

===Chinese===
信念到哪里，听众的脸便白到哪里。

这封信的口吻里有一种极能慑人的东西，所有人的目光都不由自主地投向壁龛旁那张桌子。

不错，那里有样东西，一个四四方方的黑色物事，一群立法者齐齐退缩。有那么一刻他们呆立当场——随即便是夺门而逃的疯狂奔涌。

===Original===
“Was it a hoax?” asked the prime minister anxiously, but the hastily summoned expert from Scotland Yard shook his head.

“Just as the letter described it,” he said gravely, “even to the absence of fuses.”

“Was it really﻿—”

“Enough to wreck the House, sir,” was the reply.

===Chinese===
「是恶作剧吗？」首相焦灼地问，匆匆召来的苏格兰场专家却摇了摇头。

「与信里描述的分毫不差，」他正色道，「连没装导火索这一点都是。」

「当真——」

「足以把议院掀上天，先生。」对方答道。

===Original===
The premier, with a troubled face, paced the floor of his private room.

He stopped once to look moodily through the window that gave a view of a crowded terrace and a mass of excited politicians gesticulating and evidently all speaking at once.

“Very, very serious﻿—very, very serious,” he muttered. Then aloud, “We’ve said so much we might as well continue. Give the newspapers as full an account of this afternoon’s happenings as you think necessary﻿—give them the text of the letter.” He pushed a button, and his secretary entered noiselessly.

===Chinese===
首相面带忧色，在私人房间里来回踱步。

他停下过一次，闷闷地望向窗外：露台上人头攒动，一群激动的政客指手画脚，显见得人人同时在讲话。

「非常、非常严重——非常、非常严重。」他喃喃道。随即扬声：「话已说到这个份上，索性说下去。把今天下午的事向报界交代个尽够详尽——把信的原文也给他们。」他按了按铃，秘书悄无声息地走了进来。

===Original===
“Write to the commissioner telling him to offer a reward of a thousand pounds for the arrest of the man who left this thing, and a free pardon and the reward to any accomplice.”

The secretary withdrew and the Scotland Yard expert waited.

“Have your people found how the machine was introduced?”

“No, sir; the police have all been relieved and been subjected to separate interrogation. They remember seeing no stranger either entering or leaving the House.”

The premier pursed his lips in thought.

“Thank you,” he said simply, and the expert withdrew.

===Chinese===
「给总监写信，告诉他悬赏一千英镑缉拿放置此物之人；其从犯若肯检举，赦罪之外，赏金照给。」

秘书退下，苏格兰场专家候在一旁。

「你们的人查清那机器是怎么带进来的了吗？」

「没有，先生；警员已尽数换岗，并分别受了盘问。他们记得没有见过任何陌生人进出议院。」

首相抿唇沉思。

「谢谢你。」他淡淡地说，专家退了出去。

===Original===
On the terrace Aldgate East and the oratorical member divided honours.

“I must have been standing quite close to it,” said the latter impressively; “ ’pon my word it makes me go cold all over to think about it. You remember, Mellin? I was saying about the duty of the ministry﻿—”

“I asked the waiter,” said the member for Aldgate to an interested circle, “when he brought the letter: ‘Where did you find it?’ ‘On the floor, sir,’ he said. I thought it was a medicine advertisement; I wasn’t going to open it, only somebody﻿—”

“It was me,” claimed the stout gentleman from Brondesbury proudly; “you remember I was saying﻿—”

“I knew it was somebody,” continued Aldgate East graciously. “I opened it and read the first few lines. ‘Bless my soul,’ I said﻿—”

“You said, ‘Well, I’m damned,’ ” corrected Brondesbury.

“Well, I know it was something very much to the point,” admitted Aldgate East. “I read it﻿—and, you’ll quite understand, I couldn’t grasp its significance, so to speak. Well﻿—”

===Chinese===
露台上，阿尔德盖特东区与那位雄辩议员平分了荣耀。

「我方才必定就站在它近旁，」后者一字一顿地说，「凭良心讲，如今想起来还通体发冷。你记得吗，梅林（Mellin）？我当时正讲到内阁的义务——」

「侍者送信来的时候我问他，」阿尔德盖特议员对一圈热切的听众说，「『你在哪儿捡的？』『地上，先生。』他说。我还当是药品广告；本不打算拆，结果有人——」

「是我。」布伦兹伯里来的胖绅士得意地认领，「记得吧，我当时正说——」

「我就知道是有人。」阿尔德盖特东区宽宏地续道，「我拆开来，读了头几行。『我的天，』我说——」

「你说的是『好家伙，见鬼了』。」布伦兹伯里纠正道。

「唔，反正是一句极其中肯的话。」阿尔德盖特东区承认，「我读了——这个，诸位自然明白，可以说未能领会其要义。总之——」

---
===Original===
The three stalls reserved at the Star Music Hall in Oxford Street were occupied one by one. At half-past seven prompt came Manfred, dressed quietly; at eight came Poiccart, a fairly prosperous middle-aged gentleman; at half-past eight came Gonsalez, asking in perfect English for a program. He seated himself between the two others.

When pit and gallery were roaring themselves hoarse over a patriotic song, Manfred smilingly turned to Leon and said:

“I saw it in the evening papers.”

Leon nodded quickly.

“There was nearly trouble,” he said quietly. “As I went in somebody said, ‘I thought Bascoe had paired,’ and one of them almost came up to me and spoke.”

===Chinese===
牛津街（Oxford Street）明星音乐厅里预留的三个正厅前排座，被人一一坐满。七点半整，曼弗雷德来了，衣着素淡；八点，波伊卡特来了，一副相当殷实的中年绅士模样；八点半，冈萨雷斯来了，用无懈可击的英语要了一张节目单。他在另外两人中间落了座。

正厅后排与顶层楼座为一支爱国歌曲吼哑了嗓子时，曼弗雷德含笑转向莱昂，说：

「晚报上我看到了。」

莱昂飞快地点头。

「差点出了乱子。」他轻声说。「我进去的时候，有人说：『我还当巴斯科结了对子呢。』其中一个差点就走上前来跟我搭话。」

## IV One Thousand Pounds Reward / 四 一千英镑悬赏
===Original===
To say that England was stirred to its depths﻿—to quote more than one leading article on the subject﻿—by the extraordinary occurrence in the House of Commons, would be stating the matter exactly.

The first intimation of the existence of the Four Just Men had been received with pardonable derision, particularly by those newspapers that were behindhand with the first news. Only the *Daily Megaphone* had truly and earnestly recognized how real was the danger which threatened the minister in charge of the obnoxious act. Now, however, even the most scornful could not ignore the significance of the communication that had so mysteriously found its way into the very heart of Britain’s most jealously guarded institution. The story of the “Bomb Outrage” filled the pages of every newspaper throughout the country, and the latest daring venture of the Four was placarded the length and breadth of the Isles.

Stories, mostly apocryphal, of the men who were responsible for the newest sensation made their appearance from day to day, and there was no other topic in the mouths of men wherever they met but the strange quartet who seemed to hold the lives of the mighty in the hollows of their hands.

===Chinese===
要说英国被下议院这桩异事搅得举国震动——这是引自不止一篇相关社论的说法——那可算分毫不差地道出了实情。

四义士存在的第一声通报，当初收到的多是情有可原的讪笑，尤其在那些错过了首发新闻的报纸那里。唯有《每日传声筒报》真切而恳切地认识到，威胁着那项惹怨法案主管大臣的危险有多实在。可如今，连最轻蔑的人也无法无视这封通信的分量——它竟神秘莫测地渗进了英国戒备最严的机构的心脏。「炸弹暴行」的故事占满了全国各地报纸的版面，四义士最新一次胆大包天的行动，贴遍了不列颠诸岛的天南地北。

关于这几桩最新轰动的始作俑者，种种故事——多半查无实据——日复一日地冒出来；人们无论在哪里碰头，嘴上再没有别的话题，只有那奇怪的四人组：他们仿佛把大人物的性命攥在掌心。

===Original===
Never since the days of the Fenian outrages had the mind of the public been so filled with apprehension as it was during the two days following the appearance in the Commons of the “blank bomb,” as one journal felicitously described it. Perhaps there was not exactly the same kind of apprehension, since there was a general belief, which grew out of the trend of the letters, that the Four menaced none other than one man.

The first intimation of their intentions had excited widespread interest. But the fact that the threat had been launched from a small French town, and that in consequence the danger was very remote, had somehow robbed the threat of some of its force. Such was the vague reasoning of an ungeographical people that did not realize that Dax is no farther from London than Aberdeen.

But here was the hidden terror in the metropolis itself. Why, argued London, with suspicious sidelong glances, every man we rub elbows with may be one of the Four, and we none the wiser.

Heavy, black-looking posters stared down from blank walls, and filled the breadth of every police notice-board.

===Chinese===
自芬尼亚社暴行以来，公众的心还从未像「哑弹」现身下议院后那两天一般充满忧惧——「哑弹」是一家报纸的妙称。或许这忧惧也不全然是同一种，因为从两封信的口吻里生出了一个人所共信的看法：四义士威胁的，无非一人而已。

他们意图的第一声通报激起过广泛的兴趣。但那道威胁发自法兰西的一座小镇，危险因此显得遥远，不免折了威胁的几分锋芒。这便是一个地理欠通的民族含含糊糊的推想——他们不明白，达克斯（Dax）之于伦敦，并不比阿伯丁（Aberdeen）更远。

可如今，恐怖就潜藏在都城本身。伦敦嘀咕着，眼风斜斜地扫人：咱们摩肩接踵的每一个人，保不齐就是四义士之一，而咱们还蒙在鼓里。

黑沉沉的大幅告示从光秃秃的墙上俯视下来，铺满了每一块警局公告板的宽度。

===Original===
> **Whereas**, on August 18, at about 4:30 o’clock in the afternoon, an infernal machine was deposited in the Members’ Smoke-Room by some person or persons unknown.
> **And Whereas** there is reason to believe that the person or persons implicated in the disposal of the aforesaid machine are members of an organized body of criminals known as “The Four Just Men,” against whom warrants have been issued on charges of wilful murder in London, Paris, New York, New Orleans, Sattle (U.S.A.), Barcelona, Tomsk, Belgrade, Christiana, Capetown and Caracas.
> Now, **Therefore**, the above reward will be paid by his Majesty’s Government to any person or persons who shall lay such information as shall lead to the apprehension of any or the whole of the persons styling themselves “The Four Just Men” and identical with the band before mentioned.
> And **Furthermore**, a free pardon and the reward will be paid to any member of the band for such information, providing the person laying such information has neither committed nor has been an accessory before or after the act of any of the following murders.
> (Signed) Ryday Montgomery, 

 His Majesty’s Secretary 

 of State for Home Affairs 

 J. B. Calfort, 

 Commissioner of Police
> [Here followed a list of the sixteen crimes alleged against the four men.]
> God Save the King

===Chinese===
> **鉴于** 8 月 18 日下午 4 时 30 分许，有来历不明之一人或数人，将一枚爆炸装置置入议员吸烟室；

> **又鉴于** 有理由相信，与置放上述装置有关之一人或数人，隶属于自称「四义士」的有组织犯罪团伙；对彼等已在伦敦、巴黎、纽约、新奥尔良、萨特尔（美国）、巴塞罗那、托木斯克、贝尔格莱德、克里斯蒂安尼亚、开普敦及加拉加斯各地，以蓄意谋杀罪名签发逮捕令；

> **为此**，国王陛下政府愿将上述赏金，付与任何提供情报、致使自称「四义士」且与前述团伙为同一人等之一人或全体落网之人；

> **此外**，该团伙任何成员提供此类情报者，亦获特赦并得赏金，惟其人须未曾犯下列任何谋杀罪行，亦未于案前案后为从犯。

> （签名）里迪·蒙哥马利（Ryday Montgomery）

 国王陛下内政大臣

 J. B. 卡尔福特（J. B. Calfort）

 警察总监

> ［此处继列指控这四人十六宗罪案之清单。］
> 上帝保佑吾王

===Original===
All day long little knots of people gathered before the broadsheets, digesting the magnificent offer.

It was an unusual hue and cry, differing from those with which Londoners were best acquainted. For there was no appended description of the men wanted; no portraits by which they might be identified, no stereotyped “when last seen was wearing a dark blue serge suit, cloth cap, check tie,” on which the searcher might base his scrutiny of the passerby. It was a search for four men whom no person had ever consciously seen, a hunt for a will-o’-the-wisp, a groping in the dark after indefinite shadows.

Detective Superintendent Falmouth, who was a very plainspoken man (he once brusquely explained to a Royal Personage that he hadn’t got eyes in the back of his head), told the assistant-commissioner exactly what he thought about it.

===Chinese===
一整天，一小簇一小簇的人聚在大张告示前，消化着这份气派非凡的悬赏。

这是一场不寻常的通缉，与伦敦人最熟悉的那类全然不同。通缉的对象既没有附相貌描述，也没有可供辨认的肖像，更没有那套一成不变的「最后露面时身穿深蓝哔叽西装、戴布帽、系格子领带」，好让搜寻者据此端量过路行人。这是在搜寻四个从未被任何人正面见过的人，是追捕一缕鬼火，是在黑暗中摸索无形的影子。

法尔茅斯警司（Falmouth）是个说话极直的人（他曾冲一位王室贵人没头没脑地解释说，自己后脑勺上又没长眼睛），这回他对助理总监有啥说啥。

===Original===
“You can’t catch men when you haven’t got the slightest idea who or what you’re looking for. For the sake of argument, they might be women for all we know﻿—they might be Chinamen or niggers; they might be tall or short; they might﻿—why, we don’t even know their nationality! They’ve committed crimes in almost every country in the world. They’re not French because they killed a man in Paris, or Yankee because they strangled Judge Anderson.”

“The writing,” said the commissioner, referring to a bunch of letters he held in his hand.

“Latin; but that may be a fake. And suppose it isn’t? There’s no difference between the handwriting of a Frenchman, Spaniard, Portuguese, Italian, South American, or Creole﻿—and, as I say, it might be a fake, and probably is.”

===Chinese===
「连要找的是谁、是干什么的都没半点头绪，人就无从抓起。退一万步讲，保不齐他们是女人——兴许是中国人、黑人；兴许高，兴许矮；兴许——嗐，咱们连他们的国籍都不知道！世界上差不多每个国家都有他们作的案。不能因为他们在巴黎杀了人就说他们是法国人，也不能因为掐死了安德森法官就说他们是美国佬。」

「笔迹呢。」总监说着，翻了翻手里的一沓信。

「拉丁人的手笔；但也可能是假扮的。就算不是又如何？法国人、西班牙人、葡萄牙人、意大利人、南美人、克里奥尔人的字迹，彼此之间毫无分别——再说了，我讲了，可能是假的，八成就是假的。」

===Original===
“What have you done?” asked the commissioner.

“We’ve pulled in all the suspicious characters we know. We cleaned out Little Italy, combed Bloomsbury, been through Soho, and searched all the colonies. We raided a place at Sunhead last night﻿—a lot of Armenians live down there, but﻿—”

The detective’s face bore a hopeless look.

“As likely as not,” he went on, “we should find them at one of the swagger hotels﻿—that’s if they were fools enough to bunch together; but you may be sure they’re living apart, and meeting at some unlikely spot once or twice a day.”

===Chinese===
「你做了些什么？」总监问。

「咱们把知道的嫌疑分子全抓了。小意大利扫荡了一遍，布卢姆斯伯里梳了一遍，索霍翻了个底朝天，各处侨民聚居区都搜了。昨夜还突击了森黑德的一个地方——那一带住着好多亚美尼亚人，可是——」

侦探脸上挂着一副没指望的神气。

「十有八九，」他接着说，「咱们该去哪家阔气的旅馆找他们——那还得他们蠢到聚堆儿住；可你放心，他们必定分头落脚，一天一两次挑个出人意料的地点碰头。」

===Original===
He paused, and tapped his fingers absently on the big desk at which he and his superior sat.

“We’ve had de Courville over,” he resumed. “He saw the Soho crowd, and what is more important, saw his own man who lives amongst them﻿—and it’s none of them, I’ll swear﻿—or at least he swears, and I’m prepared to accept his word.”

The commissioner shook his head pathetically.

“They’re in an awful stew in Downing Street,” he said. “They do not know exactly what is going to happen next.”

===Chinese===
他顿了顿，心不在焉地用手指敲着他和上司共坐的那张大书桌。

「咱们把德·库维尔（de Courville）请来了。」他继续道。「他见了索霍那帮人，更要紧的是，还见了他安插在里面的自己人——那帮人里一个都不是，我敢起誓——至少他敢起誓，我打算信他的话。」

总监悲戚地摇了摇头。

「唐宁街那边乱成了一锅粥。」他说。「他们说不准接下来会发生什么。」

===Original===
Mr. Falmouth rose to his feet with a sigh and fingered the brim of his hat.

“Nice time ahead of us﻿—I don’t think,” he remarked paradoxically.

“What are the people thinking about it?” asked the commissioner.

“You’ve seen the papers?”

Mr. Commissioner’s shrug was uncomplimentary to British journalism.

===Chinese===
法尔茅斯先生叹口气站起身，捻着帽檐。

「咱们往后的日子美得很——才怪。」他倒着话锋说。

「老百姓怎么看这事？」总监问。

「报您看了吧？」

总监先生一耸肩，对英国新闻界毫无敬意。

===Original===
“The papers! Who in Heaven’s name is going to take the slightest notice of what is in the papers?” he said petulantly.

“I am, for one,” replied the calm detective; “newspapers are more often than not led by the public; and it seems to me the idea of running a newspaper, in a nutshell, is to write so that the public will say, ‘That’s smart﻿—it’s what I’ve said all along.’ ”

“But the public themselves﻿—have you had an opportunity of gathering their idea?”

===Chinese===
「报纸！天底下谁会拿报纸上的东西当半根葱？」他烦躁地说。

「我就当。」这位心平气和的侦探答道，「十有八九是报纸跟着公众走；依我看，办报的门道，一句话概括，就是写得让公众拍案：『高，实在高——正合我素来所讲。』」

「可公众自己呢——你可有机会摸摸他们的想法？」

===Original===
Detective Falmouth nodded.

“I was talking in the Park to a man only this evening﻿—a master-man by the look of him, and presumably intelligent. ‘What’s your idea of this Four Just Men business?’ I asked. ‘It’s very queer,’ he said: ‘do you think there’s anything in it?’﻿—and that,” concluded the disgusted police officer, “is all the public thinks about it.”

But if there was sorrow at Scotland Yard, Fleet Street itself was all a-twitter with pleasurable excitement. Here was great news indeed: news that might be heralded across double columns, blared forth in headlines, shouted by placards, illustrated, diagramized and illuminated by statistics.

===Chinese===
法尔茅斯警司点头。

「就在今晚，我还在公园里跟一个人聊过——瞧那派头是个工匠师傅，想必不糊涂。『这四义士的事，您怎么看？』我问。『怪得很呐，』他说，『您说这里头有门道吗？』——」这位倒足胃口的警官下了结论，「公众的想法，尽在于此了。」

苏格兰场若是愁云惨雾，舰队街可正是喜滋滋地闹翻了天。这真是了不起的大新闻：可以通栏双柱地预告、大标题地呐喊、招贴画地叫卖、配上插图、画成图解、再用统计数字照得雪亮的新调。

===Original===
“Is it the Mafia?” asked the *Comet* noisily, and went on to prove that it was.

The *Evening World*, with its editorial mind lingering lovingly in the sixties, mildly suggested a vendetta, and instanced “The Corsican Brothers.”

The *Megaphone* stuck to the story of the Four Just Men, and printed pages of details concerning their nefarious acts. It disinterred from dusty files, Continental and American, the full circumstances of each murder; it gave the portraits and careers of the men who were slain, and, whilst in no way palliating the offence of the Four, yet set forth justly and dispassionately the lives of the victims, showing the sort of men they were.

It accepted warily the reams of contributions that flowed into the office; for a newspaper that has received the stigma “yellow” exercises more caution than its more sober competitors. In newspaperland a dull lie is seldom detected, but an interesting exaggeration drives an unimaginative rival to hysterical denunciations.

===Chinese===
「是不是黑手党（Mafia）？」《彗星报》咋咋呼呼地问，接着自问自答地证明就是。

《夜世界报》的社论心肠还恋恋地留在六十年代，温吞吞地提了一句「世仇血斗」，还举《科西嘉兄弟》为例。

《传声筒报》则咬定四义士这条线不放，连篇累牍地刊印他们罪行的细节。它从欧陆和美洲积尘的旧档里，把每桩命案的始末一一掘出；它刊出被杀者的肖像与生平，一面毫不为四义士开脱，一面又公允而冷静地铺陈死者的一生，让人看清那是些什么样的人。

对滚滚涌进报馆的一摞摞来稿，它小心翼翼地照单择收；因为一家挨过「黄色」骂名的报纸，行事总比端庄的同行多几分谨慎。在新闻界，乏味的谎话少有人识破，有趣的夸张却能叫缺乏想象力的对手跳脚痛斥。

===Original===
And reams of “Four Men” anecdotes did flow in. For suddenly, as if by magic, every outside contributor, every literary gentleman who made a specialty of personal notes, every kind of man who wrote, discovered that he had known the Four intimately all his life.

“When I was in Italy﻿ ﻿…” wrote the author of *Come Again* (Hackworth Press, 6s.; “slightly soiled,” Farringdon Book Mart, 2d.), “I remember I heard a curious story about these Men of Blood﻿ ﻿…”

Or﻿—

“No spot in London is more likely to prove the hiding-place of the Four Villains than Tidal Basin,” wrote another gentleman, who stuck “Collins” in the northeast corner of his manuscript. “Tidal Basin in the reign of Charles II was known as﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===
「四人组」的轶闻稿还真是滚滚而来。因为仿佛变戏法一般，忽然间，每一位外来撰稿人、每一位以文人轶事为专长的文人雅士、每一位提得起笔的人，都发现自己与四义士神交了一辈子。

「想当年我在意大利……」《再来一次》（Hackworth Press 版，定价 6 先令；法灵顿书摊「微有污渍」处理品，2 便士）的作者写道，「我记得听到过一桩关于这几位嗜血之徒的奇闻……」

再不然——

「伦敦再没有哪处比潮汐盆（Tidal Basin）更配当四大恶徒的藏身之所了，」另一位先生写道，此人惯于在稿件右上角签上「柯林斯」三字。「查潮汐盆一地，在查理二世朝尚名曰……」

===Original===
“Who’s Collins?” asked the super-chief of the *Megaphone* of his hard-worked editor.

“A liner,” described the editor wearily, thereby revealing that even the newer journalism has not driven the promiscuous contributor from his hard-fought field; “he does police-courts, fires, inquests and things. Lately he’s taken to literature and writes Picturesque-Bits-of-Old-London and Famous-Tombstones-of-Hornsey epics.﻿ ﻿…”

Throughout the office of the newspaper the same thing was happening. Every cable that arrived, every piece of information that reached the subeditor’s basket, was coloured with the impending tragedy uppermost in men’s minds. Even the police-court reports contained some allusion to the Four. It was the overnight drunk and disorderly’s justification for his indiscretion.

“The lad has always been honest,” said the peccant errand boy’s tearful mother; “it’s reading these horrible stories about the Four Foreigners that’s made him turn out like this”; and the magistrate took a lenient view of the offence.

===Chinese===
「柯林斯是谁？」《传声筒报》的大总编问手下忙得脚不沾地的编辑。

「一个凑版面的。」编辑有气无力地形容道，这话顺带泄露：即便是新派新闻业，也没能把杂投各报的撰稿人从苦守的阵地上赶走；「警察法庭、火灾、验尸，这类活儿他都干。近来他改搞文学，写起《老伦敦风致一斑》《霍恩西名墓志》一类史诗来了。……」

报馆里上上下下，处处是同一光景。每一封到的电讯、每一件落进助理编辑稿筐的消息，都染上了压在众人心头的那桩祸事的颜色。连警察法庭的新闻里都要捎带一笔四义士。这成了隔夜醉酒滋事者开脱自己的现成理由。

「这孩子一向老实，」犯了事的小听差那泪汪汪的母亲说，「就是看了这些讲四大外国人的吓人故事，才变成这副样子」；法官便从轻发落了这桩过失。

===Original===
To all outward showing, Sir Philip Ramon, the man mostly interested in the development of the plot, was the least concerned.

He refused to be interviewed any further; he declined to discuss the possibilities of assassination, even with the premier, and his answer to letters of appreciation that came to him from all parts of the country was an announcement in the *Morning Post* asking his correspondents to be good enough to refrain from persecuting him with picture postcards, which found no other repository than his wastepaper basket.

He had thought of adding an announcement of his intention of carrying the bill through Parliament at whatever cost, and was only deterred by the fear of theatricality.

To Falmouth, upon whom had naturally devolved the duty of protecting the foreign secretary from harm, Sir Philip was unusually gracious, and incidentally permitted that astute officer to get a glimpse of the terror in which a threatened man lives.

===Chinese===
从一切表面看，菲利普·拉蒙爵士——这出戏里利害最重的人——偏偏最像个没事人。

他拒绝再受采访；绝口不谈遇刺的可能，连对首相也不谈；全国各地寄来的慰问信，他的答复是登在《晨邮报》（*Morning Post*）上的一则启事，恳请诸位通信人行行好，别再拿图画明信片来围困他——这些东西除了他的字纸篓，再无别的归宿。

他本想再加一句，宣布自己不惜代价也要把法案送过议会关，只是怕显得做戏，才作罢了。

对法尔茅斯——保护外交大臣周全的职责自然落在他肩上——菲利普爵士礼遇有加，还顺带让这位精明的警官窥见了受死亡威胁的人过着怎样的日子。

===Original===
“Do you think there’s any danger, Superintendent?” he asked, not once but a score of times; and the officer, stout defender of an infallible police force, was very reassuring.

“For,” as he argued to himself, “what is the use of frightening a man who is half scared to death already? If nothing happens, he will see I have spoken the truth, and if﻿—if﻿—well, he won’t be able to call me a liar.”

===Chinese===
「警司，你看有危险吗？」他问，问了一遭又一遭；这位自认万无一失的警队的坚定卫士，每次都好言宽慰。

「因为，」他心里这样盘算，「吓一个已经半死的人有什么用？若是无事，他自会看见我说的是实话；若是——若是——嗐，他也总不能骂我撒谎。」

===Original===
Sir Philip was a constant source of interest to the detective, who must have shown his thoughts once or twice. For the foreign secretary, who was a remarkably shrewd man, intercepting a curious glance of the police officer, said sharply, “You wonder why I still go on with the bill, knowing the danger? Well, it will surprise you to learn that I do *not* know the danger, nor can I imagine it! I have never been conscious of physical pain in my life, and in spite of the fact that I have a weak heart, I have never had so much as a single ache. What death will be, what pangs or peace it may bring, I have no conception. I argue, with Epictetus, that the fear of death is by way of being an impertinent assumption of a knowledge of the hereafter, and that we have no reason to believe it is any worse condition than our present. I am not afraid to die﻿—but I am afraid of dying.”

“Quite so, sir,” murmured the sympathetic but wholly uncomprehending detective, who had no mind for nice distinctions.

===Chinese===
菲利普爵士让这位侦探一直觉得兴味无穷，他的心思想必也露过一两回。因为这位精明过人的外交大臣，截住警官一瞥好奇的目光，厉声道：「你纳闷，我明知危险，为什么还要把法案推下去？好吧，说来你准会吃惊：我并*不*知道这危险，连想象都想象不出！我这一生从未体味过肉体的疼痛，别看我心脏衰弱，连一回酸痛都不曾有过。死是什么，会带来怎样的剧痛或安宁，我毫无概念。我与爱比克泰德（Epictetus）所见相同：怕死，无异于僭越地自以为洞晓身后事；我们没有理由相信那边比眼下这一边更糟。我不怕死——我怕的是垂死。」

「正是，先生。」这位深表同情却全然听不懂的侦探含糊应道——精微的区别，非他所长。

===Original===
“But,” resumed the minister﻿—he was sitting in his study in Portland Place﻿—“if I cannot imagine the exact process of dissolution, I can imagine, and have experienced, the result of breaking faith with the chancellories, and I have certainly no intention of laying up a store of future embarrassments for fear of something that may after all be comparatively trifling.”

Which piece of reasoning will be sufficient to indicate what the Opposition of the hour was pleased to term “the tortuous mind of the right honourable gentleman.”

And Inspector Falmouth, listening with every indication of attention, yawned inwardly and wondered who Epictetus was.

===Chinese===
「不过，」大臣续道——他正坐在波特兰街自家的书房里——「死亡的确切过程我想象不出，可对外交使团失信的下场，我想象得出，也亲历过；我断然没有意思，为了畏惧一件到头来未必不是小事的事，给自己囤上一堆日后的窘迫。」

这段道理，足可说明时下的反对党乐于称道的「那位尊敬大人曲折的心思」是何光景。

而法尔茅斯警司呢，一副洗耳恭听的架势，肚里打着呵欠，心里嘀咕爱比克泰德究竟是何方神圣。

===Original===
“I have taken all possible precautions, sir,” said the detective in the pause that followed the recital of this creed. “I hope you won’t mind for a week or two being followed about by some of my men. I want you to allow two or three officers to remain in the house whilst you are here, and of course there will be quite a number on duty at the Foreign Office.”

Sir Philip expressed his approval, and later, when he and the detective drove down to the House in a closed brougham, he understood why cyclists rode before and on either side of the carriage, and why two cabs followed the brougham into Palace Yard.

At Notice time, with a house sparsely filled, Sir Philip rose in his place and gave notice that he would move the second reading of the Aliens Extradition (Political Offences) Bill, on Tuesday week, or, to be exact, in ten days.

===Chinese===
「该有的防范，我们都布置周全了，先生。」这一篇信条念完后的间隙里，侦探说道。「往后一两个星期，我派几个人跟着您四处走动，望您不要介意。还想请您准许两三名警官留守宅中——只要您在这里；外交部那边，自然也有一班人当值。」

菲利普爵士表示首肯。稍后，他和侦探乘一辆密闭的四轮马车驶往下议院，他这才明白：为什么自行车骑手在马车前头和两侧护行，为什么两辆出租马车一路跟进宫廷大院。

到了质询时刻，议院里人影稀疏，菲利普爵士起立发言，预告他将于下周星期二——确切说，十日之后——动议《外侨引渡（政治罪）法案》的二读。

---
===Original===
That evening Manfred met Gonsalez in North Tower Gardens and remarked on the fairy-like splendour of the Crystal Palace grounds by night.

A Guards’ band was playing the overture to *Tannhäuser*, and the men talked music.

===Chinese===
当晚，曼弗雷德与冈萨雷斯在北塔花园（North Tower Gardens）碰头，闲谈起水晶宫（Crystal Palace）园圃夜色里仙境也似的辉煌。

一支禁卫军乐队正奏着《唐豪瑟》（*Tannhäuser*）序曲，两人谈起了音乐。

===Original===
Then﻿—

“What of Thery?” asked Manfred.

“Poiccart has him today; he is showing him the sights.” They both laughed.

“And you?” asked Gonsalez.

“I have had an interesting day; I met that delightfully naive detective in Green Park, who asked me what I thought of ourselves!”

===Chinese===
接着——

「特里怎么样？」曼弗雷德问。

「今天归波伊卡特带着，领他观光呢。」两人都笑了。

「你呢？」冈萨雷斯问。

「我过了有味的一天；在绿园（Green Park）碰上那位天真得可爱的侦探，他问我怎么看『我们自己』！」

===Original===
Gonsalez commented on the movement in G minor, and Manfred nodded his head, keeping time with the music.

“Are we prepared?” asked Leon quietly.

Manfred still nodded and softly whistled the number. He stopped with the final crash of the band, and joined in the applause that greeted the musicians.

“I have taken a place,” he said, clapping his hands. “We had better come together.”

===Chinese===
冈萨雷斯品评起那支 G 小调乐章，曼弗雷德颔首，随着音乐打拍子。

「都备妥了？」莱昂轻声问。

曼弗雷德照旧点着头，轻轻用口哨吹那支曲子。乐队末一记铿锵收煞，他也停了，随着迎向乐师的掌声一齐鼓掌。

「我占好位置了。」他拍着手说。「咱们最好一道来。」

===Original===
“Is everything there?”

Manfred looked at his companion with a twinkle in his eye.

“Almost everything.”

The band broke into the national anthem, and the two men rose and uncovered.

===Chinese===
「家伙什都齐了？」

曼弗雷德瞧着同伴，眼里闪着一点顽皮的光。

「差不多都齐了。」

乐队奏起国歌，两人起身，脱帽。

===Original===
The throng about the bandstand melted away in the gloom, and Manfred and his companion turned to go.

Thousands of fairy lamps gleamed in the grounds, and there was a strong smell of gas in the air.

“Not that way this time?” questioned, rather than asserted, Gonsalez.

“Most certainly not that way,” replied Manfred decidedly.

===Chinese===
音乐台四周的人潮在暮色里散尽，曼弗雷德与同伴转身欲行。

园中千万盏彩灯荧荧发亮，空气里煤气味很冲。

「这回不走那条道？」冈萨雷斯半问半说道。

「千真万确，不走那条道。」曼弗雷德斩钉截铁地答。

## V Preparations / 五 准备
===Original===
When an advertisement appeared in the *Newspaper Proprietor* announcing that there was﻿—

===Chinese===
当《报业主》（*Newspaper Proprietor*）上登出一则广告，说有——

===Original===
> “**For Sale:** An old-established zinco-engraver’s business with a splendid new plant and a stock of chemicals,”

===Chinese===
> 「**出售**：老字号锌版制版所一间，配有簇新成套设备，附存化学品一批。」

===Original===
everybody in the printing world said “That’s Etheringtons’.”

To the uninitiated a photo-engraver’s is a place of buzzing saws, and lead shavings, and noisy lathes, and big bright arc lamps. To the initiated a photo-engraver’s is a place where works of art are reproduced by photography on zinc plates, and consequently used for printing purposes. To the very knowing people of the printing world, Etheringtons’ was the worst of its kind, producing the least presentable of pictures at a price slightly above the average. Etheringtons’ had been in the market (by order of the trustees) for three months, but partly owing to its remoteness from Fleet Street (it was in Carnaby Street), and partly to the dilapidated condition of the machinery (which shows that even an official receiver has no moral sense when he starts advertising), there had been no bids.

===Chinese===
印刷圈里人人都会说：「这是埃瑟林顿家（Etheringtons）的铺子。」

在外行人眼里，照相制版所是个锯子嗡嗡、铅屑纷纷、车床吵闹、弧光灯雪亮的地方。在内行人眼里，照相制版所是把艺术品用摄影法翻到锌版上、以供印刷的地方。而在印刷圈里最老于世故的人眼里，埃瑟林顿家这间是此行里最次的：出品的图版最难登大雅之堂，要价却比行市略高。埃瑟林顿家挂牌求售（奉受托人之命）已三个月，一来因为地方离舰队街远（它在卡纳比街），二来因为机器破败（这说明官方接管人一登起广告来，连道德感也没有了），一直无人问津。

===Original===
Manfred, who interviewed the trustee in Carey Street, learnt that the business could be either leased or purchased; that immediate possession in either circumstance was to be had; that there were premises at the top of the house which had served as a dwelling-place to generations of caretakers, and that a banker’s reference was all that was necessary in the way of guarantee.

“Rather a crank,” said the trustee at a meeting of creditors; “thinks that he is going to make a fortune turning out photogravures of Murillo at a price within reach of the inartistic. He tells me that he is forming a small company to carry on the business, and that so soon as it is formed he will buy the plant outright.”

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德在凯里街约见了受托人，得知这买卖租买两可；无论租买，皆可即时交割；楼顶有房间，历代看守人都住在那里；至于担保，只需一纸银行的资信证明。

「有点怪僻，」受托人在债权人会议上说，「满以为把穆里略的影印版画卖到不懂艺术的人也买得起的价格，就能发大财。他告诉我，他正在筹一家小公司来接手这买卖，公司一成立，就把整套设备一次性买断。」

===Original===
And sure enough that very day Thomas Brown, merchant; Arthur W. Knight, gentleman; James Selkirk, artist; Andrew Cohen, financial agent; and James Leech, artist, wrote to the Registrar of Joint Stock Companies, asking to be formed into a company, limited by shares, with the object of carrying on business as photo-engravers, with which object they had severally subscribed for the shares set against their names.

(In parenthesis, Manfred was a great artist.)

===Chinese===
果然就在当天，商人托马斯·布朗（Thomas Brown）、绅士阿瑟·W.·奈特（Arthur W. Knight）、艺术家詹姆斯·塞尔扣克（James Selkirk）、财务代理人安德鲁·科恩（Andrew Cohen）、艺术家詹姆斯·利奇（James Leech），联名致函股份公司注册官，申请组建一家股份有限公司，经营照相制版业，并已各自按名下认足股份。

（附带一句：曼弗雷德是位了不起的艺术家。）

===Original===
And five days before the second reading of the Aliens Extradition Act, the company had entered into occupation of their new premises in preparation to starting business.

“Years ago, when I first came to London,” said Manfred, “I learned the easiest way to conceal one’s identity was to disguise oneself as a public company. There’s a wealth of respectability behind the word ‘limited,’ and the pomp and circumstance of a company directorship diverts suspicion, even as it attracts attention.”

===Chinese===
《外侨引渡法案》二读前五天，这家公司已进驻新址，着手筹备开业。

「多年前我初到伦敦时，」曼弗雷德说，「就悟出遮掩身份最省事的法子，莫过于把自己扮成一家公众公司。『有限』二字背后有的是体面，公司董事的头衔与排场既能招人注目，也就引开了猜疑。」

===Original===
Gonsalez printed a neat notice to the effect that the Fine Arts Reproduction Syndicate would commence business on October 1, and a further neat label that “no hands were wanted,” and a further terse announcement that travellers and others could only be seen by appointment, and that all letters must be addressed to the manager.

It was a plain-fronted shop, with a deep basement crowded with the dilapidated plant left by the liquidated engraver. The ground floor had been used as offices, and neglected furniture and grimy files predominated. There were pigeonholes filled with old plates, pigeonholes filled with dusty invoices, pigeonholes in which all the debris that is accumulated in an office by a clerk with salary in arrear was deposited.

===Chinese===
冈萨雷斯印了一张雅致的启事，宣布美术复制辛迪加（Fine Arts Reproduction Syndicate）将于 10 月 1 日开业；又印了一张同样雅致的招贴：「概不招聘」；再印一则简短通告：行商及各类来访，非预约不获接见；一切函件请寄经理收启。

铺面门脸素净，深深的地下室里塞满了破产制版商留下的破旧机器。底层原作办公之用，无人打理的家具和油腻的档案柜是主角。格架上有塞满旧印版的，有塞满积尘发票的，还有一格一格，堆着欠薪职员的办公室里日积月累的一切碎屑。

===Original===
The first floor had been a workshop, the second had been a store, and the third and most interesting floor of all was that on which were the huge cameras and the powerful arc lamps that were so necessary an adjunct to the business. In the rear of the house on this floor were the three small rooms that had served the purpose of the bygone caretaker.

In one of these, two days after the occupation, sat the four men of Cadiz.

Autumn had come early in the year, a cold driving rain was falling outside, and the fire that burnt in the Georgian grate gave the chamber an air of comfort. This room alone had been cleared of litter, the best furniture of the establishment had been introduced, and on the ink-stained writing-table that filled the centre of the apartment stood the remains of a fairly luxurious lunch.

===Chinese===
二楼原是工场，三楼原是货栈，而四楼——最有意思的一层——架着这门营生必不可少的巨型照相机和大功率弧光灯。这一层屋子后部，是三间小屋，从前看守人就在此栖身。

进驻两天后，加的斯来的那四个人便坐进了其中一间。

这一年秋天来得早，外头斜飘着冷雨，乔治王朝式的炉格里燃着火，给屋子添了几分暖意。唯独这一间清出了杂物，全所最好的家具都搬了进来；屋子正中一张墨渍斑斑的写字台上，摆着一顿颇为丰盛的午餐的残局。

===Original===
Gonsalez was reading a small red book, and it may be remarked that he wore gold-rimmed spectacles; Poiccart was sketching at a corner of the table, and Manfred was smoking a long thin cigar and studying a manufacturing chemists’ price list. Thery (or, as some prefer to call him, Saimont), alone did nothing, sitting a brooding heap before the fire, twiddling his fingers, and staring absently at the leaping little flames in the grate.

Conversation was carried on spasmodically, as between men whose minds were occupied by different thoughts. Thery concentrated the attentions of the three by speaking to the point. Turning from his study of the fire with a sudden impulse he asked:

===Chinese===
冈萨雷斯在读一本小小的红皮书——顺带一提，他戴了副金丝边眼镜；波伊卡特在桌角画速写；曼弗雷德抽着一支细长的雪茄，研究着一份药剂厂商的价目表。唯有特里（或者随各位喜好称他赛蒙特）无所事事，闷坐火前成一堆，绞着手指，怔怔望着炉格里跳动的小火苗。

谈话有一搭没一搭，像一群各怀心事的人。特里一开口就切中要害，把三人的注意力都拢了过来。他猛地从对炉火的凝视里转过神来，问：

===Original===
“How much longer am I to be kept here?”

Poiccart looked up from his drawing and remarked:

“That is the third time he has asked today.”

“Speak Spanish!” cried Thery passionately. “I am tired of this new language. I cannot understand it, any more than I can understand you.”

===Chinese===
「还要把我关在这儿多久？」

波伊卡特从画上抬起眼，说道：

「这是他今天问的第三回了。」

「说西班牙话！」特里激动地嚷。「这种新语言我听腻了。我听不懂它，就像我听不懂你们一样。」

===Original===
“You will wait till it is finished,” said Manfred, in the staccato patois of Andalusia; “we have told you that.”

Thery growled and turned his face to the grate.

“I am tired of this life,” he said sullenly. “I want to walk about without a guard﻿—I want to go back to Jerez, where I was a free man. I am sorry I came away.”

“So am I,” said Manfred quietly; “not very sorry, though﻿—I hope for your sake I shall not be.”

===Chinese===
「你就等到事成那天吧。」曼弗雷德用安达卢西亚方言短促的腔调说，「这话我们早告诉过你。」

特里咆哮了一声，把脸转向炉格。

「这种日子我过腻了。」他闷闷地说。「我想一个人出去走走，不用人跟着——我想回赫雷斯去，在那儿我是个自由人。我后悔出来了。」

「我也后悔。」曼弗雷德平静地说，「不过不怎么后悔——但愿看在你的份上，我不必更后悔。」

===Original===
“Who are you?” burst forth Thery, after a momentary silence. “What are you? Why do you wish to kill? Are you anarchists? What money do you make out of this? I want to know.”

Neither Poiccart nor Gonsalez nor Manfred showed any resentment at the peremptory demand of their recruit. Gonsalez’s clean-shaven, sharp-pointed face twitched with pleasurable excitement, and his cold blue eyes narrowed.

“Perfect! perfect!” he murmured, watching the other man’s face: “pointed nose, small forehead and﻿—*articulorum se ipsos torquentium sonus; gemitus, mugitusque parum explanatis*﻿—”

===Chinese===
沉默了片刻，特里发作了。「你们是什么人？干什么的？你们凭什么要杀人？你们是无政府党吗？你们从这桩事里挣多少钱？我要知道。」

对这个新伙计不由分说的逼问，波伊卡特、冈萨雷斯和曼弗雷德谁也没有动气。冈萨雷斯那张刮得干干净净的尖脸因快意的兴奋而微微抽动，冰冷的蓝眼睛眯了起来。

「妙极！妙极！」他端详着对方的脸喃喃道，「尖鼻梁，窄额头，还有——*articulorum se ipsos torquentium sonus; gemitus, mugitusque parum explanatis*（关节自行扭绞之声；尚未成句的呻吟与哞叫）——」

===Original===
The physiognomist might have continued Seneca’s picture of the Angry Man, but Thery sprang to his feet and glowered at the three.

“Who are you?” he asked slowly. “How do I know that you are not to get money for this? I want to know why you keep me a prisoner, why you will not let me see the newspapers, why you never allow me to walk alone in the streets, or speak to somebody who knows my language? You are not from Spain, nor you, nor you﻿—your Spanish is, yes﻿—but you are not of the country I know. You want me to kill﻿—but you will not say how﻿—”

===Chinese===
这位相士本可以接着把塞内加（Seneca）那幅《怒人图》描完，特里却一跃而起，怒视三人。

「你们是什么人？」他缓缓地问。「我怎么知道你们不是为钱干这个？我要知道，你们为什么把我当囚徒关着，为什么不许我看报，为什么从不让我一个人上街，也不许我跟一个懂我话的人说话？你们不是西班牙人，你也不是，你也不是——你们的西班牙话倒是地道——可你们不是我认识的那个国家的人。你们要我去杀——却死不肯说怎么杀——」

===Original===
Manfred rose and laid his hand on the other’s shoulder.

“Señor,” he said﻿—and there was nothing but kindness in his eyes﻿—“restrain your impatience, I beg of you. I again assure you that we do not kill for gain. These two gentlemen whom you see have each fortunes exceeding six million pesetas, and I am even richer; we kill and we will kill because we are each sufferers through acts of injustice, for which the law gave us no remedy. If﻿—if﻿—” he hesitated, still keeping his grey eyes fixed unflinchingly on the Spaniard. Then he resumed gently: “If we kill you it will be the first act of the kind﻿—”

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德起身，把手搭在对方肩上。

「先生（señor），」他说——眼里别无他物，只有善意，「务请按捺性子。我再向你保证一遍：我们杀人不为图利。你眼前这两位先生，家产各逾六百万比塞塔，我比他们更有钱；我们杀过人，往后还要杀，因为我们每个人都受过不义的侵害，而法律不曾给我们救济。倘若——倘若——」他迟疑了，一双灰眼睛毫不闪避地盯着这个西班牙人。随即又温和地续道：「倘若我们杀了你，那将是开天辟地头一遭——」

===Original===
Thery was on his feet, white and snarling, with his back to the wall; a wolf at bay, looking from one to the other with fierce suspicion.

“Me﻿—me!” he breathed, “kill me?”

Neither of the three men moved save Manfred, who dropped his outstretched hand to his side.

“Yes, you.” He nodded as he spoke. “It would be new work for us, for we have never slain except for justice﻿—and to kill you would be an unjust thing.”

===Chinese===
特里早已站起，脸色煞白，龇着牙，背抵住墙：一匹被逼入绝境的狼，凶狠多疑地轮流瞪着三人。

「我——我！」他喘着气说，「杀我？」

三人之中唯有曼弗雷德动了动，把伸出去的手垂回身侧。

「对，就是你。」他一面说一面点头。「那对我们是桩新营生，因为我们从没有不为正义杀过人——而杀你，是一桩不义的事。」

===Original===
Poiccart looked at Thery pityingly.

“That is why we chose you,” said Poiccart, “because there was always a fear of betrayal, and we thought﻿—it had better be you.”

“Understand,” resumed Manfred calmly, “that not a hair of your head will be harmed if you are faithful﻿—that you will receive a reward that will enable you to live﻿—remember the girl at Jerez.”

Thery sat down again with a shrug of indifference, but his hands were trembling as he struck a match to light his cigarette.

“We will give you more freedom﻿—you shall go out every day. In a few days we shall all return to Spain. They called you the silent man in the prison at Granada﻿—we shall believe that you will remain so.”

===Chinese===
波伊卡特怜悯地望着特里。

「所以才挑中了你，」波伊卡特说，「因为背叛的隐患始终在，我们想——不如是你。」

「你要明白，」曼弗雷德平静地续道，「你若忠心，头上断不会伤一根毫发——你会得到一笔足够安身立命的酬报——别忘了赫雷斯的那位姑娘。」

特里满不在乎地耸耸肩，重新坐下，可他划火柴点烟时，双手直发抖。

「我们会放你更多自由——往后每天都可以出门。过几天我们就都回西班牙去。格拉纳达（Granada）监狱里人家管你叫哑巴——我们就信你一直哑下去。」

===Original===
After this the conversation became Greek to the Spaniard, for the men spoke in English.

“He gives very little trouble,” said Gonsalez; “now that we have dressed him like an Englishman, he does not attract attention. He doesn’t like shaving every day; but it is necessary, and luckily he is fair. I do not allow him to speak in the street, and this tries his temper somewhat.”

===Chinese===
这以后，谈话对这西班牙人便成了天书，因为几人说起英语来。

「他不大惹麻烦，」冈萨雷斯说，「如今把他打扮成英国人模样，惹不来注意。他不喜欢天天刮脸；可这是少不了的，幸亏他胡子色浅。我不许他在街上开口，这叫他有些憋气。」

===Original===
Manfred turned the talk into a more serious channel.

“I shall send two more warnings, and one of those must be delivered in his very stronghold. He is a brave man.”

“What of Garcia?” asked Poiccart.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德把话头引向更要紧的方向。

「我还要送两回警告，其中一封，务必递到他自家的堡垒里去。他是个有胆量的人。」

「加西亚那边呢？」波伊卡特问。

===Original===
Manfred laughed.

“I saw him on Sunday night﻿—a fine old man, fiery and oratorical. I sat at the back of a little hall whilst he pleaded eloquently in French for the rights of man. He was a Jean-Jacques Rousseau, a Mirabeau, a broad-viewed Bright, and the audience was mostly composed of Cockney youths, who had come that they might boast they had stood in the temple of Anarchism.”

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德笑了。

「星期天晚上我见着他了——好一位老先生，火气旺，天生演说家。我坐在小会堂后排，听他用法语滔滔地为人的权利辩护。他是一位让-雅克·卢梭，一位米拉波，一位眼界开阔的布赖特；而听众大半是伦敦城里一口土腔的毛头小子，来只为日后夸口，说自己在无政府主义的殿堂里站过一遭。」

===Original===
Poiccart tapped the table impatiently.

“Why is it, George, that an element of bathos comes into all these things?”

Manfred laughed.

“You remember Anderson? When we had gagged him and bound him to the chair, and had told him why he had to die﻿—when there were only the pleading eyes of the condemned, and the half-dark room with a flickering lamp, and you and Leon and poor Clarice masked and silent, and I had just sentenced him to death﻿—you remember how there crept into the room the scent of frying onions from the kitchen below?”

===Chinese===
波伊卡特不耐烦地敲了敲桌子。

「乔治，为什么这些事情里，总要掺进一份煞风景的俗气？」

曼弗雷德笑了。

「你记得安德森吧？我们堵上他的嘴，把他绑在椅子上，告诉他为什么必须死——那时屋里只有死囚一双哀告的眼睛，半明半暗的房间，一盏摇曳的灯，你和莱昂、还有可怜的克拉丽丝（Clarice）蒙着面，一声不响，我则刚刚宣判了他的死刑——你还记得，楼下厨房里煎洋葱的香气是怎么悄悄爬进屋里来的？」

===Original===
“I too remember,” said Leon, “the case of the regicide.”

Poiccart made a motion of agreement.

“You mean the corsets,” he said, and the two nodded and laughed.

“There will always be bathos,” said Manfred; “poor Garcia with a nation’s destinies in his hand, an amusement for shopgirls﻿—tragedy and the scent of onions﻿—a rapier thrust and the whalebone of corsets﻿—it is inseparable.”

===Chinese===
「我也记得，」莱昂说，「弑君案那一桩。」

波伊卡特点头认可。

「你说的是紧身褡那档子事。」他说，两人相视点头而笑。

「煞风景的事永远免不了，」曼弗雷德说，「可怜的加西亚，一国的命运在他掌中，却成了女店员们的消遣——悲剧配煎洋葱香——细剑一刺，配紧身褡的鲸骨——这原是分不开的。」

===Original===
