The detective finished reading. “Watch” was not the last word in the original message, as he knew. There had been an ominous postscript, “Afraid they will arrive too late.”

“Then you think?” asked the statesman.

“That your danger lies in doing one of the things that Pinkerton warns us against,” replied the detective. “There is no fear that the American police are talking idly. They have based their warning on some sure knowledge, and that is why I regard their cable as important.”

===Chinese===
侦探念完了。「监视」并不是原电文的最后一个词，这他心里有数。原电还有一句不祥的附言：「恐彼等到时已迟。」

「那么你认为？」政治家问。

「您的问题，就出在平克顿告诫的那几件事里，迟早要犯一件。」侦探答道。「不必担心美国警察在说空话。他们的警告有确凿的底子，所以我才把这份电报看得重。」

===Original===
There was a sharp rap on the panel of the door, and without waiting for invitation the private secretary walked into the room, excitedly waving a newspaper.

“Look at this!” he cried. “Read this! The Four have admitted their failure.”

“What!” shouted the detective, reaching for the journal.

===Chinese===
门板上响起急促的敲击，私人秘书不等召唤便闯进屋来，激动地挥着一份报纸。

「看这个！」他喊道。「念这个！四义士承认失败了。」

「什么！」侦探喊道，伸手去抢报纸。

===Original===
“What does this mean?” asked Sir Philip, sharply.

“Only this, sir: these beggars, it appears, have actually written an article on their ‘mission.’ ”

“In what newspaper?”

“The *Megaphone*. It seems when they recaptured Thery the editor asked the masked man to write him an article about himself, and they’ve done it; and it’s here, and they’ve admitted defeat, and﻿—and﻿—”

===Chinese===
「这是什么意思？」菲利普爵士厉声问。

「就是这么回事，先生：这帮家伙，看样子，当真就他们的『使命』写了一篇文章。」

「登在哪家报上？」

「《传声筒报》。好像是他们夺回特里那回，主编请那位蒙面人写一篇自述，他们还真写了；就登在这儿，还承认了败局，还有——还有——」

===Original===
The detective had seized the paper and broke in upon the incoherent secretary’s speech.

“ ‘The Creed of the Four Just Men,’ ” he read. “Where is their confession of failure?”

“Halfway down the column﻿—I have marked the passage﻿—here,” and the young man pointed with a trembling finger to a paragraph.

===Chinese===
侦探已一把抓过报纸，打断了语无伦次的秘书。

「『四义士的信条』。」他念道。「他们认输的话在哪儿？」

「栏的半腰——我把那段圈出来了——这儿。」年轻人用发抖的手指点着一段。

===Original===
> “ ‘We leave nothing to chance,’ ” read the detective; “ ‘if the slightest hitch occurs, if the least detail of our plan miscarries, we acknowledge defeat. So assured are we that our presence on earth is necessary for the carrying out of a great plan, so certain are we that we are the indispensable instruments of a Divine Providence, that we dare not, for the sake of our very cause, accept unnecessary risks. It is essential, therefore, that the various preliminaries to every execution should be carried out to the full. As an example, it will be necessary for us to deliver our final warning to Sir Philip Ramon; and to add point to this warning, it is, by our code, essential that that should be handed to the minister by one of us in person. All arrangements have been made to carry this portion of our programme into effect. But such are the extraordinary exigencies of our system that unless this warning can be handed to Sir Philip in accordance with our promise, and before eight o’clock this evening, our arrangements fall to the ground, and the execution we have planned must be foregone.’ ”

===Chinese===
> 「『我们不将任何事情付与偶然，』」侦探念道；「『哪怕有最细微的差池，哪怕我们计划中最微末的一环失手，我们即自认失败。我们如此确信，吾人存世乃为成就一项大计；如此笃定，吾人乃神圣天意不可或缺之器具，以至于为着我们事业本身的缘故，我们不敢领受任何不必要的风险。因此，每回行刑的种种预备，必须一一做满。举个例子：我们必须向菲利普·拉蒙爵士送出最后的警告；而为给这警告添上分量，按我们的法典，它必须由我们中的一人亲手交到大臣手上。为落实计划的这一部分，一切安排业已完成。只是我们这套法度的要求如此苛刻：倘若这警告不能如约、不能在今晚八点之前，交到菲利普爵士手中，我们的全部部署便尽数落空，筹划中的行刑也只能作罢。』」

===Original===
The detective stopped reading, with disappointment visible on every line of his face.

“I thought, sir, by the way you were carrying on that you had discovered something new. I’ve read all this; a copy of the article was sent to the Yard as soon as it was received.”

The secretary thumped the desk impatiently.

===Chinese===
侦探住了口，脸上每一道纹路都写着失望。

「先生，瞧您这大惊小怪的样子，我还当您发现了什么新大陆。这些我都读过了；文章一见报，抄件就送到了场里。」

秘书不耐烦地捶着桌子。

===Original===
“But don’t you see!” he cried. “Don’t you understand, that there is no longer any need to guard Sir Philip, that there is no reason to use him as a bait, or, in fact, to do anything if we are to believe these men﻿—look at the time﻿—”

The detective’s hand flew to his pocket; he drew out his watch, looked at the dial and whistled.

“Half-past eight, by God,” he muttered in astonishment, and the three stood in surprised silence.

===Chinese===
「可你们难道看不见！」他喊道。「难道不明白，再没有必要保护菲利普爵士了，没有理由再拿他当饵，说实话，什么都不必做了——只要信得过这几位——看看几点了——」

侦探的手飞进口袋；掏出表，看了一眼表盘，吹了声口哨。

「八点半了，老天。」他愕然喃喃道，三人立在惊愕的沉默里。

===Original===
Sir Philip broke the silence.

“Is it a ruse to take us off our guard?” he said hoarsely.

“I don’t think so,” replied the detective slowly, “I feel sure that it is not; nor shall I relax my watch﻿—but I am a believer in the honesty of these men﻿—I don’t know why I should say this, for I have been dealing with criminals for the past twenty-five years, and never once have I put an ounce of faith in the word of the best of ’em, but somehow I can’t disbelieve these men. If they have failed to deliver their message they will not trouble us again.”

===Chinese===
菲利普爵士打破了沉默。

「这是不是麻痹我们的诡计？」他哑着嗓子说。

「我看不是。」侦探缓缓答道，「我确信不是；我也不会松了看守——可我信这几个人的诚信——我不知道自己为什么说这个，因为这二十五年我打交道的都是罪犯，他们里头顶好的人的话，我也从没信过一钱重；可这几个人，我偏偏没法不信。他们若没能把信送到，就不会再来搅扰我们了。」

===Original===
Ramon paced his room with quick, nervous steps.

“I wish I could believe that,” he muttered; “I wish I had your faith.”

A tap on the door panel.

“An urgent telegram for Sir Philip,” said a grey-haired attendant.

===Chinese===
拉蒙在房里疾步来回，焦躁不安。

「但愿我信得过这话。」他喃喃道；「但愿我有你这份信心。」

门板上轻轻一叩。

「菲利普爵士的加急电报。」头发花白的侍者说。

===Original===
The minister stretched out his hand, but the detective was before him.

“Remember Pinkerton’s wire, sir,” he said, and ripped open the brown envelope.

> “Just received a telegram handed in at Charing Cross 7:52 begins: We have delivered our last message to the Foreign Secretary, signed Four. Ends. Is this true? Editor *Megaphone*.”

===Chinese===
大臣伸手去接，侦探抢在了前头。

「想想平克顿那封电报，先生。」他说着，撕开了棕色信封。

> 「刚刚收到一电，系 7 时 52 分于查令十字投发，文曰：我等已将最后一信送呈外交大臣，署名四义士，文止。此说属实否？《传声筒报》主编。」

===Original===
“What does this mean?” asked Falmouth in bewilderment when he had finished reading.

“It means, my dear Mr. Falmouth,” replied Sir Philip testily, “that your noble Four are liars and braggarts as well as murderers; and it means at the same time, I hope, an end to your ridiculous faith in their honesty!”

The detective made no answer, but his face was clouded and he bit his lips in perplexity.

===Chinese===
「这是什么意思？」法尔茅斯念完，一头雾水地问。

「这意味着，亲爱的法尔茅斯先生，」菲利普爵士没好气地答道，「你那些高贵的义士，除了杀人，还兼做撒谎的家伙、吹牛的大王；同时我还指望，它也意味着你对他们诚信那份可笑的信仰，到此为止！」

侦探不答，可他脸上阴云密布，困惑地咬着嘴唇。

===Original===
“Nobody came after I left,” he asked.

“Nobody.”

“You have seen no person besides your secretary and myself?”

“Absolutely nobody has spoken to me, or approached within a dozen yards of me,” Ramon answered, shortly.

===Chinese===
「我走之后没来过人吧。」他问。

「没有。」

「除了秘书和我，你没见过任何人？」

「绝对没有人跟我说过话，也没有人靠近过我十二码之内。」拉蒙简短地答道。

===Original===
Falmouth shook his head despairingly.

“Well﻿—I﻿—where are we?” he asked, speaking more to himself than to anybody in the room, and moved towards the door.

Then it was that Sir Philip remembered the package left in his charge.

“You had better take your precious documents,” he said, opening his drawer and throwing the package left in his charge on to the table.

===Chinese===
法尔茅斯绝望地摇了摇头。

「这么说——我——咱们这是在哪儿？」他问道，与其说是问屋里的人，不如说是问自己，一面朝门口走去。

这时菲利普爵士才想起托他保管的那件东西。

「你那些宝贝文件最好收回去。」他说着，拉开抽屉，把托他保管的那包东西丢到桌上。

===Original===
The detective looked puzzled.

“What is this?” he asked, picking up the envelope.

“I’m afraid the shock of finding yourself deceived in your estimate of my persecutors has dazed you,” said Sir Philip, and added pointedly, “I must ask the commissioner to send an officer who has a better appreciation of the criminal mind, and a less childlike faith in the honour of murderers.”

===Chinese===
侦探一脸困惑。

「这是什么？」他拾起信封问。

「恐怕是发现自己在对我迫害者的评估上栽了跟头，把你吓糊涂了。」菲利普爵士说，又意味深长地补上一句：「我得请总监另派一位警官来，一位对犯罪心理更有见识、对杀人犯的荣誉没那么孩子气的信仰的。」

===Original===
“As to that, sir,” said Falmouth, unmoved by the outburst, “you must do as you think best. I have discharged my duty to my own satisfaction; and I have no more critical taskmaster than myself. But what I am more anxious to hear is exactly what you mean by saying that I handed any papers into your care.”

The foreign secretary glared across the table at the imperturbable police officer.

“I am referring, sir,” he said harshly, “to the packet which you returned to leave in my charge.”

===Chinese===
「那个嘛，先生，」法尔茅斯对这场发作无动于衷，「您看着办就是。我的职责已尽，自己问心无愧；再挑剔的监工，也挑剔不过我自己。我倒更想听听，您说我把什么文件托付给了您，这话究竟是什么意思。」

外交大臣隔着桌子，死死瞪着这位不动声色的警官。

「我指的是，先生，」他声色俱厉，「你回来留给我保管的那一包。」

===Original===
The detective stared.

“I﻿—did﻿—not﻿—return,” he said in a strained voice. “I have left no papers in your hands.” He picked up the package from the table, tore it open and disclosed yet another envelope. As he caught sight of the grey-green cover he gave a sharp cry.

“This is the message of the Four,” said Falmouth.

===Chinese===
侦探怔住了。

「我——没——有——回来过。」他一字一顿，声音发紧。「我没有留任何文件在您手里。」他从桌上拿起那包东西，撕开，露出里头还有一只信封。一见那灰绿色的封皮，他失声叫了出来。

「这就是四义士的信。」法尔茅斯说。

===Original===
The foreign secretary staggered back a pace, white to the lips.

“And the man who delivered it?” he gasped.

“Was one of the Four Just Men,” said the detective grimly. “They have kept their promise.”

===Chinese===
外交大臣踉跄着退了一步，嘴唇全无血色。

「送信的人呢？」他喘着气问。

「是四义士之一。」侦探冷冷地说。「他们践了约。」

===Original===
He took a quick step to the door, passed through into the anteroom and beckoned the plainclothes officer who stood on guard at the outer door.

“Do you remember my going out?” he asked.

“Yes, sir﻿—both times.”

“Both times, eh!” said Falmouth bitterly. “And how did I look the second time?”

===Chinese===
他疾步到门口，穿过前厅，招手唤出守在外门的便衣警官。

「记得我出去的事吗？」他问。

「记得，先生——两回都记得。」

「两回，嗯！」法尔茅斯苦涩地说。「第二回我看上去怎么样？」

===Original===
His subordinate was bewildered at the form the question took.

“As usual, sir,” he stammered.

“How was I dressed?”

The constable considered.

===Chinese===
下属被这问法问懵了。

「跟平常一样，先生。」他结结巴巴地说。

「我穿的什么？」

警员想了想。

===Original===
“In your long dust coat.”

“I wore my goggles, I suppose?”

“Yes, sir.”

“I thought so,” muttered Falmouth savagely, and raced down the broad marble stairs that led to the entrance-hall. There were four men on duty, who saluted him as he approached.

===Chinese===
「您那件长风衣。」

「还戴着风镜，对吧？」

「是，先生。」

「我就知道。」法尔茅斯狠狠地咕哝，一阵风冲下通往门厅的宽大大理石楼梯。四名当值的人见他过来，齐齐敬礼。

===Original===
“Do you remember my going out?” he asked of the sergeant in charge.

“Yes, sir﻿—both times,” the officer replied.

“Damn your ‘both times’!” snapped Falmouth. “How long had I been gone the first time before I returned?”

“Five minutes, sir,” was the astonished officer’s reply.

===Chinese===
「记得我出去的事吗？」他问带队的巡佐。

「记得，先生——两回都记得。」警官答道。

「去你的『两回都记得』！」法尔茅斯厉声道。「头一回我走了多久才回来的？」

「五分钟，先生。」愕然的警官答道。

===Original===
“They just gave themselves time to do it,” muttered Falmouth, and then aloud﻿—“Did I return in my car?”

“Yes, sir.”

“Ah!” hope sprang into the detective’s breast. “Did you notice the number?” he asked, almost fearful to hear the reply.

“Yes!”

===Chinese===
「他们给自己留的工夫，刚好够办这件事。」法尔茅斯咕哝道，随即扬声——「我回来时坐的是我的车吗？」

「是，先生。」

「啊！」希望涌上侦探心头。「车牌号留意了吗？」他问，几乎怕听到回答。

「留意了！」

===Original===
The detective could have hugged the stolid officer.

“Good﻿—what was it?”

“A 17164.”

The detective made a rapid note of the number.

===Chinese===
侦探简直想抱住这位木讷的警官亲一口。

「好——是多少？」

「A 17164。」

侦探飞快记下车号。

===Original===
“Jackson,” he called, and one of the men in plain clothes stepped forward and saluted.

“Go to the Yard; find out the registered owner of this car. When you have found this go to the owner; ask him to explain the movements; if necessary, take him into custody.”

Falmouth retraced his steps to Sir Philip’s study. He found the statesman still agitatedly walking up and down the room, the secretary nervously drumming his fingers on the table and the letter still unopened.

===Chinese===
「杰克逊。」他唤道，一名便衣跨步上前敬礼。

「去场里；查这辆车的登记车主。查到后就去找车主；让他解释这趟出行的缘由；必要的话，把他拘留。」

法尔茅斯折回菲利普爵士的书房。他看见那位政治家还在屋里激动地走来走去，秘书紧张地用手指敲着桌面，而那封信，原封未动。

===Original===
“As I thought,” explained Falmouth; “the man you saw was one of the Four impersonating me. He chose his time admirably; my own men were deceived. They managed to get a car exactly similar in build and colour to mine, and, watching their opportunity, they drove to Downing Street a few minutes after I had left. There is one last chance of our catching him﻿—luckily the sergeant on duty noticed the number of the car, and we might be able to trace him through that﻿—hullo.” An attendant stood at the door.

Would the superintendent see Detective Jackson?

Falmouth found him waiting in the hall below.

===Chinese===
「不出我所料。」法尔茅斯解释道；「您见到的那个人，是四义士之一假扮的我。时机挑得漂亮极了；连我自己的人都被骗过。他们设法弄到一辆式样颜色同我的车一模一样的汽车，瞅准空当，在我离开几分钟后驶到唐宁街。抓住他还有最后一线机会——幸好当值的巡佐留意了车牌，咱们兴许能顺藤摸瓜——咦。」一名侍者站在门口。

侍者问，警司可愿意见杰克逊侦探？

法尔茅斯在楼下大厅里见到了他。

===Original===
“I beg your pardon, sir,” said Jackson, saluting, “but is there not some mistake in this number?”

“Why?” asked the detective, sharply.

“Because,” said the man, “A 17164 is the number of your own car.”

===Chinese===
「恕我冒昧，先生，」杰克逊敬着礼说，「这个车号，是不是有些差错？」

「怎么？」侦探厉声问。

「因为，」那人说，「A 17164 是您自己那辆车的车号。」

## IX The Pocketbook / 九 记事本
===Original===
The final warning was brief and to the point:

===Chinese===
最后的警告简短而直截了当：

===Original===
> We allow you until tomorrow evening to reconsider your position in the matter of the Aliens Extradition Bill. If by six o’clock no announcement is made in the afternoon newspapers of your withdrawing this measure we shall have no other course to pursue but to fulfil our promise. You will die at eight in the evening. We append for your enlightenment a concise table of the secret police arrangements made for your safety tomorrow. Farewell.

> (Signed) Four Just Men

===Chinese===
> 关于《外侨引渡法案》，限你到明晚重新考虑你的立场。若下午六时各家晚报尚无你撤回此案的公告，我等别无他途，唯有践诺。你将于晚八时死去。兹附上警方为你明日安全所作秘密部署简表一份，供你参详。别矣。

> （签名）四义士

===Original===
Sir Philip read this over without a tremor. He read, too, the slip of paper on which was written, in the strange foreign hand, the details that the police had not dared to put into writing.

“There is a leakage somewhere,” he said, and the two anxious watchers saw that the face of their charge was grey and drawn.

“These details were known only to four,” said the detective quietly, “and I’ll stake my life that it was neither the commissioner nor myself.”

===Chinese===
菲利普爵士把信读完，手没有一丝颤抖。他又读了那页纸——上面以一种陌生的外国笔迹，写着警方不敢落诸文字的种种细节。

「哪里漏了风。」他说；两名忧心忡忡的守护者这才看清，他们保护的对象脸色灰败，面容扭曲。

「这些细节只有四个人知道。」侦探平静地说，「我拿性命担保，总监和我都没漏。」

===Original===
“Nor I!” said the private secretary emphatically.

Sir Philip shrugged his shoulders with a weary laugh.

“What does it matter?﻿—they know,” he exclaimed; “by what uncanny method they have learnt the secret I neither know nor care. The question is, can I be adequately protected tomorrow night at eight o’clock?”

===Chinese===
「也没有我！」私人秘书斩钉截铁地说。

菲利普爵士疲惫地一笑，耸了耸肩。

「有什么要紧？——反正是他们知道了。」他扬声道，「他们用什么邪门法子探到的秘密，我不知道，也懒得知道。问题是：明晚八点，我能不能得到足够的保护？」

===Original===
Falmouth shut his teeth.

“Either you’ll come out of it alive or, by the Lord, they’ll kill two,” he said, and there was a gleam in his eye that spoke for his determination.

===Chinese===
法尔茅斯咬紧了牙关。

「要么您活着出来，要么，老天在上，他们得杀两个。」他说，眼里闪着一点光，替他的决心说话。

---
===Original===
The news that yet another letter had reached the great statesman was on the streets at ten o’clock that night. It circulated through the clubs and theatres, and between the acts grave-faced men stood in the vestibules discussing Ramon’s danger. The House of Commons was seething with excitement. In the hope that the minister would come down, a strong House had gathered, but the members were disappointed, for it was evident soon after the dinner recess that Sir Philip had no intention of showing himself that night.

“Might I ask the right honourable the prime minister whether it is the intention of his Majesty’s Government to proceed with the Aliens Extradition (Political Offence) Bill,” asked the Radical member for West Deptford, “and whether he has not considered, in view of the extraordinary conditions that this bill has called into life, the advisability of postponing the introduction of this measure?”

===Chinese===
当晚十点，那位大政治家又收到一封信的消息已经传遍街头。它在俱乐部和剧院之间流转，幕间休息时，神情凝重的男人们站在门厅里议论拉蒙的凶险。下议院沸腾了。指望大臣驾到，议院聚起了罕见的人数，议员们却落了空——晚宴休会不久，大家便看得明白，菲利普爵士那晚无意露面。

「可否请教尊贵的首相阁下，国王陛下政府是否仍打算推进《外侨引渡（政治罪）法案》，」西德普特福德选区的激进党议员问，「又是否考虑过，鉴于该法案引出的非常局面，将此项措施缓议是否更为妥当？」

===Original===
The question was greeted with a chorus of “hears,” and the prime minister rose slowly and turned an amused glance in the direction of the questioner.

“I know of no circumstance that is likely to prevent my right honourable friend, who is unfortunately not in his place tonight, from moving the second reading of the bill tomorrow,” he said, and sat down.

===Chinese===
问题迎来一片「赞成」的合唱，首相缓缓起立，朝发问者投去一缕觉得有趣的眼光。

「据我所知，没有任何情况能阻止我那位尊贵的朋友——可惜他今晚不在座——明日动议该法案的二读。」他说罢坐下。

===Original===
“What the devil was he grinning at?” grumbled West Deptford to a neighbour.

“He’s deuced uncomfortable, is J. K.,” said the other wisely, “deuced uncomfortable; a man in the cabinet was telling me today that old J. K. has been feeling deuced uncomfortable. ‘You mark my words,’ he said, ‘this Four Just Men business is making the premier deuced uncomfortable,’ ” and the hon. member subsided to allow West Deptford to digest his neighbour’s profundities.

===Chinese===
「他咧着嘴笑个什么劲？」西德普特福德向邻座咕哝。

「J. K. 他呀，别扭得很。」另一位煞有见识地说，「别扭得很；今天内阁里一位还跟我讲，老 J. K. 这阵子别扭得很。『你记住我这话，』他说，『四义士这档子事，把首相弄得益发别扭了。』」这位尊敬的议员说完便偃旗息鼓，好让西德普特福德消化邻座的高论。

===Original===
“I’ve done my best to persuade Ramon to drop the bill,” the premier was saying, “but he is adamant, and the pitiable thing is that he believes in his heart of hearts that these fellows intend keeping faith.”

“It is monstrous,” said the colonial secretary hotly; “it is inconceivable that such a state of affairs can last. Why, it strikes at the root of everything, it unbalances every adjustment of civilization.”

===Chinese===
「我已尽了力劝拉蒙撤案，」首相正说着，「可他心如铁石；可悲的是，他心底里竟真信这几位是打算践约的。」

「真是岂有此理。」殖民地大臣愤然道；「这样的局面竟能延续，不可思议。这直捣一切的根本，把文明的每一处平衡都掀翻了。」

===Original===
“It is a poetical idea,” said the phlegmatic premier, “and the standpoint of the Four is quite a logical one. Think of the enormous power for good or evil often vested in one man: a capitalist controlling the markets of the world, a speculator cornering cotton or wheat whilst mills stand idle and people starve, tyrants and despots with the destinies of nations between their thumb and finger﻿—and then think of the four men, known to none; vague, shadowy figures stalking tragically through the world, condemning and executing the capitalist, the corner-maker, the tyrant﻿—evil forces all, and all beyond reach of the law. We have said of these people, such of us as are touched with mysticism, that God would judge them. Here are men arrogating to themselves the divine right of superior judgment. If we catch them they will end their lives unpicturesquely, in a matter-of-fact, commonplace manner in a little shed in Pentonville Gaol, and the world will never realize how great the artists who perish.”

===Chinese===
「这是个诗意的主意。」不动声色的首相说，「四义士的立足点，也全然合乎逻辑。想想常握在一人手中的那种为善为恶的偌大权柄：操纵世界市场的资本家，一面囤积棉花小麦、一面任纱厂闲置百姓挨饿的投机客，把万国命运捻在拇指与食指之间的暴君独夫——再想想这四个人，无人识得；朦胧、影一般的身形，悲壮地行走人间，审判并处决那资本家、囤积者、暴君——个个是恶势力，个个在法律够不着的地方。我们中间沾点神秘主义的人谈到这类人，总说上帝自会审判。而这里竟有人，把超凡审判的神圣权利攫为己有。我们若抓住他们，他们将毫无画意地了却一生，在彭顿维尔监狱一间小棚屋里，按部就班、平淡无奇地死去；而世界永远无从知晓，陨落的是何等了得的艺术家。」

===Original===
“But Ramon?”

The premier smiled.

“Here, I think, these men have just overreached themselves. Had they been content to slay first and explain their mission afterwards I have little doubt that Ramon would have died. But they have warned and warned and exposed their hand a dozen times over. I know nothing of the arrangements that are being made by the police, but I should imagine that by tomorrow night it will be as difficult to get within a dozen yards of Ramon as it would be for a Siberian prisoner to dine with the Czar.”

===Chinese===
「可拉蒙呢？」

首相微微一笑。

「这一回，我看他们恰恰是弄巧成拙。他们若安于先杀人、再宣讲使命，拉蒙多半早已死了。可他们警告了又警告，把底牌亮了十几遍。警方在作什么部署，我一概不知，但我想，到了明晚，要靠近拉蒙十二码之内，只怕同西伯利亚的囚徒邀沙皇共进晚餐一样难。」

===Original===
“Is there no possibility of Ramon withdrawing the bill?” asked the Colonies.

The premier shook his head.

“Absolutely none,” he said.

===Chinese===
「拉蒙就没有可能撤案吗？」殖民地大臣问。

首相摇头。

「绝无可能。」他说。

===Original===
The rising of a member of the Opposition front bench at that moment to move an amendment to a clause under discussion cut short the conversation.

The House rapidly emptied when it became generally known that Ramon did not intend appearing, and the members gathered in the smoking-room and lobby to speculate upon the matter which was uppermost in their minds.

===Chinese===
恰在此时，反对党前座一位议员起立，就讨论中的某条款提出修正案，把这场谈话截断了。

拉蒙无意露面的消息一传开，议院很快空了下来；议员们聚到吸烟室和走廊里，就那件压在心头的事胡猜乱想。

===Original===
In the vicinity of Palace Yard a great crowd had gathered, as in London crowds will gather, on the off-chance of catching a glimpse of the man whose name was in every mouth. Street vendors sold his portrait, frowsy men purveying the real life and adventures of the Four Just Men did a roaring trade, and itinerant street singers, introducing extemporized verses into their repertoire, declined the courage of that statesman bold, who dared for to resist the threats of coward alien and deadly anarchist.

There was praise in these poor lyrics for Sir Philip, who was trying to prevent the foreigner from taking the bread out of the mouths of honest workingmen.

===Chinese===
宫廷大院四周聚起了大群的人——伦敦的人群向来是说聚就聚——指望侥幸一睹那个名字挂在每个人嘴边的人。街头小贩兜售他的肖像，衣冠褴褛的汉子叫卖《四义士实录奇遇》，生意红火；走街串巷的歌手把即兴编的唱词掺进曲目，把那位大胆政治家的胆量折了一折——他竟敢抵抗懦怯外国佬与夺命无政府党人的威胁。

这些蹩脚唱词里也不乏对菲利普爵士的褒扬——他正努力不让外国佬抢走诚实工人口中的面包。

===Original===
The humour of which appealed greatly to Manfred; he and Poiccart had driven to the Westminster end of the Embankment and, having dismissed their cab, were walking to Whitehall.

“I think the verse about the ‘deadly foreign anarchist’ taking the bread out of the mouth of the homemade variety is distinctly good,” chuckled Manfred.

Both men were in evening dress, and Poiccart wore in his buttonhole the silken button of a Chevalier of the Legion d’Honneur.

===Chinese===
这份幽默正中曼弗雷德的下怀；他和波伊卡特乘车到了河滨大道的威斯敏斯特一端，打发走马车，正步行往白厅（Whitehall）去。

「那句唱『夺命外国无政府党人』抢『土产无政府党人』嘴里面包的词，我觉得尤其高明。」曼弗雷德轻笑着说。

两人都穿着晚礼服，波伊卡特钮孔里佩着一枚荣誉军团骑士的丝质小徽记。

===Original===
Manfred continued:

“I doubt whether London has had such a sensation since﻿—when?”

Poiccart’s grim smile caught the other’s eye and he smiled in sympathy.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德续道：

「伦敦上一次这般轰动，还得追溯到——哪一年来着？」

波伊卡特那抹冷冷的笑意落进对方眼里，他会意地笑了。

===Original===
“Well?”

“I asked the same question of the maître d’hôtel,” he said slowly, like a man loath to share a joke; “*he* compared the agitation to the atrocious East-End murders.”

Manfred stopped dead and looked with horror on his companion.

“Great heavens!” he exclaimed in distress, “it never occurred to me that we should be compared with﻿—him!”

===Chinese===
「嗯？」

「我也拿这个问题问过那位侍者领班。」他慢慢地说，像个舍不得把笑话讲出来的人；「*他*把这场骚动比作了东伦敦那些骇人的连环凶案。」

曼弗雷德猛地站住，骇然望着同伴。

「天哪！」他痛心地喊道，「我竟从没想到，我们会被人比作——他！」

===Original===
They resumed their walk.

“It is part of the eternal bathos,” said Poiccart serenely; “even De Quincey taught the English nothing.”

They were traversing that portion of Whitehall from which Scotland Yard runs.

===Chinese===
两人继续走。

「这原是永恒的煞风景之一。」波伊卡特安之若素，「连德·昆西都没教会英国人什么。」

他们正走过白厅上苏格兰场岔出的那一段。

===Original===
A man slouching along with bent head and his hands thrust deep into the pockets of his tattered coat, gave them a swift sidelong glance, stopped when they had passed, and looked after them. Then he turned, and, quickening his shuffle, followed on their trail. A press of people and a seemingly ceaseless string of traffic at the corner of Cockspur Street brought Manfred and Poiccart to a standstill, waiting for an opportunity to cross the road. They were subjected to a little jostling as the knot of waiting people thickened, but eventually they crossed and walked towards St. Martin’s Lane.

===Chinese===
一个人垂着头、双手深插在破外套口袋里，趿拉着脚步迎面过来，飞快斜了他们一眼；待他们走过，便停步回望，随即转身，加快趿拉的速度，缀上了他们的踪迹。科克斯珀街（Cockspur Street）拐角，人潮汹涌，车流望不到头，曼弗雷德和波伊卡特只好站住，等空子过街。等候的人群越聚越厚，他们挨了几下挤撞，总算过了马路，朝圣马丁巷（St. Martin's Lane）走去。

===Original===
The comparison which Poiccart had quoted still rankled with Manfred.

“There will be people at His Majesty’s tonight,” he said, “applauding Brutus as he asks, ‘What villain touched his body and not for justice?’ You will not find a serious student of history, or any commonplace man of intelligence, for the matter of that, who, if you asked, Would it not have been God’s blessing for the world if Bonaparte had been assassinated on his return from Egypt? would not answer without hesitation, Yes. But we﻿—we are murderers!”

“They would not have erected a statue to Napoleon’s assassin,” said Poiccart easily, “any more than they have enshrined Felton, who slew a profligate and debauched Minister of Charles I. Posterity may do us justice”; he spoke half mockingly; “for myself I am satisfied with the approval of my conscience.”

===Chinese===
波伊卡特转述的那个比方，还在曼弗雷德心里作痛。

「今晚陛下的剧院里，将有人为布鲁图鼓掌，听他问道：『什么恶徒触碰他的身体，竟不是为了正义？』你找不出一位认真的读史者——说白了，任何有点智识的寻常人——若问他：波拿巴自埃及归来时若遇刺身亡，岂非上天赐给世界的福祉？他不会不假思索地答一声：是。可我们——我们是杀人犯！」

「人们不会给刺杀拿破仑的人立像，」波伊卡特从容地说，「正如不曾把费尔顿（Felton）供上神龛——他杀的是查理一世那位荒淫无度的国务大臣。后世或许会还我们公道」；他说得半带讥诮；「至于我，有良心的首肯就够了。」

===Original===
He threw away the cigar he was smoking, and put his hand to the inside pocket of his coat to find another. He withdrew his hand without the cigar and whistled a passing cab.

Manfred looked at him in surprise.

“What is the matter? I thought you said you would walk?”

Nevertheless he entered the hansom and Poiccart followed, giving his direction through the trap, “Baker Street Station.”

===Chinese===
他丢掉抽着的雪茄，伸手到大衣内袋里再摸一支。手抽出来，却没有雪茄；他吹哨唤住一辆过路的马车。

曼弗雷德诧异地看着他。

「怎么了？你不是说要走路吗？」

他还是上了那辆双轮马车，波伊卡特跟着上来，隔着顶棚活门报了去处：「贝克街车站。」

===Original===
The cab was rattling through Shaftesbury Avenue before Poiccart gave any explanation.

“I have been robbed,” he said, sinking his voice, “my watch has gone, but that does not matter; the pocketbook with the notes I made for the guidance of Thery has gone﻿—and that matters a great deal.”

===Chinese===
马车辚辚驶过沙夫茨伯里大道（Shaftesbury Avenue），波伊卡特才作解释。

「我遭了扒。」他压低声音说，「表没了，那不要紧；给特里作指南的札记，连同记事本，也没了——这就大大要紧了。」

===Original===
“It may have been a common thief,” said Manfred: “he took the watch.”

Poiccart was feeling his pockets rapidly.

“Nothing else has gone,” he said; “it may have been, as you say, a pickpocket, who will be content with the watch and will drop the notebook down the nearest drain; but it may be a police agent.”

===Chinese===
「兴许只是个寻常小贼，」曼弗雷德说，「他不就拿了表嘛。」

波伊卡特飞快地浑身摸口袋。

「别的都没少。」他说；「或许如你所言，是个扒手，拿了表就知足，把本子丢进最近的阴沟；但也可能是个警方探子。」

===Original===
“Was there anything in it to identify you?” asked Manfred, in a troubled tone.

“Nothing,” was the prompt reply; “but unless the police are blind they would understand the calculations and the plans. It may not come to their hands at all, but if it does, and the thief can recognize us, we are in a fix.”

The cab drew up at the down station at Baker Street, and the two men alighted.

===Chinese===
「里头有什么能认出你身份的东西吗？」曼弗雷德问，声调不安。

「没有。」回答来得干脆；「可警方只要不是瞎子，那些演算和计划就看得懂。它也许根本到不了他们手里；可一旦到了，那贼又认得出我们，咱们就被动了。」

马车在贝克街的下行月台前停住，两人下了车。

===Original===
“I shall go east,” said Poiccart; “we will meet in the morning. By that time I shall have learnt whether the book has reached Scotland Yard. Good night.”

And with no other farewell than this the two men parted.

If Billy Marks had not had a drop of drink he would have been perfectly satisfied with his night’s work. Filled, however, with that false liquid confidence that leads so many good men astray, Billy thought it would be a sin to neglect the opportunities that the gods had shown him. The excitement engendered by the threats of the Four Just Men had brought all suburban London to Westminster, and on the Surrey side of the Bridge Billy found hundreds of patient suburbanites waiting for conveyance to Streatham, Camberwell, Clapham, and Greenwich. So, the night being comparatively young, Billy decided to work the trams.

===Chinese===
「我往东去。」波伊卡特说；「明早再见。到那时，本子到了没到苏格兰场，我也就打听到了。晚安。」

道别的话止于此，两人分手。

比利·马克斯（Billy Marks）这晚若不是灌了几杯黄汤，本该对这一夜的活计心满意足。可惜，灌满了那种把多少好人引入歧途的液体勇气，比利觉得，神明既已把机会摆到他眼前，白白放过便是罪过。四义士的威胁激起的兴奋，把整个伦敦郊外的人都引到了威斯敏斯特；大桥萨里一侧，比利发现成百上千有耐性的郊区客，等着车去斯特里厄姆、坎伯韦尔、克拉珀姆和格林尼治。夜色尚早，比利决定去电车上做一票。

===Original===
He touched a purse from a stout old lady in black, a Waterbury watch from a gentleman in a top hat, a small hand-mirror from a dainty bag, and decided to conclude his operations with the exploration of a superior young lady’s pocket. Billy’s search was successful. A purse and a lace handkerchief rewarded him, and he made arrangements for a modest retirement. Then it was that a gentle voice breathed into his ear. “Hullo, Billy!”

===Chinese===
他从一个穿黑衣的胖老太太身上摸了一只钱袋，从一位戴大礼帽的绅士身上摸了一块沃特伯里表，从一只精致的手袋里摸出一面小手镜，随后决定以探索一位上等小姐的口袋收束全局。比利搜得成功。一只钱袋、一方蕾丝手帕入了账，他筹划起一次适可而止的退场。就在此时，一个温和的声音贴着他耳朵响起：「哟，比利！」

===Original===
He knew the voice, and felt momentarily unwell.

“Hullo, Mister Howard,” he exclaimed with feigned Joy; “ ’ow are you, sir? Fancy meetin’ you!”

“Where are you going, Billy?” asked the welcome Mr. Howard, taking Billy’s arm affectionately.

“ ’Ome,” said the virtuous Billy.

===Chinese===
这声音他认得，当下便觉得身子有些不适。

「哟，霍华德先生！」他装出欢喜，嚷道；「您老可好？真是有缘咧！」

「这是上哪儿去呀，比利？」受欢迎的霍华德先生问，亲亲热热挽住比利的手臂。

「回府。」品行端方的比利说。

===Original===
“Home it is,” said Mr. Howard, leading the unwilling Billy from the crowd; “home, sweet home, it is, Billy.” He called another young man, with whom he seemed to be acquainted: “Go on that car, Porter, and see who has lost anything. If you can find anybody bring them along,” and the other young man obeyed.

“And now,” said Mr. Howard, still holding Billy’s arm affectionately, “tell me how the world has been using you.”

“Look ’ere, Mr. Howard,” said Billy earnestly, “what’s the game? Where are you takin’ me?”

===Chinese===
「回府，好极了。」霍华德先生把不情愿的比利从人群里领出来，说；「回府，甜蜜的家，是吧，比利。」他又唤来另一个年轻人，看样子是他的熟人：「波特，上那辆车，看看谁丢了东西。找得着人就带过来。」那年轻人领命去了。

「现在，」霍华德先生仍旧亲热地挽着比利的手臂，说，「讲讲吧，这世道待你如何呀。」

「您听我说，霍华德先生，」比利郑重其事，「这是唱的哪出？您这是带我上哪儿？」

===Original===
“The game is the old game,” said Mr. Howard sadly﻿—“the same old game, Bill, and I’m taking you to the same old sweet spot.”

“You’ve made a mistake this time, guv’nor,” cried Billy fiercely, and there was a slight clink.

“Permit me, Billy,” said Mr. Howard, stooping quickly and picking up the purse Billy had dropped.

===Chinese===
「戏码还是老戏码。」霍华德先生伤感地说——「老一套，比尔，我带你去的，还是那个老熟地方。」

「这回您可弄岔了，长官。」比利凶巴巴地嚷，接着轻轻一声脆响。

「让我来，比利。」霍华德先生飞快俯身，拾起比利丢下的钱袋。

===Original===
At the police station the sergeant behind the charge desk pretended to be greatly overjoyed at Billy’s arrival, and the jailer who put Billy into a steel-barred dock and passed his hands through cunning pockets greeted him as a friend.

“Gold watch, half a chain, gold, three purses, two handkerchiefs, and a red moroccer pocketbook,” reported the jailer.

The sergeant nodded approvingly.

===Chinese===
到了警察局，立案台后的巡佐假装为比利的到来喜出望外；狱卒把比利关进铁栏囚座，一双巧手遍探他那些机关口袋，也把他当故人招呼。

「金表一块，金半链一条，钱袋三只，手帕两方，红色摩洛哥皮记事本一本。」狱卒报账。

巡佐赞许地点头。

===Original===
“Quite a good day’s work, William,” he said.

“What shall I get this time?” inquired the prisoner, and Mr. Howard, a plainclothes officer engaged in filling in particulars of the charge, opined nine moon.

“Go on!” exclaimed Mr. Billy Marks in consternation.

===Chinese===
「干得相当漂亮，威廉。」他说。

「这回能吃几个月？」囚徒打听；便衣警官霍华德先生正填着立案单，估了个九个月。

「去你的吧！」比利·马克斯先生愕然嚷道。

===Original===
“Fact,” said the sergeant; “you’re a rogue and a vagabond, Billy, you’re a petty larcenist, and you’re for the sessions this time﻿—Number Eight.”

This latter was addressed to the jailer, who bore Billy off to the cells protesting vigorously against a police force that could only tumble to poor blokes, and couldn’t get a touch on sanguinary murderers like the Four Just Men.

“What do we pay rates and taxes for?” indignantly demanded Billy through the grating of his cell.

===Chinese===
「实话。」巡佐说；「你是个流氓加游民，比利，是个小偷小摸，这回要过堂了——八号。」

后半句是对狱卒说的；狱卒把比利押去囚室，一路上比利大声抗议：这警察队伍，就会拿穷哥儿们开刀，像四义士那样杀人不眨眼的凶徒，倒一根毛也摸不着。

「咱们缴捐纳税，图的是什么？」比利隔着囚室铁栅，愤然质问。

===Original===
“Fat lot you’ll ever pay, Billy,” said the jailer, putting the double lock on the door.

In the charge office Mr. Howard and the sergeant were examining the stolen property, and three owners, discovered by P.C. Porter, were laying claim to their own.

“That disposes of all the articles except the gold watch and the pocketbook,” said the sergeant after the claimants had gone, “gold watch, Elgin half-hunter, No. 5029020; pocketbook containing no papers, no card, no address, and only three pages of writing. What this means I don’t know.” The sergeant handed the book to Howard. The page that puzzled the policeman contained simply a list of streets. Against each street was scrawled a cabalistic character.

===Chinese===
「你缴的那点，够干什么的，比利。」狱卒说着，给门上了双道锁。

立案室里，霍华德先生和巡佐正在查验赃物；波特警员寻来的三位失主，正各自认领自己的东西。

「这样一来，除了金表和记事本，东西都有了去处。」失主走后，巡佐说，「金表，埃尔金双盖半开式，编号 5029020；记事本，无文件，无名片，无地址，只有三页字。什么意思，我就不懂了。」巡佐把本子递给霍华德。让警察犯难的那一页，只有一张街道清单，每条街对面涂着一个天书似的符号。

===Original===
“Looks like the diary of a paper-chase,” said Mr. Howard. “What is on the other pages?”

They turned the leaf. This was filled with figures.

“H’m,” said the disappointed sergeant, and again turned overleaf. The contents of this page was understandable and readable, although evidently written in a hurry as though it had been taken down at dictation.

===Chinese===
「像撒纸追踪游戏的日记。」霍华德先生说。「别的页上是什么？」

两人翻过一页。满纸数字。

「哼。」巡佐大失所望，又翻过一页。这一页的内容倒明白易读，只是显然写得匆忙，像是一边听人口述一边记的。

===Original===
“The chap who wrote this must have had a train to catch,” said the facetious Mr. Howard, pointing to the abbreviations:

===Chinese===
「写这个的家伙准是赶火车去了。」爱说笑的霍华德先生指着那些缩语说：

===Original===
> “Will not leave D.S., except to Hs. Will drive to Hs in M.C. (4 dummy brghms first), 8:30. At 2 600 p arve traf divtd Embank, 80 spls inside D.S. One each rm, three each cor, six basemt, six rf. All drs wide opn allow each off see another, all spls will carry revr. Nobody except F. and H. to approach R. In Hse strange gal filled with pl, all press vouched for. 200 spl in cor. If nec battalion guards at disposal.”

===Chinese===
> 「不离 D.S.，唯赴 Hs.。乘 M.C. 赴 Hs.（先发 4 辆伪 bhms），8:30。2 时 600 警抵，车辆分流河滨大道，D.S. 内 80 spls。每室一人，每角三人，地下室六人，屋顶六人。诸门大开，使各警互见；众警携左轮。除 F. 与 H. 外任何人不得近 R.。议院异廊满员，报人皆具保。走廊 200 警。必要时可调营卫。」

===Original===
The policemen read this over slowly.

“Now what the devil does that mean?” asked the sergeant helplessly.

It was at that precise moment that Constable Howard earned his promotion.

===Chinese===
两个警察慢慢读了一遍。

「这鬼东西到底是什么意思？」巡佐无可奈何地问。

恰恰就在这一刻，霍华德警员挣得了他的升迁。

===Original===
“Let me have that book for ten minutes,” he said excitedly. The sergeant handed the book over with wondering stare.

“I think I can find an owner for this,” said Howard, his hand trembling as he took the book, and ramming his hat on his head he ran out into the street.

He did not stop running until he reached the main road, and finding a cab he sprang in with a hurried order to the driver.

===Chinese===
「把本子给我十分钟。」他激动地说。巡佐瞪着惊奇的眼，把本子递了过去。

「我想我能给这本子找到失主。」霍华德说，接本子的手直抖；他把帽子往头上一扣，冲到街上。

他一路跑到大马路才停下，寻着一辆马车，跳上去急急吩咐了车夫。

===Original===
“Whitehall, and drive like blazes,” he called, and in a few minutes he was explaining his errand to the inspector in charge of the cordon that guarded the entrance of Downing Street.

“Constable Howard, 946 L. reserve,” he introduced himself. “I’ve a very important message for Superintendent Falmouth.”

That officer, looking tired and beaten, listened to the policeman’s story.

===Chinese===
「白厅，拼命跑！」他喊道；几分钟后，他已把来意讲给把守唐宁街入口警戒线的督察听。

「后备队霍华德警员，946 L。」他自报家门。「有要紧消息面呈法尔茅斯警司。」

那位警官面带倦色、一败涂地的模样，听完了警员的讲述。

===Original===
“It looks to me,” went on Howard breathlessly, “as though this had something to do with your case, sir. D.S. is Downing Street, and﻿—” He produced the book and Falmouth snatched at it.

He read a few words and then gave a triumphant cry.

“Our secret instructions,” he cried, and catching the constable by the arm he drew him to the entrance hall.

===Chinese===
「依我看，」霍华德上气不接下气地往下说，「这东西跟您的案子有干系，先生。D.S. 就是唐宁街，还有——」他掏出本子，法尔茅斯一把抢了过去。

他读了几个词，随即发出一声得胜的呼喊。

「咱们的机密指令！」他喊道，一把攥住警员的手臂，把他拽到门厅。

===Original===
“Is my car outside?” he asked, and in response to a whistle a car drew up. “Jump in, Howard,” said the detective, and the car slipped into Whitehall.

“Who is the thief?” asked the senior.

“Billy Marks, sir,” replied Howard; “you may not know him, but down at Lambeth he is a well-known character.”

===Chinese===
「我的车在外头吗？」他问；一声哨响，一辆车靠了过来。「上车，霍华德。」侦探说，汽车滑进白厅。

「贼是谁？」长官问。

「比利·马克斯，先生。」霍华德答道；「您也许不认得，可在兰贝斯一带，他是个人物。」

===Original===
“Oh, yes,” Falmouth hastened to correct, “I know Billy very well indeed﻿—we’ll see what he has to say.”

The car drew up at the police station and the two men jumped out.

The sergeant rose to his feet as he recognized the famous Falmouth, and saluted.

===Chinese===
「哦，认得。」法尔茅斯忙更正，「比利我熟得很——看看他有什么可说。」

车在警察局门口停住，两人跳下车。

巡佐认出大名鼎鼎的法尔茅斯，起身敬礼。

===Original===
“I want to see the prisoner Marks,” said Falmouth shortly, and Billy, roused from his sleep, came blinking into the charge office.

“Now, Billy,” said the detective, “I’ve got a few words to say to you.”

“Why, it’s Mr. Falmouth,” said the astonished Billy, and something like fear shaded his face. “I wasn’t in that ’Oxton affair, s’help me.”

===Chinese===
「我要见在押的马克斯。」法尔茅斯简短地说；比利被从睡梦中唤醒，眨巴着眼走进立案室。

「听着，比利。」侦探说，「我有几句话跟你讲。」

「哎呀，是法尔茅斯先生。」比利吃了一惊，脸上掠过一丝近乎恐惧的东西。「霍克斯顿那档子事可没我的份，老天爷作证。」

===Original===
“Make your mind easy, Billy; I don’t want you for anything, and if you’ll answer my questions truthfully, you may get off the present charge and get a reward into the bargain.”

Billy was suspicious.

“I’m not going to give anybody away, if that’s what you mean,” he said, sullenly.

“Nor that, either,” said the detective impatiently. “I want to know where you found this pocketbook,” and he held it up.

===Chinese===
「把心放肚子里，比利；我不办你什么事；你若肯如实回答我的问题，眼下的罪名兴许一笔勾销，另有一笔赏钱好拿。」

比利满腹狐疑。

「要出卖人，我可不干，您要是指望这个的话。」他阴着脸说。

「也不是这个。」侦探不耐烦地说。「我要知道，这记事本你哪儿弄来的。」他把本子举起来。

===Original===
Billy grinned.

“Found it lyin’ on the pavement,” he lied.

“I want the truth,” thundered Falmouth.

“Well,” said Billy sulkily, “I pinched it.”

===Chinese===
比利咧嘴一笑。

「躺在大街上捡的。」他撒谎。

「我要听实话。」法尔茅斯雷鸣般喝道。

「好吧，」比利怄气地说，「是我顺的。」

===Original===
“From whom?”

“I didn’t stop to ask him his name,” was the impudent reply.

The detective breathed deeply.

“Now, look here,” he said, lowering his voice, “you’ve heard about the Four Just Men?”

===Chinese===
「从谁身上？」

「我还没停下来问他贵姓呢。」回答痞气十足。

侦探深深吸了口气。

「这么着，」他压低声音说，「四义士的事你听说过吧？」

===Original===
Billy nodded, opening his eyes in amazement at the question.

“Well,” exclaimed Falmouth impressively, “the man to whom this pocketbook belongs is one of them.”

“What!” cried Billy.

“For his capture there is a reward of a thousand pounds offered. If your description leads to his arrest that thousand is yours.”

===Chinese===
比利点头，被这问题惊得睁大了眼。

「那么，」法尔茅斯郑重其事地说，「这本记事本的主人，就是其中之一。」

「什么！」比利喊。

「缉拿他，悬赏一千英镑。你若能描述得他落网，那一千英镑就归你。」

===Original===
Marks stood paralyzed at the thought.

“A thousand﻿—a thousand?” he muttered in a dazed fashion. “And I might just as easily have caught him!”

“Come, come!” cried the detective, sharply. “You may catch him yet﻿—tell us what he looked like.”

Billy knitted his brows in thought.

===Chinese===
马克斯想到这里，整个人僵住了。

「一千——一千？」他晕头晕脑地喃喃。「我本来唾手就能拿住他的！」

「喂，喂！」侦探厉声道。「兴许还抓得着——说说他长什么样。」

比利皱眉苦想。

===Original===
“He looked like a gentleman,” he said, trying to recall from the chaos of his mind a picture of his victim; “he had a white weskit, a white shirt, nice patent shoes﻿—”

“But his face﻿—his face!” demanded the detective.

“His face?” cried Billy, indignantly. “How do I know what it looked like? I don’t look a chap in the face when I’m pinching his watch, do I?”

===Chinese===
「看着像位爷。」他说，努力从满脑子的乱麻里召回苦主的模样；「他穿白坎肩，白衬衫，锃亮的漆皮鞋——」

「可他的脸——他的脸！」侦探追着问。

「他的脸？」比利愤愤地嚷。「我哪知道长什么样？顺人表的时候，我还能盯着人家脸瞧不成？」

## X The Cupidity of Marks / 十 马克斯的贪心
===Original===
“You cursed dolt, you infernal fool!” stormed the detective, catching Billy by the collar and shaking him like a rat. “Do you mean to tell me that you had one of the Four Just Men in your hand, and did not even take the trouble to look at him?”

Billy wrenched himself free.

“You leave me alone!” he said defiantly. “How was I to know it was one of the Four Just Men, and how do you know it was?” he added with a cunning twist of his face. Billy’s mind was beginning to work rapidly. He saw in this staggering statement of the detective a chance of making capital out of the position which to within a few minutes he had regarded as singularly unfortunate.

===Chinese===
「你这该死的蠢货，你这不可救药的糊涂蛋！」侦探勃然大怒，一把揪住比利的领子，拎耗子似的摇晃他。「你是说，四义士之一落进你手里，你竟连正眼瞧他一看都懒得瞧？」

比利挣脱开来。

「你少来这一套！」他梗着脖子说。「我哪知道那是四义士之一？再说，你又怎么知道就是？」他脸上一副狡黠的扭捏，补了这么一句。比利的脑子开始飞转。侦探这句惊人的话让他看见了转机：几分钟前他还当作倒霉透顶的处境，眼下大有油水可捞。

===Original===
“I did get a bit of a glance at ’em,” he said, “they﻿—”

“Them﻿—they?” said the detective quickly. “How many were there?”

“Never mind,” said Billy sulkily. He felt the strength of his position.

===Chinese===
「我倒是瞅了他们一眼。」他说，「他们——」

「他们——他们？」侦探疾声问。「一共几个？」

「不告诉你。」比利怄气地说。他掂得出自己这位置的分量。

===Original===
“Billy,” said the detective earnestly, “I mean business; if you know anything you’ve got to tell us.”

“Ho!” cried the prisoner in defiance. “Got to, ’ave I? Well, I know the lor as well as you﻿—you can’t make a chap speak if he don’t want. You can’t﻿—”

The detective signalled the other police officers to retire, and when they were out of earshot he dropped his voice and said:

===Chinese===
「比利，」侦探恳切地说，「我是来真的；你知道什么，就得告诉我们。」

「嚯！」囚徒叫板。「就得，是吧？法条我懂得不比你少——人家不愿意开口，你还能掰开人家的嘴？你掰不了——」

侦探示意其他警员退下；待他们走远了听不见，他压低嗓门说：

===Original===
“Harry Moss came out last week.”

Billy flushed and lowered his eyes.

“I don’t know no Harry Moss,” he muttered, doggedly.

“Harry Moss came out last week,” continued the detective shortly, “after doing three years for robbery with violence﻿—three years and ten lashes.”

===Chinese===
「哈里·莫斯上礼拜放出来了。」

比利涨红了脸，垂下眼皮。

「我不认得什么哈里·莫斯。」他犟着头咕哝。

「哈里·莫斯上礼拜放出来了。」侦探简短地往下说，「暴力抢劫，吃了三年官司——三年，外加十下鞭子。」

===Original===
“I don’t know anything about it,” said Marks, in the same tone.

“He got clean away and the police had no clues,” the detective went on remorselessly, “and they might not have caught him to this day, only﻿—only ‘from information received’ they took him one night out of his bed in Leman Street.”

Billy licked his dry lips but did not speak.

“Harry Moss would like to know who he owes his three stretch to﻿—and the ten. Men who’ve had the cat have a long memory, Billy.”

===Chinese===
「这事我一概不知。」马克斯还是那个腔调。

「他溜得干干净净，警方一点线索没有，」侦探毫不留情地往下说，「到今天兴许还没抓着他，只可惜——只可惜『据举报』，一天夜里，他们把他从利曼街的被窝里拎了出来。」

比利舔了舔发干的嘴唇，不作声。

「哈里·莫斯一定很想知道，他那三年是谁送的——还有那十鞭。挨过猫尾鞭的人，记性长着呢，比利。」

===Original===
“That’s not playing the game, Mr. Falmouth,” cried Billy thickly. “I﻿—I was a bit hard up, an’ Harry Moss wasn’t a pal of mine﻿—and the p’lice wanted to find out﻿—”

“And the police want to find out now,” said Falmouth.

Billy Marks made no reply for a moment.

“I’ll tell you all there is to be told,” he said at last, and cleared his throat. The detective stopped him.

===Chinese===
「这可不地道，法尔茅斯先生。」比利嗓子发堵地嚷。「我——我那阵子手头紧，哈里·莫斯又不是我的拜把子——警察又非要打听——」

「眼下警察也非要打听。」法尔茅斯说。

比利·马克斯有一会儿没吭声。

「能说的我全说。」他终于开口，清了清嗓子。侦探拦住了他。

===Original===
“Not here,” he said. Then turning to the officer in charge:

“Sergeant, you may release this man on bail﻿—I will stand sponsor.” The humorous side of this appealed to Billy at least, for he grinned sheepishly and recovered his former spirits.

“First time I’ve been bailed out by the p’lice,” he remarked, facetiously.

===Chinese===
「这儿不行。」他说。随即转向主事警官：

「巡佐，这人可以保释——我做保人。」这话滑稽的一面至少打动了比利，他讪讪一笑，精神头又回来了。

「头一回叫警察保出去。」他打趣道。

===Original===
The motorcar bore the detective and his charge to Scotland Yard, and in Superintendent Falmouth’s office Billy prepared to unburden himself.

“Before you begin,” said the officer, “I want to warn you that you must be as brief as possible. Every minute is precious.”

So Billy told his story. In spite of the warning there were embellishments, to which the detective was forced to listen impatiently.

At last the pickpocket reached the point.

===Chinese===
汽车载着侦探和他的人犯到了苏格兰场；在法尔茅斯警司的办公室里，比利准备和盘托出。

「你开口之前，」警官说，「我得提醒你，能多简短就多简短。每一分钟都金贵。」

于是比利讲了他的故事。尽管有言在先，添油加醋总归少不了，侦探只得耐着性子听。

扒手总算说到了正题。

===Original===
“There was two of ’em, one a tall chap and one not so tall. I heard one say ‘My dear George’﻿—the little one said that, the one I took the ticker from and the pocketbook. Was there anything in the notebook?” Billy asked suddenly.

“Go on,” said the detective.

“Well,” resumed Billy, “I follered ’em up to the end of the street, and they was waitin’ to cross towards Charing Cross Road when I lifted the clock, you understand?”

“What time was this?”

“ ’Arf-past ten﻿—or it might’ve been eleven.”

===Chinese===
「他们俩，一个高个儿，一个不那么高。我听见一个说『我亲爱的乔治』——是矮个儿说的，就是我从他身上摸了怀表和记事本的那个。本子里有什么没有？」比利突然问。

「往下说。」侦探道。

「是这么着，」比利接着讲，「我跟他们跟到街口，他们正等着往查令十字街那边过马路，我就把钟给起了，明白吗？」

「这是几点的事？」

===Original===
“And you did not see their faces?”

The thief shook his head emphatically.

“If I never get up from where I’m sittin’ I didn’t, Mr. Falmouth,” he said earnestly.

The detective rose with a sigh.

===Chinese===
「他们的脸你没看着？」

贼把头摇得像拨浪鼓。

「我要是瞧了，叫我这椅子坐塌了都成，法尔茅斯先生。」他说得情词恳切。

侦探叹口气站起来。

===Original===
“I’m afraid you’re not much use to me, Billy,” he said ruefully. “Did you notice whether they wore beards, or were they clean-shaven, or﻿—”

Billy shook his head mournfully.

“I could easily tell you a lie, Mr. Falmouth,” he said frankly, “and I could easily pitch a tale that would take you in, but I’m playin’ it square with you.”

The detective recognized the sincerity of the man and nodded.

===Chinese===
「恐怕你对我用处不大，比利。」他怅然道。「他们留没留胡子，脸刮没刮干净，这些你留意了吗，或者——」

比利悲苦地摇头。

「我要想骗您，容易得很，法尔茅斯先生，」他坦白地说，「编套瞎话把您装进去也不费事，可我这回跟您来的是正的。」

侦探看出这人的诚意，点了点头。

===Original===
“You’ve done your best, Billy,” he said, and then﻿—“I’ll tell you what I’m going to do. You are the only man in the world who has ever seen one of the Four Just Men﻿—and lived to tell the story. Now, although you cannot remember his face, perhaps if you met him again in the street you would know him﻿—there may be some little trick of walking, some habit of holding the hands that you cannot recall now, but if you saw again you would recognize. I shall therefore take upon myself the responsibility of releasing you from custody until the day after tomorrow. I want you to find this man you robbed. Here is a sovereign; go home, get a little sleep, turn out as early as you can and go west.” The detective went to his desk and wrote a dozen words on a card. “Take this; if you see the man or his companion, follow them, show this card to the first policeman you meet, point out the man, and you’ll go to bed a thousand pounds richer than when you woke.”

Billy took the card.

“If you want me at any time you will find somebody here who will know where I am. Good night,” and Billy passed into the street, his brain in a whirl, and a warrant written on a visiting-card in his waistcoat pocket.

===Chinese===
「你尽力了，比利。」他说，随即——「告诉你我打算怎么办。普天之下，见过四义士之一、还活着讲这段故事的，只有你一个。如今你虽记不起他的脸，可若在街上再碰上，没准你认得出——兴许是某种走路的做派，某种搁手的习惯，眼下想不起来，再见着便认得出。因此我来自担干系，放你出去，后天为限。我要你去找你偷的那个人。这一枚沙弗林拿去；回家，睡一小觉，天一亮就出动，往西去。」侦探走到桌前，在一张名片上写了十来个字。「带上它；见到那人或他的同伴，就跟住，把片子亮给你碰见的第一个警察，把人指出来，等你上床的时候，口袋里就比起床时多出一千英镑。」

比利接过名片。

「随时要找我，这儿总有人知道我在哪儿。晚安。」比利上了街，脑子一团旋，坎肩口袋里揣着一张写在名片上的逮捕令。

---
===Original===
The morning that was to witness great events broke bright and clear over London. Manfred, who, contrary to his usual custom, had spent the night at the workshop in Carnaby Street, watched the dawn from the flat roof of the building.

He lay face downwards, a rug spread beneath him, his head resting on his hands. Dawn, with its white, pitiless light, showed his strong face, seamed and haggard. The white streaks in his trim beard were accentuated in the light of morning. He looked tired and disheartened, so unlike his usual self that Gonsalez, who crept up through the trap just before the sun rose, was as near alarmed as it was possible for that phlegmatic man to be. He touched him on the arm and Manfred started.

“What is the matter?” asked Leon softly.

===Chinese===
将要见证大事的那个早晨，明亮晴朗地降临伦敦。曼弗雷德一反常态，夜里就宿在卡纳比街的工场里，此刻正在大楼平顶上看天亮。

他趴着，身下垫一方毯子，头枕在手上。黎明那惨白无情的亮光，照出他那张坚毅的脸，沟壑纵横，憔悴不堪。修整的胡须里那几缕白痕，在晨光里愈发扎眼。他一脸倦怠颓唐，全然不像平日的他，以致日出前从活门里爬上来的冈萨雷斯，惊得差一点破了那位冷性子人的功。他碰碰他的手臂，曼弗雷德猛地一惊。

「怎么了？」莱昂轻声问。

===Original===
Manfred’s smile and shake of head did not reassure the questioner.

“Is it Poiccart and the thief?”

“Yes,” nodded Manfred. Then speaking aloud, he asked: “Have you ever felt over any of our cases as you feel in this?”

They spoke in such low tones as almost to approach whispering. Gonsalez stared ahead thoughtfully.

===Chinese===
曼弗雷德笑笑，摇摇头，并没能让发问的人安心。

「是为波伊卡特和那贼？」

「是。」曼弗雷德点头。随即扬声问：「从前的案子，你可有过这一桩里的感觉？」

两人说话的声音低得近乎耳语。冈萨雷斯若有所思地望着前方。

===Original===
“Yes,” he admitted, “once﻿—the woman at Warsaw. You remember how easy it all seemed, and how circumstance after circumstance thwarted us﻿ ﻿… till I began to feel, as I feel now, that we should fail.”

“No, no, no!” said Manfred fiercely. “There must be no talk of failure, Leon, no thought of it.”

He crawled to the trapdoor and lowered himself into the corridor, and Gonsalez followed.

===Chinese===
「有过。」他承认，「一回——华沙那个女人。你记得，起初一切看着多么顺手，又一桩又一桩的意外跟我们作对……直到我开始觉得——就像现在那样——我们要输了。」

「不，不，不！」曼弗雷德厉声道。「不许说败，莱昂，想也不许想。」

他爬到活门处，缒身下到过道，冈萨雷斯跟着下来。

===Original===
“Thery?” he asked.

“Asleep.”

They were entering the studio, and Manfred had his hand on the door-handle when a footstep sounded on the bottom floor.

“Who’s there?” cried Manfred, and a soft whistle from below sent him flying downstairs.

===Chinese===
「特里呢？」他问。

「睡着。」

两人正要进摄影棚，曼弗雷德的手刚搭上门把，底层响起一阵脚步声。

「谁？」曼弗雷德喝了一声；楼下一声轻哨，他飞奔下楼。

===Original===
“Poiccart!” he cried.

Poiccart it was, unshaven, dusty, weary.

“Well?” Manfred’s ejaculation was almost brutal in its bluntness.

“Let us go upstairs,” said Poiccart, shortly. The three men ascended the dusty stairway, not a word being spoken until they had reached the small living-room.

===Chinese===
「波伊卡特！」他喊。

正是波伊卡特，胡子没刮，一身尘土，一脸倦容。

「怎么样？」曼弗雷德这一声短促，直率得近乎粗暴。

「上楼说。」波伊卡特简短地说。三人登上积尘的楼梯，直到走进那间小起居室，一路上没说一句话。

===Original===
Then Poiccart spoke.

“The very stars in their courses are fighting against us,” he said, throwing himself into the only comfortable chair in the room, and flinging his hat into a corner. “The man who stole my pocketbook has been arrested by the police. He is a well-known criminal of a sneak-thief order, and unfortunately he had been under observation during the evening. The pocketbook was found in his possession, and all might have been well, but an unusually smart police officer associated the contents with us.

“After I had left you I went home and changed, then made my way to Downing Street. I was one of the curious crowd that stood watching the guarded entrance. I knew that Falmouth was there, and I knew, too, if there was any discovery made it would be communicated immediately to Downing Street. Somehow I felt sure the man was an ordinary thief, and that if we had anything to fear it was from a chance arrest. Whilst I was waiting a cab dashed up, and out an excited man jumped. He was obviously a policeman, and I had just time to engage a hansom when Falmouth and the new arrival came flying out. I followed them in the cab as fast as possible without exciting the suspicion of the driver. Of course, they outdistanced us, but their destination was evident. I dismissed the cab at the corner of the street in which the police station is situated, and walked down and found, as I had expected, the car drawn up at the door.

===Chinese===
波伊卡特这才开口。

「老天爷摆明了跟咱们作对。」他说着，一屁股坐进屋里唯一舒适的椅子，把帽子甩到墙角。「偷我记事本的人，叫警察抓了。他是个有名有姓的溜门贼，不巧晚上又一直处在监视之下。记事本在他身上搜了出来，本该没事，偏偏一个格外精明的警官，把本子里的内容跟咱们挂上了钩。

「同你们分手后，我回家换了装，便往唐宁街去。守着那道戒备森严入口看热闹的人群里，就有我一个。我知道法尔茅斯在那里；我也知道，一旦有了什么发现，会立刻报到唐宁街。不知怎么，我笃定那人只是个寻常小贼；咱们若有可怕的，只怕是撞运气的盘查。正等着，一辆马车疾驰而至，跳下来一个神色激动的人。一看便知是警察；我赶在法尔茅斯和来人飞奔而出之前，刚来得及雇上一辆双轮车。我让车夫尽力跟，又不叫他起疑。他们自然把我们甩了，可去向明摆着。我在警察局那条街的街角下了车，步行过去，果然见那辆汽车停在门口。

===Original===
“I managed to get a fleeting glance at the charge room﻿—I was afraid that any interrogation there might be would have been conducted in the cell, but by the greatest of good luck they had chosen the charge room. I saw Falmouth, and the policeman, and the prisoner. The latter, a mean-faced, long-jawed man with shifty eyes﻿—no, no, Leon, don’t question me about the physiognomy of the man﻿—my view was for photographic purposes﻿—I wanted to remember him.

“In that second I could see the detective’s anger, the thief’s defiance, and I knew that the man was saying that he could not recognize us.”

“Ha!” It was Manfred’s sigh of relief that put a period to Poiccart’s speech.

===Chinese===
「我设法朝立案室飞快瞥了一眼——我原本担心，若有盘问，会在囚室里进行，万幸他们选了立案室。我看见了法尔茅斯、那警员，还有那囚犯。后者一张刻薄脸，长下巴，眼睛滴溜溜——不，不，莱昂，别问我那人的相——我看那一眼，是为着照相的目的——我要记住他。

「那一秒钟里，我看得见侦探的怒气、贼的抗辩，我知道那人正在一口咬定，认不出我们。」

「哈！」曼弗雷德这一声如释重负的长气，给波伊卡特的话画了个句号。

===Original===
“But I wanted to make sure,” resumed the latter. “I walked back the way I had come. Suddenly I heard the hum of the car behind me, and it passed me with another passenger. I guessed that they were taking the man back to Scotland Yard.

“I was content to walk back; I was curious to know what the police intended doing with their new recruit. Taking up a station that gave me a view of the entrance of the street, I waited. After a while the man came out alone. His step was light and buoyant. A glimpse I got of his face showed me a strange blending of bewilderment and gratification. He turned on to the Embankment, and I followed close behind.”

“There was a danger that he was being shadowed by the police too,” said Gonsalez warningly.

“Of that I was well satisfied,” Poiccart rejoined. “I took a very careful survey before I acted. Apparently the police were content to let him roam free. When he was abreast of the Temple steps he stopped and looked undecidedly left and right, as though he were not quite certain as to what he should do next. At that moment I came abreast of him, passed him, and then turned back, fumbling in my pockets.

===Chinese===
「可我要确凿。」他继续说。「我循原路折回。忽然听见身后汽车嗡嗡，驶过我身边，车里多了一个人。我猜，他们正把那人送回苏格兰场。

「我乐得步行折回；我好奇，警方打算怎么发落他们这位新伙计。我拣了个望得见街口的位置，等着。过了一阵，那人独自出来了。脚步轻快，弹跳跳的。我瞥见他脸上一种古怪的混合：又茫然，又餍足。他转上河滨大道，我紧跟在后。」

「只怕他自己也有警察盯着。」冈萨雷斯提醒道。

「这一层我放心得很。」波伊卡特答道。「动手之前，我四面八方看了个仔细。看样子，警察乐得放他野跑。走到与律师学院台阶平齐处，他停下，踌躇地左顾右盼，好像拿不准下一步做什么。就在那时，我赶到与他并齐，走了过去，又折回来，在口袋里摸索。

===Original===
“ ‘Can you oblige me with a match?’ I asked.

“He was most affable; produced a box of matches and invited me to help myself.

“I took a match, struck it, and lit my cigar, holding the match so that he could see my face.”

“That was wise,” said Manfred gravely.

===Chinese===
「『劳驾，借个火？』我问。

「他客气极了；掏出一盒火柴，请我自取。

「我抽出一根，划着，点雪茄，火柴举得恰好叫他看清我的脸。」」

「这一手高明。」曼弗雷德正色道。

===Original===
“It showed his face, too, and out of the corner of my eye I watched him searching every feature. But there was no sign of recognition and I began a conversation. We lingered where we had met for a while and then by mutual consent we walked in the direction of Blackfriars, crossed the bridge, chatting on inconsequent subjects, the poor, the weather, the newspapers. On the other side of the bridge is a coffee-stall. I determined to make my next move. I invited him to take a cup of coffee, and when the cups were placed before us, I put down a sovereign. The stall-keeper shook his head, said he could not change it. ‘Hasn’t your friend any small change?’ he asked.

“It was here that the vanity of the little thief told me what I wanted to know. He drew from his pocket, with a nonchalant air﻿—a sovereign. ‘This is all that I have got,’ he drawled. I found some coppers﻿—I had to think quickly. He had told the police something, something worth paying for﻿—what was it? It could not have been a description of ourselves, for if he had recognized us then, he would have known me when I struck the match and when I stood there, as I did, in the full glare of the light of the coffee-stall. And then a cold fear came to me. Perhaps he had recognized me, and with a thief’s cunning was holding me in conversation until he could get assistance to take me.”

Poiccart paused for a moment, and drew a small phial from his pocket; this he placed carefully on the table.

===Chinese===
「火光也照出了他的脸；我眼角吊着他，看他把我一张脸逐处搜检。可毫无认出的迹象，我便起了话头。我们在相遇处盘桓了一阵，又一前一后朝黑衣修士桥方向走去，过了桥，一路闲扯些不相干的题目：穷人，天气，报纸。桥那头有一个咖啡摊。我决定走下一步。我请他喝杯咖啡；杯子端上来，我搁下一枚沙弗林。摊主摇头，说找不开。『您这位朋友没有零钱吗？』他问。

「就在这里，小贼的虚荣心把我想知道的告诉了我。他一副满不在乎的神气，从口袋里摸出——一枚沙弗林。『我就这些了。』他慢条斯理地说。我翻出几个铜板——我不得不快点想。他向警察交代了什么，一件值得出钱买的事——是什么？总不会是对我们的描述，因为那时他若认出了我们，我划火柴时、我站在咖啡摊灯下像刚才那样时，他就该认出我。随即一股寒意袭来。说不定他早认出了我，凭着贼的狡猾，拖着我攀谈，等帮手来拿我。」

波伊卡特停了停，从口袋里摸出一只小药瓶，端端正正放在桌上。

===Original===
“He was as near to death then as ever he has been in his life,” he said quietly; “but somehow the suspicion wore away. In our walk we had passed three policemen﻿—there was an opportunity if he had wanted it.

“He drank his coffee and said, ‘I must be going home.’

“ ‘Indeed!’ I said. ‘I suppose I really ought to go home too﻿—I have a lot of work to do tomorrow.’ He leered at me. ‘So have I,’ he said with a grin, ‘but whether I can do it or not I don’t know.’

“We had left the coffee-stall, and now stopped beneath a lamp that stood at the corner of the street. I knew that I had only a few seconds to secure the information I wanted﻿—so I played boldly and led directly to the subject. ‘What of these Four Just Men?’ I asked, just as he was about to slouch away. He turned back instantly. ‘What about them?’ he asked, quickly. I led him on from that by gentle stages to the identity of the Four. He was eager to talk about them, anxious to know what I thought, but most concerned of all about the reward. He was engrossed in the subject, and then suddenly he leaned forward, and, tapping me on the chest with a grimy forefinger, he commenced to state a hypothetical case.”

===Chinese===
「那一刻，是他这辈子离死最近的一回。」他平静地说；「可那疑心不知怎么淡了下去。一路走来，我们经过三个警察——他要真想动手，机会有的是。

「他喝完咖啡，说：『我该回家了。』

「『是嘛！』我说。『说起来我也该回家了——明天我有一大堆事。』他斜眼瞟我。『我也是。』他咧嘴一笑，『干不干得成，另说。』

「我们离了咖啡摊，在街角一盏灯下站住。我知道，要把想知道的弄到手，只剩几秒钟——我便放胆直入正题。『这四义士，你怎么看？』趁他正要趿拉着走开，我问。他立刻折回来。『他们怎么了？』他急问。我便顺着这话，一步一步引到四义士的身份上。他谈他们的兴头很足，急着知道我的看法，最上心的还是那笔赏金。他说得入了港，忽然身子前倾，用一根脏兮兮的食指点着我的胸口，讲起了一个假设的案例。」

===Original===
Poiccart stopped to laugh﻿—his laugh ended in a sleepy yawn.

“You know the sort of questions,” said he, “and you know how very naive the illiterate are when they are seeking to disguise their identities by elaborate hypotheses. Well, this is the story. He﻿—Marks is his name﻿—thinks he may be able to recognize one of us by some extraordinary trick of memory. To enable him to do this, he has been granted freedom﻿—tomorrow he would search London, he said.”

“A full day’s work,” laughed Manfred.

“Indeed,” agreed Poiccart soberly, “but hear the sequel. We parted, and I walked westward perfectly satisfied of our security. I made for Covent Garden Market, because that is one of the places in London where a man may be seen at four o’clock in the morning without exciting suspicion.

===Chinese===
波伊卡特停下来笑了——笑着笑着，收在一个昏昏欲睡的呵欠里。

「那一类问题你们是知道的，」他说，「你们也知道，不识字的人想借精心编派的假设遮掩自己时，是何等天真。喏，故事是这样：他——他姓马克斯——觉得凭某种奇妙的记忆把戏，兴许认得出我们中的一个。为成全他这桩本事，人家放了他自由——他说，明天他要搜遍伦敦。」

「好一个整天的活计。」曼弗雷德笑道。

「正是。」波伊卡特正色应道，「可还有下文。我们分子手，我往西去，对我们的安全十分放心。我朝科文特加登市场走，因为那是伦敦少数几个地方，凌晨四点钟有人影也不惹疑。

===Original===
“I had strolled through the market, idly watching the busy scene, when, for some cause that I cannot explain, I turned suddenly on my heel and came face to face with Marks! He grinned sheepishly, and recognized me with a nod of his head.

“He did not wait for me to ask him his business, but started in to explain his presence.

“I accepted his explanation easily, and for the second time that night invited him to coffee. He hesitated at first, then accepted. When the coffee was brought, he pulled it to him as far from my reach as possible, and then I knew that Mr. Marks had placed me at fault, that I had underrated his intelligence, that all the time he had been unburdening himself he had recognized me. He had put me off my guard.”

“But why﻿—?” began Manfred.

===Chinese===
「我信步穿过市场，闲看那繁忙景象，忽然——缘由我至今说不清——我脚跟一转，竟与马克斯撞了个对脸！他讪讪一笑，点头认出了我。

「不等我问他的来意，他抢先解释起自己何以在此。

「我轻巧地把他的解释收下，当夜第二回请他喝咖啡。他先还迟疑，随后应了。咖啡端上来，他把杯子拉到自己跟前，拉得离我越远越好——这时我才明白，马克斯先生将了我一军：我小看了他的智力；他倾吐衷肠的当口，其实一直认得我。他早卸下了我的防备。」

「可为什么——？」曼弗雷德刚起头。

===Original===
“That is what I thought,” the other answered. “Why did he not have me arrested?” He turned to Leon, who had been a silent listener. “Tell us, Leon, why?”

“The explanation is simple,” said Gonsalez quietly; “why did not Thery betray us?﻿—cupidity, the second most potent force of civilization. He has some doubt of the reward. He may fear the honesty of the police﻿—most criminals do so; he may want witnesses.” Leon walked to the wall, where his coat hung. He buttoned it thoughtfully, ran his hand over his smooth chin, then pocketed the little phial that stood on the table.

“You have slipped him, I suppose?” he asked.

Poiccart nodded.

===Chinese===
「我也这么想。」对方答道。「他为什么不叫人抓我？」他转向一直沉默旁听的莱昂。「你说说，莱昂，为什么？」

「解释很简单。」冈萨雷斯静静地说；「特里为什么没出卖我们？——贪心，文明里第二股最厉害的力。他对那笔赏金存着疑。他也许信不过警察的诚实——罪犯多半如此；他也许要证人。」莱昂走到墙边，他的大衣挂在那里。他若有所思地扣上扣子，手抹了抹光溜的下巴，然后把桌上那只小药瓶揣进了口袋。

「你已经甩掉他了，想必？」他问。

波伊卡特点头。

===Original===
“He lives﻿—?”

“At 700, Red Cross Street, in the Borough﻿—it is a common lodging-house.”

Leon took a pencil from the table and rapidly sketched a head upon the edge of a newspaper.

“Like this?” he asked.

===Chinese===
「他住——？」

「红十字会街 700 号，萨瑟克那边——一家下等公寓。」

莱昂从桌上拿起一支铅笔，在报纸边上飞快勾出一个头颅。

「像不像？」他问。

===Original===
Poiccart examined the portrait.

“Yes,” he said in surprise; “have you seen him?”

“No,” said Leon carelessly, “but such a man would have such a head.”

He paused on the threshold.

===Chinese===
波伊卡特端详那幅速写。

「像。」他惊讶地说；「你见过他？」

「没有。」莱昂淡淡地说，「不过，那样的人，就该长这样一颗头。」

他在门槛上停了停。

===Original===
“I think it is necessary.” There was a question in his assertion. It was addressed rather to Manfred, who stood with his folded arms and knit brow staring at the floor.

For answer Manfred extended his clenched fist.

Leon saw the down-turned thumb, and left the room.

===Chinese===
「我想，这是必要的。」这断言里含着探问。这话与其说是对谁，不如说是对曼弗雷德说的——他抱臂蹙眉，正盯着地板。

作为回答，曼弗雷德伸出攥紧的拳头。

莱昂看见那根朝下的拇指，便离了房间。

---
===Original===
Billy Marks was in a quandary. By the most innocent device in the world his prey had managed to slip through his fingers. When Poiccart, stopping at the polished doors of the best hotel in London, whither they had strolled, casually remarked that he would not be a moment and disappeared into the hotel, Billy was nonplussed. This was a contingency for which he was not prepared. He had followed the suspect from Blackfriars; he was almost sure that this was the man he had robbed. He might, had he wished, have called upon the first constable he met to take the man into custody; but the suspicion of the thief, the fear that he might be asked to share the reward with the man who assisted him, restrained him. And besides, it might not be the man at all, argued Billy, and yet﻿—

Poiccart was a chemist, a man who found joy in unhealthy precipitates, who mixed evil-smelling drugs and distilled, filtered, carbonated, oxydized, and did all manner of things in glass tubes, to the vegetable, animal, and mineral products of the earth. Billy had left Scotland Yard to look for a man with a discoloured hand. Here again, he might, had he been less fearful of treachery, have placed in the hands of the police a very valuable mark of identification.

It seems a very lame excuse to urge on Billy’s behalf that his cupidity alone stayed his hand, when he came face to face with the man he was searching for. And yet it was so. Then again there was a sum in simple proportion to be worked out. If one Just Man was worth a thousand pounds, what was the commercial value of four? Billy was a thief with a business head.

So that when Poiccart disappeared within the magnificent portals of the Royal Hotel in Northumberland Avenue, Billy was hipped. He realized in a flash that his captive had gone whither he could not follow without exposing his hand; that the chances were he had gone forever. He looked up and down the street; there was no policeman in sight. In the vestibule, a porter in shirt sleeves was polishing brasses. It was still very early; the streets were deserted, and Billy, after a few moments’ hesitation, took a course that he would not have dared at a more conventional hour.

===Chinese===
比利·马克斯进退两难了。他的猎物用天下最无辜的一招，从他指缝里滑了出去。两人一路闲逛，逛到伦敦头号大酒店那扇锃亮的大门前，波伊卡特站住，随口说了句「去去就来」，便闪进酒店没了影，比利傻了眼。这一手变故他没有防备。他从黑衣修士桥一路跟着这位嫌疑犯；他几乎拿准，这就是他偷过的人。他本可以随自己的便，拦下碰见的第一个警察把人拿下；可是贼的多疑——怕人家要他同帮忙的人分享赏金——把他拉住了。再说，兴许根本不是这个人，比利如此辩道，然而——

波伊卡特是位化学家，一个从不健康的沉淀物里找乐趣的人，调恶臭的药剂，蒸馏，过滤，碳化，氧化，在玻璃管里对世上的植物、动物、矿物做尽各样手脚。比利离开苏格兰场时，要找的是一个手上带异色的人。这一点上，他若不是那么怕遭暗算，本可以把一个极值钱的识别标记送到警察手里。

替比利辩护，说他与寻访的对象打了个照面却单单因贪心缩了手，这借口实在蹩脚。然而事实如此。再说，还有一道简单的比例题要算：一个义士值一千英镑，四个的市价几何？比利是个有生意头脑的贼。

于是，当波伊卡特隐入诺森伯兰大街皇家饭店堂皇的大门里，比利蔫了。他一闪念便明白：他的俘虏去了一个他一跟就露馅的地方；八成是一去不回。他朝街两头张望；一个警察的影子也没有。门厅里，一个挽着袖子的门房正在擦铜器。天还早得很；街上空无一人；比利犹豫了片刻，走了一步在正经钟点他决不敢走的棋。

===Original===
He pushed open the swing doors and passed into the vestibule. The porter turned on him as he entered and favoured him with a suspicious frown.

“What do you want?” asked he, eyeing the tattered coat of the visitor in some disfavour.

“Look ’ere, old feller,” began Billy, in his most conciliatory tone.

Just then the porter’s strong right arm caught him by the coat collar, and Billy found himself stumbling into the street.

“Outside﻿—you,” said the porter firmly.

===Chinese===
他推开弹簧门进了门厅。门房听见动静转过身，赏了他一道狐疑的皱眉。

「干什么的？」他问，很不待见地打量来客那件破外套。

「您听我说，老哥。」比利拿出了他最息事宁人的腔调。

话音未落，门房有力的右臂已揪住他的领子，比利踉跄着跌回了街上。

「外头待着去——说你呢。」门房斩钉截铁地说。

===Original===
It needed this rebuff to engender in Marks the necessary self-assurance to carry him through.

Straightening his ruffled clothing, he pulled Falmouth’s card from his pocket and returned to the charge with dignity.

“I am p’lice officer,” he said, adopting the opening that he knew so well, “and if you interfere with me, look out! young feller.”

The porter took the card and scrutinized it.

“What do you want?” he asked in more civil tones. He would have added “sir,” but somehow it stuck in his throat. If the man is a detective, he argued to himself, he is very well disguised.

===Chinese===
非得有这一记抢白，才能给马克斯生出闯过关卡所必需的自信。

他抻平揉皱的衣裳，从口袋里抽出法尔茅斯的名片，体体面面卷土重来。

「我是警察。」他说，祭出了他烂熟的那句开场白，「你要是碍我的事，小心着！小伙子。」

门房接过名片，端详起来。

===Original===
“I want that gentleman that came in before me,” said Billy.

The porter scratched his head.

“What is the number of his room?” he asked.

“Never mind about the number of his room,” said Billy rapidly. “Is there any back way to this hotel﻿—any way a man can get out of it? I mean, besides through the front entrance?”

“Half a dozen,” replied the porter.

===Chinese===
「有何贵干？」他的口气文明了些。他本想添一声「先生」，不知怎么卡在了喉咙里。这人要真是个侦探，他心里辩道，那化装得可真到家。

「我要找先进去的那位先生。」比利说。

门房挠了挠头。

「他住几号房？」他问。

===Original===
Billy groaned.

“Take me round to one of them, will you?” he asked. And the porter led the way.

One of the tradesmen’s entrances was from a small back street; and here it was that a street scavenger gave the information that Marks had feared. Five minutes before a man answering to the description had walked out, turned towards the Strand and, picking up a cab in the sight of the street cleaner, had driven off.

===Chinese===
「几号房不关你的事。」比利语速飞快。「这饭店有没有后路——有没有人能溜出去的道？我是说，除了正门之外？」

「半打不止。」门房答道。

比利呻吟了一声。

「带我上其中一处，成不成？」他问。门房便头前带路。

一道送货员工门开在小后街上；正是在这里，一个扫街的道出了马克斯最怕听的消息。五分钟前，有个与描述相符的人步行出去，拐向河滨街，当着扫街人的面拦了辆马车，坐车走了。

===Original===
Baffled, and with the added bitterness that had he played boldly he might have secured at any rate a share of a thousand pounds, Billy walked slowly to the Embankment, cursing the folly that had induced him to throw away the fortune that was in his hands. With hands thrust deep into his pockets, he tramped the weary length of the Embankment, going over again and again the incidents of the night and each time muttering a lurid condemnation of his error. It must have been an hour after he had lost Poiccart, that it occurred to him all was not lost. He had the man’s description, he had looked at his face, he knew him feature by feature. That was something, at any rate. Nay, it occurred to him that if the man was arrested through his description he would still be entitled to the reward﻿—or a part of it. He dared not see Falmouth and tell him that he had been in company with the man all night without effecting his arrest. Falmouth would never believe him, and, indeed, it was curious that he should have met him.

This fact struck Billy for the first time. By what strange chance had he met this man? Was it possible﻿—the idea frightened Marks﻿—that the man he had robbed had recognized *him*, and that he had deliberately sought him out with murderous intent?

A cold perspiration broke upon the narrow forehead of the thief. These men were murderers, cruel, relentless murderers: suppose﻿—?

===Chinese===
落了空，还平添一重苦涩——他若放胆行事，一千英镑好歹能分上一份——比利慢慢走向河滨大道，一路咒骂自己糊涂，竟把捧在手里的财运亲手扔了。他双手深插口袋，沿着河滨大道疲惫地踱了一程又一程，把这一夜的事翻来覆去地过，每过一遍，就粗声恶气把自己骂上一顿。丢了波伊卡特约莫一个钟头之后，他才想起：未必全丢了。那人的模样他有，那张脸他看过，一眉一眼他都记得。这总算是一点本钱。不但如此，他还想到：那人的落网若凭他的描述，赏金——或它的一部分——照样有他的份。他不敢去见法尔茅斯，告诉他自己同那人厮混了一整夜却没拿人。法尔茅斯断不会信他；说真的，他竟会碰上那人，也确实蹊跷。

这个事实头一回敲在比利心上。他撞见这个人，是何等奇怪的机缘？有没有可能——这念头吓着了马克斯——他偷的那人早已认出了*他*，存心寻上门来，带着杀人的算盘？

贼那窄窄的额头上沁出一层冷汗。这几个人是杀人犯，狠毒、不肯罢休的杀人犯：倘若——？

===Original===
He turned from the contemplation of the unpleasant possibilities to meet a man who was crossing the road toward him. He eyed the stranger doubtingly. The newcomer was a young-looking man, clean-shaven, with sharp features and restless blue eyes. As he came closer, Marks noted that first his appearance had been deceptive; the man was not so young as he looked. He might have been forty, thought Marks. He approached, looked hard at Billy, then beckoned him to stop, for Billy was walking away.

“Is your name Marks?” asked the stranger authoritatively.

“Yes, sir,” replied the thief.

“Have you seen Mr. Falmouth?”

“Not since last night,” replied Marks in surprise.

===Chinese===
他从那些不祥的可能里转过神来，正迎上一个穿马路朝他走来的人。他狐疑地打量这陌生人。来人看着年轻，脸刮得干净，五官尖削，一双不安分的蓝眼睛。待走近了些，马克斯才察觉起初的印象靠不住：这人不像看上去那么年轻。四十上下吧，马克斯想。来人走近，死死看了比利一眼，随即抬手示意他站住——比利本正在走开。

「你叫马克斯？」陌生人威严地问。

「是，先生。」贼答。

「见过法尔茅斯先生吗？」

===Original===
“Then you are to come at once to him.”

“Where is he?”

“At Kensington Police Station﻿—there has been an arrest, and he wants you to identify the man.”

Billy’s heart sank.

“Do I get any of the reward?” he demanded, “that is if I recognize ’im?”

===Chinese===
「昨儿夜里之后没见过。」马克斯愕然答道。

「那请你立刻去见他。」

「他在哪儿？」

「肯辛顿警察局——抓了个人，他要你去认一认。」

比利的心沉了下去。

===Original===
The other nodded and Billy’s hopes rose.

“You must follow me,” said the newcomer; “Mr. Falmouth does not wish us to be seen together. Take a first-class ticket to Kensington and get into the next carriage to mine﻿—come.”

He turned and crossed the road toward Charing Cross, and Billy followed at a distance.

He found the stranger pacing the platform and gave no sign of recognition. A train pulled into the station, and Marks followed his conductor through a crowd of workmen the train had discharged. He entered an empty first-class carriage, and Marks, obeying instructions, took possession of the adjoining compartment, and found himself the solitary occupant.

===Chinese===
「赏金有我的份吗？」他追问，「我是说，要是我认出他来？」

对方点头，比利的希望抬了头。

「你得跟在我后面。」来人说；「法尔茅斯先生不愿让人看见我们同路。买一张去肯辛顿的头等票，坐我隔壁那节车厢——走吧。」

他转身穿过马路朝查令十字去，比利隔着一段距离跟上。

===Original===
Between Charing Cross and Westminster Marks had time to review his position. Between the last station and St. James’s Park, he invented his excuses to the detective; between the Park and Victoria he had completed his justification for a share of the reward. Then as the train moved into the tunnel for its five minutes’ run to Sloane Square, Billy noticed a draught, and turned his head to see the stranger standing on the footboard of the swaying carriage, holding the half-opened door.

Marks was startled.

“Pull up the window on your side,” ordered the man, and Billy, hypnotized by the authoritative voice, obeyed. At that moment he heard the tinkle of broken glass.

He turned with an angry snarl.

===Chinese===
从查令十字到威斯敏斯特，马克斯把处境盘算了一遍。从威斯敏斯特到圣詹姆斯公园，他编好了对付侦探的托词；从圣詹姆斯公园到维多利亚，他把自己应分赏金的理由论证完毕。随后，列车钻进通往斯隆广场的五分钟隧道，比利觉出一股过堂风，回头一看——陌生人正站在摇晃的车厢踏板上，扶着半开的门。

马克斯吃了一惊。

「把你那边的窗拉上去。」那人命令道；比利被那威严的嗓音催了眠，照办了。就在这时，他听见玻璃碎裂的一声脆响。

他怒吼一声，转过身来。

===Original===
“What’s the game?” he demanded.

For answer the stranger swung himself clear of the door and, closing it softly, disappeared.

===Chinese===
「搞什么名堂？」他质问。

作为回答，陌生人身子一荡离了车门，轻轻掩上，人不见了。

===Original===
“What’s his game?” repeated Marks drowsily. Looking down he saw a broken phial at his feet, by the phial lay a shining sovereign. He stared stupidly at it for a moment, then, just before the train ran into Victoria Station, he stopped to pick it up.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
「他搞什么名堂？」马克斯昏沉沉地又问了一遍。低头一看，脚边一只碎裂的药瓶，药瓶旁躺着一枚亮闪闪的沙弗林。他傻傻盯着它看了一会儿，随后，就在列车驶进维多利亚车站之前，他弯腰把它拾了起来。……

## XI Three Who Died / 十一 三个死者
===Original===
A passenger leisurely selecting his compartment during the wait at Kensington opened a carriage door and staggered back coughing. A solicitous porter and an alarmed station official ran forward and pulled open the door, and the sickly odour of almonds pervaded the station. A little knot of passengers gathered and peered over one another’s shoulders, whilst the station inspector investigated. By and by came a doctor, and a stretcher, and a policeman from the street without. Together they lifted the huddled form of a dead man from the carriage and laid it on the platform.

“Did you find anything?” asked the policeman.

“A sovereign and a broken bottle,” was the reply.

The policeman fumbled in the dead man’s pockets.

===Chinese===
肯辛顿站停车当口，一位悠闲挑着车厢的旅客拉开一扇车门，呛得连声咳嗽倒退回来。一名殷勤的搬运工和一位大惊失色的车站职员抢上前来拉开车门，一股杏仁味的甜腥顿时弥漫全站。一小簇乘客聚拢来，扒着彼此的肩头张望，站长在里头查验。不多时来了医生，来了担架，还从街上叫来一名警察。众人合力，把车厢里一具蜷缩的死尸抬出，放在月台上。

「发现什么了吗？」警察问。

「一枚沙弗林，一只碎瓶子。」对方答。

警察在死人衣袋里翻检。

===Original===
“I don’t suppose he’ll have any papers to show who he is,” he said with knowledge. “Here’s a first-class ticket﻿—it must be a case of suicide. Here’s a card﻿—”

He turned it over and read it, and his face underwent a change.

He gave a few hurried instructions, then made his way to the nearest telegraph office.

===Chinese===
「谅他也没有证件能表明身份。」他一副行家的口气。「这儿一张头等车票——准是自杀。这儿一张名片——」

他把名片翻过来一读，脸色变了。

他急急交代了几句，便直奔最近的电报局。

===Original===
Superintendent Falmouth, who had snatched a few hours’ sleep at the Downing Street house, rose with a troubled mind and an uneasy feeling that in spite of all his precautions the day would end disastrously. He was hardly dressed before the arrival of the assistant-commissioner was announced.

“I have your report, Falmouth,” was the official’s greeting; “you did perfectly right to release Marks﻿—have you had news of him this morning?”

“No.”

===Chinese===
法尔茅斯警司在唐宁街那宅子里抓着睡了几个钟头，起身时心事重重，隐隐觉得任凭他千防万防，这一天终要以灾祸收场。他还没穿戴齐整，助理总监到访的通报已经进来。

「你的报告我看过了，法尔茅斯。」这位官员开场道；「放掉马克斯，你做得一点不错——今天早上有他的消息吗？」

「没有。」

===Original===
“H’m,” said the commissioner thoughtfully. “I wonder whether﻿—” he did not finish his sentence. “Has it occurred to you that the Four may have realized their danger?”

The detective’s face showed surprise.

“Why, of course, sir.”

“Have you considered what their probable line of action will be?”

===Chinese===
「唔。」总监沉吟道。「我在想会不会——」话没说完。「你可想过，四义士也许已察觉自身的危险？」

侦探面露讶色。

「这个，自然想过，先生。」

「他们可能的下一步，你推演过吗？」

===Original===
“N﻿—no﻿—unless it takes the form of an attempt to get out of the country.”

“Has it struck you that whilst this man Marks is looking for them, they are probably seeking him?”

“Bill is smart,” said the detective, uneasily.

“So are they,” said the commissioner with an emphatic nod. “My advice is, get in touch with Marks and put two of your best men to watch him.”

===Chinese===
「没——没有——除非是想逃出国去。」

「你可想到：这个马克斯在找他们，他们多半也在找他？」

「比尔滑得很。」侦探不安地说。

「他们更滑。」总监重重一点头。「我的忠告：联络上马克斯，派你两个最得力的人盯住他。」

===Original===
“That shall be done at once,” replied Falmouth; “I am afraid that it is a precaution that should have been taken before.”

“I am going to see Sir Philip,” the commissioner went on, and he added with a dubious smile, “I shall be obliged to frighten him a little.”

“What is the idea?”

===Chinese===
「这就去办。」法尔茅斯答道；「只怕这层防范，本该早一步做。」

「我去见菲利普爵士。」总监续道，又含着一点没把握的笑补了一句，「少不得要吓他一吓。」

「什么用意？」

===Original===
“We wish him to drop this bill. Have you seen the morning papers?”

“No, sir.”

“They are unanimous that the bill should be abandoned﻿—they say because it is not sufficiently important to warrant the risk, that the country itself is divided on its merit; but as a matter of fact they are afraid of the consequence; and upon my soul I’m a little afraid, too.”

===Chinese===
「我们盼他撤案。晨报看了吗？」

「没有，先生。」

「报界异口同声要法案作罢——说是法案不够分量，犯不上冒这个险，说国人对其利弊本就分裂；可实情是他们怕了后果；老实说，我心里也有点怕。」

===Original===
He mounted the stairs, and was challenged at the landing by one of his subordinates. This was a system introduced after the episode of the disguised “detective.” The foreign minister was now in a state of siege. Nobody was to be trusted, a password had been initiated, and every precaution taken to ensure against a repetition of the previous mistake. His hand was raised to knock upon the panel of the study, when he felt his arm gripped. He turned to see Falmouth with white face and startled eyes.

“They’ve finished Billy,” said the detective breathlessly. “He has just been found in a railway carriage at Kensington.”

The commissioner whistled.

===Chinese===
他登上楼梯，在楼梯口被自己的一名部下喝问口令。这是假扮「侦探」事件之后立下的新规。外交大臣如今处于围城状态。谁也不能信，口令已经设立，种种防范一一落实，只求前番的失误不再重演。他抬手正要叩书房的门板，忽觉手臂被人一把攥住。回头一看，是法尔茅斯，脸色煞白，目光惊惶。

「他们把比利结果了。」侦探上气不接下气地说。「刚在肯辛顿的一节车厢里发现了他。」

总监吹了声口哨。

===Original===
“How was it done?” he asked.

Falmouth was the picture of haggard despair.

“Prussic acid gas,” he said bitterly; “they are scientific. Look you, sir, persuade this man to drop his damned bill.” He pointed to the door of Sir Philip’s room. “We shall never save him. I have got the feeling in my bones that he is a doomed man.”

“Nonsense!” the commissioner answered sharply. “You are growing nervous﻿—you haven’t had enough sleep, Falmouth. That isn’t spoken like your real self﻿—we *must* save him.”

===Chinese===
「怎么下的手？」他问。

法尔茅斯活脱一幅憔悴绝望的画像。

「氢氰酸毒气。」他恨声道；「他们玩的是科学。您听我说，先生，劝这个人撤了他那该死的法案吧。」他指着菲利普爵士的房门。「我们救不了他。我骨头缝里都有这个感觉：他是个死定的人。」

「胡说！」总监厉声答道。「你神经紧张了——你缺觉，法尔茅斯。这可不像你平时说的话——我们*必须*救下他。」

===Original===
He turned from the study and beckoned one of the officers who guarded the landing.

“Sergeant, tell Inspector Collins to send an emergency call throughout the area, for reserves to gather immediately. I will put such a cordon round Ramon today,” he went on addressing Falmouth, “so that no man shall reach him without the fear of being crushed to death.”

And within an hour there was witnessed in London a scene that has no parallel in the history of the metropolis. From every district there came a small army of policemen. They arrived by train, by tramway car, by motorbus, by every vehicle and method of traction that could be requisitioned or seized. They streamed from the stations, they poured through the thoroughfares, till London stood aghast at the realization of the strength of her civic defences.

===Chinese===
他离开书房，招手唤过一名把守楼梯口的警官。

「巡佐，传话柯林斯探长（Inspector Collins），向全区发出紧急召集令，后备警力即刻集结。今天我要给拉蒙布下一道天罗地网，」他接着对法尔茅斯说，「叫任何人都休想近他的身，除非不怕被挤成肉泥。」

不出一个钟头，伦敦便目睹了这座都城史上无先例的一幕。各区都开来了小小一支警察大军。他们乘火车来，乘电车来，乘公共汽车来，凡能征用抢到的车辆与牵引手段，无所不用。他们从车站涌出，灌满一条条通衢，直到伦敦惊愕地认清了自己治安防卫的实力。

===Original===
Whitehall was soon packed from end to end; St. James’s Park was black with them. Automatically Whitehall, Charles Street, Birdcage Walk, and the eastern end of the Mall, were barred to all traffic by solid phalanxes of mounted constables.
